=== NOTES 3:105 | Fiscal Policy - Objectives, Instruments; Fiscal, Revenue, Primary and Effective Revenue Deficits
## SUB 0 | Deficit Definitions and Formulas
[105:0:0] TABLE head: Deficit | Formula | Shows
  [105:0:0.0] Revenue (RD) | Revenue expenditure − revenue receipts | Borrowing for day-to-day consumption (dissaving)
  [105:0:0.1] Effective RD (ERD) | RD − grants for creation of capital assets | True consumption deficit (C. Rangarajan concept)
  [105:0:0.2] Fiscal (FD / GFD) | Total expenditure − (revenue receipts + non-debt capital receipts, i.e. recovery of loans + disinvestment) | Total borrowing requirement
  [105:0:0.3] Primary (PD) | FD − interest payments | Current imbalance, excluding past debt
  [105:0:0.4] Budget deficit (old) | Total expenditure − total receipts (incl. borrowings) | Ad hoc T-bill era measure
  [105:0:0.5] Monetised | Part of FD financed by the RBI (high-powered money) | Inflationary
  [105:0:0.6] Net FD | GFD − net lending of the Centre | e.g. 1,200 − 400 = ₹800 crore
[105:0:1] - **FD** = total expenditure minus total receipts **excluding borrowings** = **budget deficit + borrowings and other liabilities** (market borrowings) (IAS 1994; APPSC Lecturers 2023; State PSC).
[105:0:2] - FD "closest to" the **interest-bearing borrowings during a year** (APPSC G2 2017) – a flow, not accumulated debt.
[105:0:3] - Loose exam wording: "excess of total expenditure over revenue receipts" = **fiscal deficit** (APPSC G2 2011) – strictly non-debt capital receipts are also deducted.
[105:0:4] - **GFD (financing side) = net borrowing at home + borrowing from RBI + borrowing from abroad** (RAS 2023). Net borrowing at home includes small savings and, indirectly, bank SLR holdings.
[105:0:5] - **FD = RD + capital expenditure − non-debt capital receipts**; RD is a part of FD.
[105:0:6] - **PD = FD − interest** (= **gross primary deficit** when taken from GFD) (APPSC G2 2017, UPPSC ×4); **PD + interest = FD** (UPPSC 2010). It is NOT revenue deficit minus interest.
[105:0:7] - **ERD** introduced in **Union Budget 2011-12**; added to FRBM by the **2012 amendment**; dropped as a target in 2018. ERD shows capital receipts used for consumption.
[105:0:8] - RD, FD, PD were adopted as official terms from **1997-98** (Ramesh Singh); budget deficit stopped being the headline measure after ad hoc T-bills ended (1997).
[105:0:9] - **Headline marker:** the **fiscal deficit** is the headline marker of fiscal health; RD and ERD show what the borrowing is used for.
[105:0:10] # Worked Examples
[105:0:11] - FD ₹1,50,000 crore − interest ₹1,20,000 crore = **PD ₹30,000 crore**.
[105:0:12] - Gross primary deficit: ₹50,000 − ₹1,500 = **₹48,500 crore** (non-debt capital receipts not deducted again).
[105:0:13] - FD ₹4 crore on GDP ₹400 crore = **1% of GDP** (NextGen).
[105:0:14] > Trap: "Primary deficit = revenue deficit − interest" is wrong; PD = **fiscal** deficit − interest.
[105:0:15] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.11-12 (RD, FD, non-debt receipts, GFD financing, PD); NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.1 (FD 1% of GDP example); Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.18.6 (ERD Budget 2011-12), p.18.9 (FD, PD, monetised deficit from 1997-98); Union Budget 2026-27 Budget at a Glance p.2, p.5 (definitions); app notes 3:105 sub 4, 5
## SUB 1 | Relationships Between Deficits
[105:1:0] - **FD > PD** always (interest is positive); **FD > RD** in practice (FD also covers borrowing for capital spending) (UPPSC 2008); **ERD ≤ RD**.
[105:1:1] - **PD is never higher than FD** – "PD always higher than FD" is false, "PD = FD − interest" is true.
[105:1:2] - **FD > budgetary deficit**: FD also counts borrowings (incl. from the RBI) and other liabilities (UPSC 1999 A-R: both true, R explains A).
[105:1:3] - Largest deficit in the GoI budget = **fiscal deficit** (UPPSC 2002 ×3). Descending order: **fiscal > revenue > primary** (2026-27 BE: **4.3 > 1.5 > 0.7**).
[105:1:4] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [105:1:4.0] Zero primary deficit | FD = interest; borrowing just equals interest payment – debt-trap signal
  [105:1:4.1] Debt trap | Borrowing to make interest payments
  [105:1:4.2] Zero RD | Balanced revenue budget
  [105:1:4.3] Primary surplus | Non-interest spending covered by non-debt receipts
[105:1:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.12 (PD, RD part of FD); Union Budget 2026-27 Budget at a Glance p.5 (FD 4.3, RD 1.5, PD 0.7); app notes 3:105 sub 5
## SUB 2 | Union Deficit Trends and Targets
[105:2:0] TABLE head: Year | FD (% GDP) | Note
  [105:2:0.0] 1990-91 | 8.4 (most books; Ramesh Singh 7.85) | Crisis peak; RD 3.3
  [105:2:0.1] 1992-93 | – | RD fell to 2.5
  [105:2:0.2] 1996-97 / 2000-01 | 5.0 / 5.1 | Budget targets
  [105:2:0.3] 2001-02 | 4.7 | Budget
  [105:2:0.4] 2009-10 | 6.8 | Budget (post-crisis stimulus)
  [105:2:0.5] 2010-11 | 5.5 BE (4.9 actual) | PD below 2.0
  [105:2:0.6] 2011-12 | 5.9 | Highest of 2011-12 to 2014-15
  [105:2:0.7] 2016-17 | 3.5 | Target
  [105:2:0.8] 2017-18 | 3.2 BE → 3.5 RE | Slower indirect tax growth after GST
  [105:2:0.9] 2019-20 | 3.3 BE (4.6 actual) | Did not meet BE
  [105:2:0.10] 2020-21 | 9.2 | COVID peak, highest since 1990-91; RD 7.3, PD 5.7
  [105:2:0.11] 2021-22 | 6.8 BE; 6.7 actual | not 7.7; RD 4.4 (above 4)
  [105:2:0.12] 2024-25 | 4.9 BE (full Budget July 2024); 4.8 RE/actual | RD 1.7, PD 1.4
  [105:2:0.13] 2025-26 | 4.4 BE and RE | RD 1.5, PD 0.8
  [105:2:0.14] 2026-27 BE | 4.3 = ₹16,95,768 crore | RD 1.5, ERD 0.3, PD 0.7
[105:2:1] - **FD RE 2024-25 (4.8%) > BE 2025-26 (4.4%)** (APPSC G2: both statements correct).
[105:2:2] - Fiscal deficit has **not risen steadily** over the last decade, nor has tax-GDP risen steadily (UPSC 2015: neither).
[105:2:3] - 2019-20: growth decelerated, tax revenue below BE, **non-tax revenue grew strongly**; FD **did not** stay at BE.
[105:2:4] - Revenue collections did **not** fall in the years after FY21; FY22 FD moderated to 6.7%, not 7.7%.
[105:2:5] - Consolidation 9.2% → 4.4% brought **three rating upgrades in 2025** (Morningstar DBRS, S&P, R&I).
[105:2:6] - **End-July 2026**: FD ₹4,55,144 crore = **26.8%** of target vs 29.9% a year earlier (CGA, 1 Sep 2026).
[105:2:7] - Early-1990s double-digit inflation was controlled by **containing the budgetary deficit and unproductive expenditure**.
[105:2:8] - India has a **long history of fiscal deficits** (true); but farm subsidies are **lower** in India than in Western countries (UPPSC 2002, 2004 A-R: A true, R false).
[105:2:9] # Fiscal Rules Abroad
[105:2:10] - **EU Stability and Growth Pact (1997)**: limits budget deficits (below **3%** of GDP) and debt (60%); it does **not** share infrastructure or technology (UPSC 2023: only one).
[105:2:11] # AP Angle
[105:2:12] - AP BE 2026-27: **RD 1.11%** (₹22,002 crore), **FD 3.84%** (₹75,868 crore) of GSDP; 2025-26 RE: RD 2.33%, FD 4.57%. AP still runs a **primary deficit**.
[105:2:13] - 18 States above 3% FD in FY24; 10 States slipped into revenue deficit between FY19 and FY25.
[105:2:14] ~~ Sources: Union Budget 2026-27 Budget at a Glance p.5 (2024-25 to 2026-27 deficits); Union Budget 2026-27 Speech para 92-96; Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.18.16 (Centre FD 7.85% in 1990-91); app notes 3:105 sub 8 (older budget figures, AP data)
## SUB 3 | Fiscal Policy – Meaning, Makers and Persons
[105:3:0] - **Fiscal policy** = government policy on **taxation, expenditure and public debt** (its income, spending and debt) to shape aggregate demand, allocation and distribution (MPPSC 1996; APPSC Dental 2019).
[105:3:1] - Word from Latin **fiscus** = basket / public purse.
[105:3:2] - Formulated by the **Ministry of Finance** – not the RBI (monetary policy), Finance Commission or Planning Commission (APSLPRB 2012; UPPSC 2012); Union Budget prepared by the Budget Division, **Department of Economic Affairs (DEA)**.
[105:3:3] - **Article 112** calls it the **"Annual Financial Statement"**; the word "Budget" is not in the Constitution. The **budget is an instrument of fiscal policy** (JPSC 2013).
[105:3:4] TABLE head: Person / body | Contribution
  [105:3:4.0] J.M. Keynes | *General Theory* (1936); fiscal policy as the tool against the Great Depression (UKPSC 2012)
  [105:3:4.1] V.P. Singh | Long-Term Fiscal Policy, laid in Parliament Dec 1985 (Budget year 1985-86) as Finance Minister
  [105:3:4.2] LTFP's key reform | MODVAT – introduced in the 1986-87 Budget (from 1 March 1986), on L.K. Jha Committee lines
  [105:3:4.3] Richard Musgrave | Three budget functions; differential tax incidence; popularised "fiscal federalism" (1959)
  [105:3:4.4] Montek Singh Ahluwalia | Chaired the RBI Advisory Group on Fiscal Transparency (report 2001)
  [105:3:4.5] N.K. Singh | Committee 2016, report 2017: general govt debt 60% = Centre 40% + States 20%
[105:3:5] ~~ Sources: Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.18.13 (Keynes), p.18.15 (LTFP Dec 1985); Rocket Indian Economy p.55 (V.P. Singh LTFP 1985-86); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.26, p.39 (MODVAT 1986, L.K. Jha); NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.2 (Budget Division, DEA); rbi.org.in / Business Standard report on the Advisory Group (web search, 2001); app notes 3:105 sub 0
## SUB 4 | Objectives and Instruments; Fiscal vs Monetary
[105:4:0] # Objectives (Musgrave functions)
[105:4:1] TABLE head: Function | Meaning
  [105:4:1.0] Allocation | Provide public goods (judiciary, lighthouse, fireworks display); education is a merit good, not a pure public good
  [105:4:1.1] Distribution | Reduce inequality: progressive taxation + progressive expenditure (UPSC 1996)
  [105:4:1.2] Stabilisation | Control inflation and unemployment – minimising the risk of inflation is a fiscal aim (APPSC G2 2011)
[105:4:2] - Objectives: **economic growth, distributive justice and price stability** (APPSC G2 2011), **full employment**, resource mobilisation, **balanced regional development**.
[105:4:3] - **Regulation of inter-state trade** is NOT a fiscal objective (UPPSC 2006); directing interest rates and managing money supply are monetary objectives.
[105:4:4] - **Economic growth, full employment, exchange stabilisation and price stability** = objectives of **monetary policy** (APPSC G2 2011) – exchange-rate stability is the monetary marker.
[105:4:5] - Progressive taxation evidence: direct taxes = **58.8%** of total taxes (FY25).
[105:4:6] - Components (taxation, expenditure, debt) and objectives (growth, employment, price stability, equity) are different things; the objectives do not explain the components.
[105:4:7] # Instruments
[105:4:8] - Fiscal tools: **taxation (tax policy)**, **public expenditure** (capex as **pump priming**), **public borrowing/debt**, the **budget**, subsidies/DBT, transfer payments, **deficit financing** (State PSC; RAS 2023).
[105:4:9] - **Interest rate, CRR, bank rate, credit ceiling** = monetary tools, NOT fiscal (APPSC FSO; RAS 2023).
[105:4:10] - Public borrowing: **internal** and **external**; external borrowing carries **currency risk**.
[105:4:11] - Public expenditure (Centre + States) ≈ **28% of GDP** (ES 2016-17); Union alone **13.6%** (BE 2026-27).
[105:4:12] TABLE head: Policy | Authority | Tools | Speed
  [105:4:12.0] Fiscal | Govt (MoF) | Taxation, expenditure, public debt, subsidies | Slow (budget cycle)
  [105:4:12.1] Monetary | RBI / MPC | Repo, CRR, SLR, OMOs, LAF | Fast (MPC bi-monthly)
[105:4:13] - Monetary policy is **complementary** to fiscal policy (UPPSC RO 2016).
[105:4:14] - **IS-LM classical range** (vertical LM): **fiscal policy is ineffective** (full crowding out) even if investment is interest-elastic; monetary policy is effective.
[105:4:15] ~~ Sources: Rocket Indian Economy p.54-55 (fiscal policy = tax + expenditure + public debt; objectives growth + employment + price stability + equity); Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.18.13 (definition); app notes 3:105 sub 1, 2; IS-LM, public goods, monetary objectives: general knowledge, not checked
[105:4:16] - **Progressive taxation:** the tax rate rises as income rises, so the rich pay a larger share – this reduces income inequality.
## SUB 5 | Financing the Fiscal Deficit
[105:5:0] - Sources: **market borrowings (G-Secs)**, T-bills, **small savings/NSSF**, **State Provident Funds**, external debt, **draw-down of cash**.
[105:5:1] - **Domestic borrowings** fill most of the FD (about **98-99%**) (UPPSC 2005); 2026-27 BE: net market borrowings **₹11.73 lakh crore** of ₹16.96 lakh crore, external debt only ₹15,385 crore.
[105:5:2] - **Ad hoc T-bills**: automatic monetisation from the mid-1950s; **1994** RBI-Government agreement; abolished **31 March 1997**; **WMA** from 1 April 1997.
[105:5:3] - From **2006-07** RBI barred from subscribing to **primary issues** of G-Secs, except under escape clause; WMA beyond the limit = **overdraft**.
[105:5:4] - **8th Plan** public outlay ₹4,34,100 crore – largest source **government borrowings (46.6%)**.
[105:5:5] - UPSC 2002 (all four true): **external liabilities in the Union Budget are shown at historical (book-value) exchange rates**; continued high borrowing kept **real interest rates high**; rising FD/GDP **crowds out private investment**; **interest payments = single largest item of non-plan revenue expenditure**.
[105:5:6] - Since the 2018 FRBM debt definition, the debt statement also values external debt at **current** exchange rates.
[105:5:7] - **PSU dividends** are **non-tax revenue**, NOT a source of financing the fiscal deficit.
[105:5:8] - Best way to finance a **current account deficit** = **FDI**; twin deficit = FD + **CAD** (dissaving cuts national savings).
[105:5:9] ~~ Sources: Union Budget 2026-27 Budget at a Glance p.8 (sources of financing FD), p.9 (FRBM debt at current exchange rate); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.10 (interest largest non-plan item), p.12 (net borrowing at home); Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.18.18 (WMA, RBI bar 2006-07); app notes 3:105 sub 6
[105:5:10] - **Ways and Means Advances (WMA):** temporary RBI advances to the government to cover short gaps between receipts and payments.
## SUB 6 | Fiscal Stance, Recession and Crowding Out
[105:6:0] TABLE head: Stance | Action | Use / risk
  [105:6:0.0] Expansionary | More spending / lower taxes | Recession; inflation risk
  [105:6:0.1] Contractionary | Less spending / higher taxes | Overheating
  [105:6:0.2] Neutral | Balanced effect | Stable times
[105:6:1] - **Recession**: **increase expenditure on public projects** (UPSC 2021); **fiscal stimulus = cutting tax rates + more government spending** – abolishing subsidies is NOT (UPSC 2010 ×2).
[105:6:2] - **Counter-cyclical**: deficits in slumps (**Atma Nirbhar Bharat, 2020**), consolidation in booms.
[105:6:3] - **Double-dip recession:** recession → short-lived recovery → **another recession**; an ordinary recession = two consecutive quarters of falling GDP (APPSC G2 Mains 2017).
[105:6:4] TABLE head: Term | Meaning
  [105:6:4.0] Crowding out | Government borrowing raises interest rates, reducing private investment (UPSC 2026)
  [105:6:4.1] Crowding in | Public capex draws private investment
  [105:6:4.2] Fiscal multiplier | Change in GDP ÷ change in govt spending or taxes
  [105:6:4.3] Capex multiplier | 2.5-3 in India – largest for capital expenditure
[105:6:5] - **Quality of deficit**: high **RD/FD** ratio = borrowing funds consumption; 2026-27 RD 1.5 / FD 4.3 ≈ **one-third**.
[105:6:6] ~~ Sources: Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.18.11 (crowding out); app notes 3:105 sub 7; UPSC 2010/2021/2026 PYQ explanations checked against these lines
## SUB 7 | Reducing the Deficit; Subsidies
[105:7:0] - Measures: **downsizing bureaucracy** (cuts spending) and **selling/offloading PSU shares** (disinvestment, raises receipts) – **not** FDI inflows or privatising higher education (UPSC 2010 ×3: 3 and 4 only).
[105:7:1] - **Reducing revenue expenditure** and **rationalising subsidies** cut the deficit; new welfare schemes add to it (UPSC 2015: I and III).
[105:7:2] - Spending side: **rationalising subsidies through DBT**, cutting unproductive spending; receipts side: widening tax base, disinvestment.
[105:7:3] - **Financial subsidy** is a big source of budget deficit (true); "subsidy is higher in Indian agriculture than in developed countries" – **false** (UPPSC 2002).
[105:7:4] - Major heads: **food, fertiliser, petroleum (LPG)**; total subsidies ≈ **8.5%** of Union expenditure (2026-27). **Urea** = largest fertiliser subsidy item.
[105:7:5] - **Fertiliser DBT (2018)**: paid to companies after retail sale on **PoS machines** with **Aadhaar** authentication.
[105:7:6] - **Hidden subsidies**: **tax concessions, cheap credit**, under-priced health and education. Forex reserves are not a subsidy head.
[105:7:7] - Q1 FY27 major subsidies up **37.4%** on crude prices.
[105:7:8] - Development needs a **multi-dimensional approach** (true); the Government focuses on both micro and macro issues (UPPSC 2015: A true, R false).
[105:7:9] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:105 sub 9; UPSC 2010/2015 and UPPSC 2002/2015 explanations
## SUB 8 | Budget Types and Expenditure Heads
[105:8:0] TABLE head: Type | Meaning
  [105:8:0.0] Balanced | Revenue = expenditure (APPSC G2 2008)
  [105:8:0.1] Surplus | Receipts > expenditure
  [105:8:0.2] Deficit | Expenditure > receipts
  [105:8:0.3] Zero-based | Every scheme reviewed and justified afresh, no base from last year; Peter Pyhrr, Jimmy Carter; introduced in India (UPPSC 2011: A and R true, R explains A)
  [105:8:0.4] Performance | Spending linked to work done – work management, unit costs, performance standards; NOT personnel selection (APPSC APRO 2008)
  [105:8:0.5] Outcome | Outlays against measurable results; first 2005-06
  [105:8:0.6] Gender | First Gender Budget Statement with Union Budget 2005-06
[105:8:1] # Receipts
[105:8:2] TABLE head: Receipt | Types
  [105:8:2.0] Revenue | Tax and non-tax (e.g. PSU dividends)
  [105:8:2.1] Capital – non-debt | Recovery of loans, disinvestment, asset monetisation
  [105:8:2.2] Capital – debt | Market borrowings, small savings, other liabilities
[105:8:3] - Budget 2026-27: **borrowings and other liabilities = 24 paise** of every rupee received – largest single item.
[105:8:4] - **Plan vs non-plan** distinction **ended in Budget 2017-18**. Developmental vs **non-developmental** (interest, defence, administration).
[105:8:5] - **Charged (Art. 112(3))** – **not voted** by Parliament: President's emoluments, debt charges, SC judges' salaries.
[105:8:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.5-6 (deficit/surplus/balanced budget, ZBB, outcome budget 2005); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.11 (balanced budget); app notes 3:105 sub 3

[n37003dfac58] sub0
  Which deficit indicates government borrowing for day-to-day consumption?
  Fiscal deficit | Primary deficit | Monetised deficit | *Revenue deficit
  x: Revenue deficit shows borrowing for consumption; fiscal deficit shows the total borrowing requirement from all sources.
[n0e354e33469] sub0
  What does the term 'Monetised Deficit' refer to?
  The portion of revenue deficit covered by grants | The portion of deficit financed through external commercial borrowing | The portion of deficit financed by borrowing from the public | *The portion of deficit financed by the Reserve Bank of India through creation of high-powered money
  x: Monetised Deficit is the part of the deficit financed by the RBI, involving an increase in RBI credit to the government and creation of high-powered money.
[nf927dfccd7f] sub0
  Arrange the following developments in chronological order:
A. FRBM amendment incorporating Effective Revenue Deficit
B. Agreement between the RBI and Government relating to ad hoc Treasury Bills
C. Abolition of ad hoc Treasury Bills
D. Introduction of Effective Revenue Deficit in the Union Budget
  *B, C, D, A | C, B, D, A | B, D, C, A | D, B, C, A
  x: The correct sequence is the 1994 agreement, abolition of ad hoc Treasury Bills from 1 April 1997, introduction of ERD in 2011-12, and its incorporation through the 2012 amendment.
[nb9d35bf2cdb] sub0
  Which one of the following statements regarding the old concept of Budget Deficit is NOT correct?
  It included revenue and capital receipts in total receipts. | It was calculated as total expenditure minus total receipts including borrowings. | *It is calculated as fiscal deficit minus interest payments. | It was used when ad hoc Treasury Bills financed the gap.
  x: Fiscal Deficit minus interest payments gives Primary Deficit, not Budget Deficit. Budget Deficit was the difference between total expenditure and total receipts including borrowings.
[ne55213395a9] sub0
  Which one of the following is regarded as the headline marker of fiscal health?
  Effective revenue deficit | *Fiscal deficit | Primary deficit | Revenue deficit
  x: Fiscal deficit is the headline marker of fiscal health. Revenue deficit and effective revenue deficit provide additional information about the nature of government borrowing.
[g5b3021a94dec] sub0
  Which deficit shows the total borrowing requirement of the government?
  Revenue deficit | *Fiscal deficit | Primary deficit | Effective revenue deficit
  x: Fiscal deficit = total expenditure minus revenue receipts and non-debt capital receipts; it equals the total borrowing requirement.
[g20280b04f78f] sub0
  Consider the following statements:
1. Fiscal deficit is total expenditure minus revenue receipts and non-debt capital receipts.
2. Revenue deficit is revenue receipts minus revenue expenditure.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 only | 2 only | Both 1 and 2 | Neither 1 nor 2
  x: Statement 1 is the fiscal deficit formula. Revenue deficit is revenue expenditure minus revenue receipts, not the other way round.

  x: Effective Revenue Deficit (RD minus grants for capital assets) is a Rangarajan concept; it came in Budget 2011-12.
[n4e3bf107af3] sub0
  A government has a fiscal deficit of ₹1,50,000 crore and interest payments of ₹1,20,000 crore. What is its primary deficit?
  *₹30,000 crore | ₹50,000 crore | ₹1,50,000 crore | ₹2,70,000 crore
  x: PD = FD − interest payments = 1,50,000 − 1,20,000 = ₹30,000 crore.
[n57db492c3a7] sub0
  If the Fiscal Deficit is ₹4 crore and GDP is ₹400 crore, what is the Fiscal Deficit as a percentage of GDP?
  0.1 percent | *1 percent | 4 percent | 10 percent
  x: Fiscal Deficit as a percentage of GDP = (₹4 crore ÷ ₹400 crore) × 100 = 1 percent.
[nd68326b4e05] sub1
  Which of the following is NOT correct about the relationship between deficits?
  Primary deficit equals fiscal deficit minus interest payments | *The effective revenue deficit is always greater than the revenue deficit | Fiscal deficit exceeds revenue deficit when capex exceeds non-debt capital receipts | Effective revenue deficit equals revenue deficit minus grants for creation of capital assets
  x: Since ERD = RD minus grants for creation of capital assets, ERD is less than or equal to RD.
[f9936cffed150] sub1
  Consider the following statements about deficits:
1. Fiscal deficit is always greater than primary deficit.
2. Effective revenue deficit can be greater than revenue deficit.
3. A zero primary deficit means borrowing only to pay interest.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | *1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: FD exceeds PD because interest is positive, and zero PD means borrowing equals interest; ERD is never greater than RD, so 2 is wrong.

## SUB 1 | Receipts, Expenditure and Deficits – Classification
[107:1:0] # Receipts
[107:1:1] TABLE head: Basis | Revenue receipts | Capital receipts
  [107:1:1.0] Liability | Create no liability | Create a liability
  [107:1:1.1] Assets | Do not reduce assets | Or reduce assets
  [107:1:1.2] Examples | Tax: income, corporation tax, GST, customs, excise; non-tax: interest receipts, dividends and profits, fees, fines, grants | Borrowings (market loans, T-Bills, World Bank loans), small savings/NSC, recovery of loans, disinvestment
[107:1:2] - **Order of classification:** **Revenue receipts → Tax revenue → Non-tax revenue → Capital receipts** (APPSC: A → C → D → B; the question was later deleted).
[107:1:3] - **UPSC 2025:** capital receipts create a liability or reduce assets (I ✓); borrowings and disinvestment are capital receipts (II ✓); **interest received** is **non-tax revenue**, it creates no liability (III ✗) → **I and II only**.
[107:1:4] - **Disinvestment** = capital receipt (reduces assets), **not** a revenue receipt (CDS 2017).
[107:1:5] - **Small savings** – not in the revenue account; debt receipts in the capital account (RAS 2018).
[107:1:6] - **Current (revenue) account income:** **corporation tax, profits of public enterprises, excise duties**; NSC sales and World Bank loans are capital (UPPCS 1998).
[107:1:7] - **Non-tax revenue of the Centre:** mainly interest, dividends and profits, fees; among mines, forests, opium and roads, the biggest head is **Roads and Bridges** (national-highway fees: ₹3,681 crore in 2012-13 BE, against forestry ₹23 crore, non-ferrous mining ₹27.5 crore); mines and forests yield mainly to the **States**.
[107:1:8] - **Paper taxes:** wealth tax, gift tax, estate duty – never large, now abolished.
[107:1:9] # Expenditure
[107:1:10] TABLE head: Pair | Distinction
  [107:1:10.0] Revenue vs capital | Revenue creates no asset (salaries, pensions, interest, subsidies, grants); capital creates assets or cuts liabilities (capital works, loans given, debt repayment)
  [107:1:10.1] Plan vs non-plan (to 2016-17) | Non-plan = interest, salaries, pensions, defence, subsidies, public administration (WBCS 2018)
  [107:1:10.2] Developmental vs non-developmental | Development = economic and social services (railways, power, S&T, education, health, grants to States); defence = non-development
[107:1:11] - **Interest payments** = **largest non-plan item (2015-16)** and the **most important revenue-account expenditure** (UPPCS 2008, 2015); still the largest single item of spending.
[107:1:12] - **Plan/non-plan ended 2017-18** (revenue/capital only), not 2010-11.
[107:1:13] - **Capital receipts < revenue receipts** – in 2010-11 and now (2026-27 BE: revenue ₹35.33 lakh crore, capital ₹18.14 lakh crore).
[107:1:14] # Deficits
[107:1:15] - **Budget (budgetary) deficit** = **total expenditure − total receipts** (all receipts, revenue + capital); discontinued from **1996-97**.
[107:1:16] - **Fiscal deficit** = total expenditure − receipts excluding borrowings; **primary deficit** = FD − interest; **revenue deficit** = revenue expenditure − revenue receipts.
[107:1:17] - Worked: FD ₹80,000 crore − interest ₹25,000 crore = **PD ₹55,000 crore**.
[107:1:18] - **Golden rule:** borrow only to invest, not to finance revenue spending.
[107:1:19] > Trap: "Excess of all expenditure over revenue receipts" is not the budget deficit – the budget deficit nets **all** receipts.
[107:1:20] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.8-9 (revenue/capital receipts, non-tax revenue, paper taxes), p.26 (budgetary deficit discontinued 1996-97); NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 09 p.11 (budget deficit = total receipts − total expenditure; PD); NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.7-8 (developmental vs non-developmental, golden rule); indiabudget.gov.in Receipt Budget 2012-13 ntr.pdf (non-tax heads); Union Budget 2026-27 At a Glance p.5; app notes 3:107 sub 3, 4, 10
[107:1:21] - **Treasury Bills:** short-term (91, 182, 364-day) borrowing of the Centre – a debt capital receipt, not long-term.
## SUB 2 | Budget Basics – Constitution, Ministry of Finance and Funds
[107:2:0] - **Budget** comes from the old **French "bougette"** = little bag; the Constitution does **not** use the word "budget".
[107:2:1] - **Article 112: Annual Financial Statement** – laid before **both Houses** by the President; the main instrument of **fiscal policy** (allocation, redistribution, stabilisation); primary purpose = **planning the allocation of resources**.
[107:2:2] - **Prepared and presented by** the **Budget Division, Department of Economic Affairs (DEA), Ministry of Finance** (UPSC 2010; APPSC 2018; APSLPRB 2023), with NITI Aayog and spending ministries; DEA also prepares budgets of States under President's Rule and UTs.
[107:2:3] - **Revenue estimates:** the **Ministry of Finance** (Department of Revenue with **CBDT and CBIC**) (Jharkhand PCS 2013).
[107:2:4] - **Departments of the Ministry of Finance:** Economic Affairs, Expenditure, Revenue, Financial Services, DIPAM, Public Enterprises. **Not departments:** **"Banking Division"**, **"Department of Banking Supervision"** (an RBI department).
[107:2:5] - **Financial year:** 1 April – 31 March; **Madhya Pradesh** decided in 2017 to move to **January–December**.
[107:2:6] # Articles and funds
[107:2:7] TABLE head: Article | Provision
  [107:2:7.0] 110(1)(a) | Finance Bill (tax provisions) as Money Bill
  [107:2:7.1] 112 / 112(3) | Annual Financial Statement / list of charged expenditure
  [107:2:7.2] 113 | Demands for Grants – only Lok Sabha votes
  [107:2:7.3] 114 / 114(3) | Appropriation Bill / no withdrawal from CFI without an Appropriation Act
  [107:2:7.4] 115 | Supplementary, additional or excess grants
  [107:2:7.5] 116 | Vote on account, vote of credit, exceptional grants
  [107:2:7.6] 266(1) / 266(2) | Consolidated Fund of India / Public Account
  [107:2:7.7] 267 | Contingency Fund – corpus ₹30,000 crore from FY 2021-22
[107:2:8] - **Consolidated Fund of India:** **all revenues**, loans raised and loan repayments received are credited to it; no withdrawal without a parliamentary law (UPSC 2011).
[107:2:9] - **Public Account:** provident funds, small savings, deposits – government acts as **banker**; withdrawals need **no appropriation**.
[107:2:10] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.2-3 (bougette, Budget Division DEA, Halwa ceremony, Art. 112, 114(3), 266); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.8, p.53 (Art. 112; DEA not Expenditure); Laxmikanth Indian Polity 01 p.363-364, p.370 (AFS, funds); app notes 3:107 sub 0
## SUB 3 | Budget History and Firsts
[107:3:0] TABLE head: Event | Fact
  [107:3:0.0] First budget of India | James Wilson, 7 April 1860
  [107:3:0.1] Interim Government budget 1947-48 | Liaquat Ali Khan
  [107:3:0.2] First budget of independent India | R.K. Shanmukham Chetty (first Finance Minister), 26 November 1947
  [107:3:0.3] First budget of the Republic | John Mathai (1950)
  [107:3:0.4] Separate Railway Budget | From 1924, on the Acworth Committee Report (1921)
  [107:3:0.5] Railway Budget merged | 2017 (Budget 2017-18) – after 92 years; last separate Railway Budget 2016-17
  [107:3:0.6] Date moved to 1 February | 2017-18 (from the last working day of February)
  [107:3:0.7] Plan/non-plan ended | 2017-18 (Expenditure Management Commission)
  [107:3:0.8] Outcome Budget; Gender Budget | 2005-06
  [107:3:0.9] Paperless (digital) Budget | Since 2021
[107:3:1] - **Why 1 February:** so the Budget is passed **before 31 March** and ministries can **spend from the start of the financial year** (no vote on account in normal years).
[107:3:2] - Until 2016 the **General Budget came after the Railway Budget** (a few days later).
[107:3:3] - **Railway Budget 2013-14:** hyper-luxury class **Anubhuti** (selected Rajdhani and Shatabdi trains).
[107:3:4] - **Morarji Desai** presented the **most budgets (10)**; 1973-74 "Black Budget"; 1997-98 "Dream Budget"; **Yashwant Sinha** moved the time from 5 pm to 11 am (2001).
[107:3:5] - **Nirmala Sitharaman** has presented every Budget since 2019.
[107:3:6] > Trap: Acworth reported in **1921**; separation came in **1924** – an option "1921" is the committee year, not the separation year.
[107:3:7] ~~ Sources: Laxmikanth Indian Polity 01 p.363-364 (Railway Budget separated 1924 on Acworth Committee Report 1921; merged 2017), p.366, p.369 (1 February, avoid vote on account); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.25-26 (James Wilson 1860, Chetty 1947, Morarji Desai 10, Black/Dream budgets; it calls the Acworth Committee "1924"); Rocket Indian Economy p.5 (1 February; merged 2017-18); app notes 3:107 sub 1
## SUB 4 | Types of Budgeting – Zero-based, Performance, Gender
[107:4:0] TABLE head: Type | Meaning
  [107:4:0.0] Zero-based (ZBB) | Every item justified afresh from zero each period; fresh evaluation of every item of expenditure; looks to future needs, not past spending
  [107:4:0.1] Performance | Links spending to activities and work done; from the USA – first Hoover Commission (1949); India late 1960s (ARC)
  [107:4:0.2] Outcome | Links outlays to outputs and outcomes; India 2005-06
  [107:4:0.3] Gender | Gender-sensitive allocation and assessment; not a separate budget for women
  [107:4:0.4] Balanced / surplus / deficit | Receipts = / > / < expenditure
[107:4:1] - **ZBB:** proposed by **Peter Pyhrr**; first adopted in the **USA**; first used in government by **Jimmy Carter** (Georgia, 1973 budget; US budget 1979).
[107:4:2] - **ZBB in India:** first in the **Department of Science and Technology (1983)**; adopted by the Government of India for all ministries from **1986-87** (the usual exam answer).
[107:4:3] - **Gender budgeting:** started with **Union Budget 2005-06** (Gender Budget Statement; Ramesh Singh dates it to 2006-07, ₹28,737 crore and gender-budget cells in 32 ministries). Components: aligning gender-equality policy with macro policy, gender assessment of outcomes, better targeting – a **separate budget for women is not** one.
[107:4:4] - **Gender Budget Statement = Statement 13**; 2026-27: **₹5.01 lakh crore (9.37%)** – highest ever. Part A 100% for women, Part B 30-99%, Part C below 30%.
[107:4:5] - **Child budget:** **Madhya Pradesh** presented one for the first time with its 2022-23 budget.
[107:4:6] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.5-6 (ZBB Phyrr, Carter; gender, outcome budget 2005); NextGen Indian Economy Part 1 - APPSC 04 p.26 (gender budgeting 2005-06, not a separate budget for women); Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.24-26 (ZBB, gender budgeting 2006-07); Rocket Indian Economy p.5 (ZBB first adopted USA); app notes 3:107 sub 2
## SUB 5 | Budget in Parliament – Stages, Motions and Grants
[107:5:0] TABLE head: Stage | Key facts
  [107:5:0.0] 1. Presentation | FM in Lok Sabha, on 1 February; President's recommendation; no discussion that day
  [107:5:0.1] 2. General discussion | Both Houses, 3-4 days; no cut motion, no vote
  [107:5:0.2] 3. Committee scrutiny | Departmental standing committees
  [107:5:0.3] 4. Voting on demands | Only Lok Sabha; cut motions here; guillotine on the last day
  [107:5:0.4] 5. Appropriation Bill | Art. 114
  [107:5:0.5] 6. Finance Bill | Money Bill; enacted within 75 days (Provisional Collection of Taxes Act, 1931)
[107:5:1] # Motions and special grants
[107:5:2] TABLE head: Motion / term | Meaning
  [107:5:2.0] Policy cut | Demand reduced to ₹1 – disapproval of policy
  [107:5:2.1] Economy cut | Reduced by a specified amount
  [107:5:2.2] Token cut | Reduced by ₹100 – a specific grievance
  [107:5:2.3] Guillotine | Speaker puts all outstanding demands to vote without further discussion
  [107:5:2.4] Vote on account (Art. 116) | Grant in advance for part of the year pending the budget; normally two months (one-sixth); 3-5 months in election years
  [107:5:2.5] Interim budget | Full statement of receipts and expenditure by the government in an election year
[107:5:3] - **Cut motion** is the motion linked with the Budget (not censure, call-attention or adjournment motions).
[107:5:4] - **Vote on account vs interim budget (UPSC 2011):** vote on account deals **only with expenditure**; interim budget has **both receipts and expenditure** (II ✓); both are used by a regular government in an election year, not a "caretaker" device (I ✗).
[107:5:5] - **Charged expenditure (Art. 112(3)):** discussable, not voted – President's emoluments, presiding officers' salaries, **SC judges' salaries and pensions**, **HC judges' pensions only**, CAG, UPSC, **debt charges**, court decrees.
[107:5:6] - **Public Accounts Committee** = Parliament's control over the **audit** of the Budget (examines CAG reports); approves excess grants first.
[107:5:7] - **Budget documents:** Budget Speech, AFS, Demands for Grants, Finance Bill + Memorandum, FRBM statements (MEFS, FPSS, MTFPS), Expenditure Budget, Receipt Budget, Expenditure Profile, **Outcome Budget**, Budget at a Glance.
[107:5:8] - **Process:** Budget circular in **September**; **Halwa ceremony** marks printing at North Block.
[107:5:9] ~~ Sources: Laxmikanth Indian Polity 01 p.365-370 (charged list, six stages, documents, cut motions ₹1/₹100, guillotine, vote on account two months/one-sixth, 3-5 months in election year, 75 days, PAC for excess grants); Ramesh Singh Indian Economy 06 p.27 (token, economy, policy cut; guillotine); NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 10 p.2-3 (Budget circular, President's recommendation, vote on account/interim budget); app notes 3:107 sub 5, 6, 7
## SUB 6 | Union Budget 2026-27 – Highlights
[107:6:0] - Presented **1 February 2026 (a Sunday)** by **Nirmala Sitharaman**; a **"Yuva Shakti-driven Budget"** with **three Kartavyas** – growth, human capital, inclusion; no big tax cuts.
[107:6:1] TABLE head: Item | 2026-27 BE
  [107:6:1.0] Total expenditure = total receipts | ₹53,47,315 crore
  [107:6:1.1] Revenue / capital receipts | ₹35,33,150 crore (66.07%) / ₹18,14,165 crore
  [107:6:1.2] Capex | ₹12.22 lakh crore
  [107:6:1.3] Fiscal deficit | 4.3% of GDP (₹16,95,768 crore); 2024-25 actual 4.8%, 2025-26 BE/RE 4.4%
  [107:6:1.4] Gross market borrowing | ₹17.2 lakh crore
  [107:6:1.5] Disinvestment ("other receipts") | ₹80,000 crore (2025-26 RE ₹33,837 crore)
  [107:6:1.6] Gross tax-GDP | 11.2%
[107:6:2] # Allocations
[107:6:3] TABLE head: Ministry / scheme | Allocation
  [107:6:3.0] Defence (highest) | ₹7,84,678 crore
  [107:6:3.1] Road Transport and Highways | ₹3,09,875 crore
  [107:6:3.2] Education | about ₹1.39 lakh crore
  [107:6:3.3] VB-G RAM G (replaces MGNREGA) | ₹95,692 crore
  [107:6:3.4] Jal Jeevan Mission | ₹67,670 crore
[107:6:4] - **Tax:** **new Income-tax Act, 2025** effective **1 April 2026**; **MAT cut 15% → 14%**.
[107:6:5] - **Rupee comes from:** **borrowings and other liabilities 24 paise** (largest), **income tax 21**, corporation tax 18, GST 15. **Rupee goes to:** **States' share of taxes 22** (largest), **interest 20**, central sector schemes 17, defence 11.
[107:6:6] - **Earlier:** **Purvodaya** (Budget 2024-25) – Bihar, Jharkhand, West Bengal, Odisha, Andhra Pradesh (**not Gujarat**); supplementary demands 2025-26: **Economic Stabilisation Fund ₹1 lakh crore**.
[107:6:7] - **States:** combined FD FY25 **3.2%** of GDP; **18 States** above 3% in FY24; **AP Budget 2026-27** expenditure **₹3,10,058 crore**.
[107:6:8] ~~ Sources: Union Budget 2026-27 At a Glance p.4-5 (₹53,47,315 crore, FD 4.3%, other receipts ₹80,000 crore); Union Budget 2026-27 Highlights p.17 (FD 4.3%); app notes 3:107 sub 8, 9, 10

  x: Wealth tax, gift tax and estate duty were described as paper taxes because they never generated large revenue and are now abolished. GST is not a paper tax.
[ncd5494bf184] sub1
  How is the fiscal deficit of the Union Government defined?
  Revenue expenditure minus revenue receipts | *Total expenditure minus total receipts excluding borrowings | Fiscal deficit minus interest payments | Total expenditure minus tax revenue only
  x: Fiscal deficit = total expenditure minus receipts excluding borrowings. Revenue expenditure minus revenue receipts is the revenue deficit; fiscal deficit minus interest payments is the primary deficit; subtracting only tax revenue ignores non-tax and non-debt capital receipts.
[nindian-economy-32-5] sub1
  How are dividends and profits from government enterprises classified?

  x: Dividends and profits are revenue receipts; land, machinery and loans to States are capital payments.
[nindian-economy-32-3] sub1
  Assertion (A): Revenue receipts do not create liability and do not reduce government assets.
Reason (R): Capital receipts may create liability or reduce financial assets.
  Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A | *Both A and R are true, but R is NOT the correct explanation of A | A is true, but R is false | A is false, but R is true
  x: Both are true: revenue receipts neither create a liability nor reduce assets, and capital receipts do one or the other. A fact about capital receipts does not explain revenue receipts, so R does not explain A.
[nindian-economy-32-4] sub1
  Which of the following pairs is correctly matched?
  Tax revenue – Non-tax revenue receipt | *Disinvestment – Capital receipt that reduces assets | Borrowings – Capital receipt that reduces assets | Interest receipts – Capital receipt that creates a liability
  x: Disinvestment is a capital receipt that reduces assets; borrowings create a liability, interest receipts are non-tax revenue and tax revenue comes from compulsory levies.
[nindian-economy-48-5] sub1

  x: Interest receipts are revenue receipts, like tax revenue, dividends/profits and fees; the others are capital receipts.

  x: Treasury Bills are short-term domestic borrowing instruments.

  x: Interest received creates no liability, so it is revenue; small savings are debt receipts in the capital account.

  x: In the Public Account the government acts as banker, so withdrawals need no appropriation.

  x: On the last day fixed for the discussion of demands, all outstanding demands are put to vote without further discussion. This procedure is called the guillotine.

=== NOTES 3:108 | Economic Survey - Structure and Key Themes of the Latest Survey
## SUB 0 | Survey Year → Signature Idea and Findings Asked
[108:0:0] TABLE head: Survey | Idea / finding
  [108:0:0.0] 2014-15 | JAM trinity (Jan Dhan, Aadhaar, Mobile) for targeted DBT
  [108:0:0.1] 2015-16 | Chakravyuha challenge – easy entry, blocked exit of firms
  [108:0:0.2] 2016-17 | Universal Basic Income – the "radical idea" against poor targeting and misallocation of welfare schemes; Twin Balance Sheet; Bengaluru property tax by satellite imagery; sanitation burden on women
  [108:0:0.3] 2017-18 | Pink cover on gender
  [108:0:0.4] 2018-19 (4 July 2019) | Behavioural economics – nudge; "pious obligation" to repay debts willingly
  [108:0:0.5] 2019-20 | Wealth creation – quotes Thiruvalluvar's Thirukkural: "Make money – there is no weapon sharper than it to sever the pride of your foes"; Thalinomics; theme "Enable Markets, Promote Pro-Business Policies, Strengthen Trust"
  [108:0:0.6] 2020-21 (Jan 2021) | Theme "Saving Lives and Livelihoods"; Bare Necessities Index; IMF estimate of 11.5% real growth for 2021-22
  [108:0:0.7] 2021-22 | 80 high-frequency indicators, "agile approach"
  [108:0:0.8] 2022-23 | Response to supply disruptions: cut taxes on fuel and certain imported products and raise food and fertiliser subsidies; projected 6.5% for 2023-24
  [108:0:0.9] 2023-24 | "Letting go"; FY24 real GDP growth 8.2%
  [108:0:0.10] 2024-25 | Deregulation; services robust in high-frequency indicators in FY25
  [108:0:0.11] 2025-26 | "Building Strategic Indispensability"
[108:0:1] - **ES 2016-17 on sanitation:** a "disproportionate burden" on women in households without toilets – **threat to life and safety** going out to defecate, and **reduced food and water intake** to avoid leaving the home (APPSC G1 2017 key: travelling long distances).
[108:0:2] - **ES 2016-17, Bengaluru:** the gap between **property-tax collection and potential** was estimated from **satellite imagery** of built-up area.
[108:0:3] - **A–R (APPSC G2 Mains 2025):** India's economy stayed **resilient** amid geopolitical shocks **because** fiscal and monetary policy kept macro and financial stability post-COVID; **8.2% growth in FY24** owed to the focus on **macroeconomic stability** – both A and R true, R explains A.
[108:0:4] ~~ Sources: Economic Survey 2016-17 summary (jsscacs.edu.in Economic_Survey_Summary_2016-2017.pdf: burden on women, safety, food and water intake); app notes 3:108 sub 2, 3, 4; other survey themes general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 1 | Who Prepares the Survey and How It Is Presented
[108:1:0] - Published every year by the **Ministry of Finance** – prepared by the **Economic Division, Department of Economic Affairs**, under the **Chief Economic Adviser (CEA)**; not RBI, NITI Aayog, Planning Commission, NDC or ISI.
[108:1:1] - Presented in **both Houses** during the Budget Session, **before the Union Budget** (a day or a few days earlier); a **convention**, not a constitutional duty (Art. 112 is the Budget).
[108:1:2] - **First Survey 1950-51**; separate from the Budget since **1964**; single volume + statistical appendix since 2021-22.
[108:1:3] - **First Economic Adviser** after Independence: **J.J. Anjaria**.
[108:1:4] TABLE head: CEA | Surveys
  [108:1:4.0] Arvind Subramanian | 2014-15 to 2017-18

## SUB 2 | Economic Survey 2025-26 – Theme and Data
[108:2:0] - Theme **"Building Strategic Indispensability"**; Chapter 1 "State of the Economy: **Pushing the Growth Frontier**"; **17 chapters** (new: **Artificial Intelligence**, **urbanisation**).
[108:2:1] TABLE head: Indicator | Value
  [108:2:1.0] Real GDP FY26 (First Advance Estimates) | 7.4%
  [108:2:1.1] FY27 projection | 6.8% – 7.2%
  [108:2:1.2] Potential growth | 7.0%
  [108:2:1.3] Sectors FY26 | Agriculture 3.1%, manufacturing 7.0%, services 9.1% ("stabilising anchor")
  [108:2:1.4] CPI inflation | 1.7% (Apr-Dec FY26)
  [108:2:1.5] Bank GNPA | 2.2% (multi-decade low)
  [108:2:1.6] Repo cut since Feb 2025 | 125 bps
[108:2:2] - Fiscal: FD **4.8%** in FY25 vs 4.9% budgeted; **S&P upgrade BBB- to BBB**; concerns – **State revenue deficits and unconditional cash transfers**.
[108:2:3] - External: low CAD and forex cover over 11 months of imports seen as **an external strength**.
[108:2:4] - Climate chapter: **adaptation over mitigation**; conclusion **Sreya vs Preya** (**Katha Upanishad**).
[108:2:5] - Statistics: National Accounts and IIP **rebased to 2022-23**; reforms of 2025 – GST overhaul, 100% FDI in insurance, nuclear power opened, Labour Codes (not abolition of GST).
[108:2:6] - Deregulation line: ES 2023-24 "**letting go**", ES 2024-25 central theme, ES 2025-26 from "regulation and control" to "**enabling**", **trust-based compliance** in place of inspection; public capex **₹12.2 lakh crore** for 2026-27.
[108:2:7] - **Earlier frameworks:** Twin Balance Sheet hidden NPAs exposed by the **RBI Asset Quality Review (2015)**; Chakravyuha and Twin Balance Sheet answered by the **IBC**; Bare Necessities Index (ES 2020-21) at **State level**, from **NSO 69th and 76th rounds**, excludes primary education; Centre's gross tax growth highest in **2016-17 (17.9%)**.

  The term ‘Chakravyuha challenge’ in the Economic Survey referred to which of the following situations?
  Low agricultural productivity and high food imports | High public debt and low tax collection | High inflation combined with low economic growth | *Easy entry but blocked exit of firms
  x: The Chakravyuha challenge described an economy in which firms could enter relatively easily but faced difficulty in exiting. The exit problem was subsequently addressed through the Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code.

=== NOTES 3:109 | Andhra Pradesh Economy After Bifurcation – Gsdp, Growth Rate, Sectoral Shares
## SUB 0 | Sectoral Shares in AP's GVA
[109:0:0] # Broad shares over time (current prices)
[109:0:1] TABLE head: Year (estimate) | Agriculture & allied | Industry | Services
  [109:0:1.0] At bifurcation, 2014-15 | 30% | 26% | 44% (largest)
  [109:0:1.1] TE 2016-17 average | Primary 30% | Secondary 19% | Tertiary 42% (largest)
  [109:0:1.2] 2015-16 | about 30-31% (primary) | – | –
  [109:0:1.3] 2016-17 (AE) | 31.77% (₹2,03,860 cr) | 22.23% | 46.0% (of ₹6,41,697 cr)
  [109:0:1.4] 2017-18 | about 34% (34.4%) – up from about 23% before bifurcation | about 22% | –
  [109:0:1.5] 2018-19 | about 34% (34.6% in today's revised series) | about 24% | 43.5% (2019 PYQ)
  [109:0:1.6] 2021-22 (AE) | 36.19% | 23.36% | 40.45% (≈ 42% in the PYQ options)
  [109:0:1.7] 2024-25 (FAE) | 35.29% | 23.18% | 41.53%
  [109:0:1.8] 2025-26 (FAE) | 33% (33.20% of GSDP, ₹5,39,454 of ₹17,62,357 cr) | 24% | 43%
[109:0:2] - **Largest sector at reorganisation (2014):** **services** – it stays the largest in every year.
[109:0:3] - **2011-12 to 2016-17:** **agriculture's share showed steady improvement** (it rose after Hyderabad's services and industry went to Telangana); industry's share fell.
[109:0:4] - **2015-16 at constant 2011-12 prices:** agriculture's share **23.33%** (estimate then; today's revised series gives about 27%).
[109:0:5] - **NITI Aayog:** agriculture gives **more than one-third** of AP's GSDP; AP's farm share ≈ **twice India's** (~18%); India's services ~53%, AP's ~42%.
[109:0:6] - **2024-25 at constant prices:** about **30 : 28 : 42** (agriculture ₹2.32, industry ₹2.23, services ₹3.29 lakh cr) – agriculture is smaller in real terms.
[109:0:7] - **Main occupation of AP's people = agriculture**: **45.6%** of workers (manufacturing 22.8%, services 31.6%; SES 2024-25); about 65% in 2019; rural casual labour 33.8%.
[109:0:8] - **Missing middle:** manufacturing only about **11% of GSDP**; **Industrial Development Policy 4.0 (2024-29)** puts manufacturing at the centre.
[109:0:9] # Sub-sectors (current prices)
[109:0:10] TABLE head: Sub-sector, 2024-25 (FAE) | Share of GVA
  [109:0:10.0] Livestock | 12% (largest)
  [109:0:10.1] Horticulture | 10%
  [109:0:10.2] Manufacturing | 10%
  [109:0:10.3] Fishing & aquaculture | 9%
  [109:0:10.4] Construction | 8%
  [109:0:10.5] Real estate / Other services | 8% each
  [109:0:10.6] Trade, hotels & restaurants | 7%
  [109:0:10.7] Crops (agriculture) | 4%
  [109:0:10.8] Communications | 2%
  [109:0:10.9] Forestry, railways | 1% each
[109:0:11] - **Livestock** = largest contributor to GVA at current prices in the **2021-22 AE** too (ahead of fishing, manufacturing, horticulture); about ₹1.5 lakh cr = **30.87%** of agri GVA (2023-24).
[109:0:12] - **2025-26:** **Manufacturing = Livestock (10.84% each)**, then **Fishing & Aquaculture 9.42%**, other services 8.81%, horticulture 8.78%.
[109:0:13] - **2015-16, highest GVA in agriculture & allied:** **crops (agriculture + horticulture)** ₹58,460 cr, ahead of livestock ₹36,219 cr, fishing ₹24,479 cr, forestry ₹2,361 cr (constant prices).
[109:0:14] - **At constant prices** the largest agri sub-sector is **fishing & aquaculture** (₹77,085 cr in 2024-25, ₹90,673 cr in 2025-26).
[109:0:15] - **Services sub-sectors:** largest GVA = **Trade, Hotels & Restaurants** (₹69,836 cr, 2024-25); smallest = **Railways and Communications**.

## SUB 1 | GSDP Size, Growth Rate and AP's Rank
[109:1:0] # Concepts
[109:1:1] - **GSDP = GVA + product taxes – product subsidies**; State income = value of all goods and services produced within the State, counted once.
[109:1:2] - Compiled by the **Directorate of Economics & Statistics (DES), AP** (not NSO) at current and constant **2011-12** prices; **3 sectors, 17 sub-sectors** (agriculture 5, industry 4 incl. **construction**, services **8**).
[109:1:3] - **Estimate cycle:** AE/FAE → FRE → SRE → TRE; figures are revised (e.g. 2024-25 real growth 9.24% FAE → 8.27% FRE).
[109:1:4] - **India's new GDP series** (MoSPI, 27 Feb 2026) has base **2022-23**; AP tables still use 2011-12.
[109:1:5] # Growth as first estimated (the figures PYQs use)

  [109:1:6.0] Before bifurcation | Undivided AP grew about 7% a year | –
  [109:1:6.1] 2015-16 | Only State with double-digit growth; AP 1st in GSDP growth; 10.95% (match PYQ); C&AG: 13.22% | 12.16% constant (series peak)

  [109:1:6.3] 2014-17 | Per capita income growth 31.18% | –
  [109:1:6.4] 2017-18 | 1st among States in GSDP growth (about 11.2%); 11.32% (FRE PYQ) | 10.09%
  [109:1:6.5] 2020-21 (AE) | 1.58% while India's GDP contracted | 1.52% (series low)

  [109:1:6.8] 2011-12 to 2019-20 | Average annual growth about 12% at current prices (about 7% real) | –

[109:1:9] - AP grew **slower than India in 2023-24** and **faster from 2024-25**; in 2025-26 faster on **both current and constant prices**.
[109:1:10] - Current-price record: **17.36% (2021-22)** in the earlier SRE (15.64% TRE).
[109:1:11] - SES 2025-26 was **not tabled in the Budget session**; released **12 April 2026**.

[109:1:14] - Share of India's GDP **4.7% (2023-24, EAC-PM)**; about 4.9% in 2025-26.

## SUB 2 | Sector-wise Growth Rates and Agriculture
[109:2:0] # Broad sectors (constant 2011-12 prices)
[109:2:1] TABLE head: Year | Agriculture & allied | Industry | Services | GSDP
  [109:2:1.0] 2014-15 | 3.55 | 12.58 | 8.98 | 9.20
  [109:2:1.1] 2015-16 | 8.30 | 10.74 | 12.08 | 12.16
  [109:2:1.2] 2016-17 | 14.98 (highest) | 12.48 | 1.76 | 8.34
  [109:2:1.3] 2017-18 | 18.23 (peak) | 5.75 | 7.98 | 10.09
  [109:2:1.4] 2018-19 | 3.54 | 3.17 | 4.84 | 5.36
  [109:2:1.5] 2023-24 (AE) | 1.69 | 8.02 | 5.21 | –
  [109:2:1.6] 2023-24 (FRE) | -1.58 | 7.52 | 7.71 | 6.18
  [109:2:1.7] 2024-25 (FAE) | 10.70 | 6.58 | 8.53 | 9.24
  [109:2:1.8] 2025-26 (FAE) | 10.14 | 9.15 | 10.67 (highest) | 9.88
[109:2:2] - **SES 2016-17:** maximum growth at constant prices = **agriculture** (14% then); agriculture is the **short-run engine of double-digit growth**.
[109:2:3] - **Average agricultural growth since bifurcation (to 2018): about 11%** (2014-15 to 2017-18 average of the constant-price rates above = 11.3%); 18.23% (2017-18) is the single-year peak, not the average.
[109:2:4] - **2016-17 (AE): Railways' GVA growth (-) 3.5%** (revised now to +0.56%).
[109:2:5] - **Q2 2025-26:** agriculture best-performing (13.5%); industry 10.6%, services 9.9%.
[109:2:6] # Sub-sectors
[109:2:7] - **Industry 2024-25:** **construction fastest (9.55%)**; mining slowest (2.70%); **manufacturing ≈ half of industry GVA** (₹1,06,281 of ₹2,22,741 cr). 2025-26: construction 14.18% (fastest). **Mining low -26.5% (2020-21)**.
[109:2:8] - **Services:** **Banking & Insurance highest growth in 2024-25 (14.25%)**; **Communications fastest in 2025-26 (15.13%)**; public administration lowest.
[109:2:9] - **Agriculture 2024-25:** **crops fastest (14.99%)**, fishing 11.29%, livestock 9.53%; 2025-26: **fishing & aquaculture fastest (17.69%)**.
[109:2:10] # Inputs, outputs and ranks
[109:2:11] - **Fertiliser use (% of allotment): 80.78% (2015-16) → 38.1% (2016-17 AE)**.
[109:2:12] - **White Paper (2014, Govt of AP)** on **Agriculture, Horticulture, Sericulture, Animal Husbandry, Dairy, Fisheries and Agricultural Marketing**: AP was left **without livestock-vaccine production and disease-diagnostic facilities** because the **Veterinary Biological Research Institute (Hyderabad) went to Telangana**.
[109:2:13] - Agriculture supports about 60% of people, so **GSDP swings with the monsoon**. Foodgrains: peak **123.57 lakh t (2019-20)**; drought dip **108.39 lakh t (2018-19)**.
[109:2:14] - SES 2024-25 target: **30% agricultural growth over five years** via **17 growth engines**; AP gives about **10%** of India's farm output.
[109:2:15] - **National ranks (SES 2025-26):** **1st** – eggs, fruits (**15.6%** of India), fish (**51 lakh t**, 2024-25), marine exports (**43%**; ~**75%** of cultured shrimp), tobacco exports (**67%**), **SHG-bank linkage**; **2nd** raw silk; **3rd** MGNREGS employment; meat **4th**; milk **7th**.
[109:2:16] - **India:** horticulture production has **exceeded foodgrain production since 2012-13**.
[109:2:17] - Horticulture output **365.92 lakh MT (2023-24)**; **ICAR Breed Conservation award – Punganur cattle**; **ICAR-NRC Banana** – AP largest banana exporter.
[109:2:18] > Exam angle: 2023-24 AE match = agriculture 1.69, industry 8.02, services 5.21. "Average agri growth since bifurcation" = about 11%.

## SUB 3 | Vision 2029, Swarna Andhra @2047 and Growth Strategy
[109:3:0] # Sunrise AP Vision 2029 (2016)
[109:3:1] TABLE head: Goal | Target year
  [109:3:1.0] Among the top three States | 2022
  [109:3:1.1] Best State in India | 2029
  [109:3:1.2] Leading / most preferred global investment destination | 2050 (2050-51)
[109:3:2] - **Sunrise AP Vision 2029** (launched **2016**): **growth target 12%** a year (CAGR) till 2029; PCI target about **₹9.61 lakh** by 2029; objective: **double per capita income every five years**.
[109:3:3] - **HDI 0.9 by 2029** through the **saturation approach**.
[109:3:4] - Other 2029 targets: **30% renewable energy share; 100% drought proofing; 100% solid and liquid waste management**.
[109:3:5] - Priorities aligned with the **UN Sustainable Development Goals** (not the MDGs).
[109:3:6] - **Mission-based development: 7 Missions, 5 Campaigns and 5 Grids**.
[109:3:7] - **Growth engines:** about **40 growth engines** giving about **80% of GSDP** were identified for double-digit growth.
[109:3:8] - Budget 2016-17: cornerstones of the Vision 2029 strategy – keyed answer **developing the mining sector** (with infrastructure, human capacity and the campaigns).
[109:3:9] - **VIADUCT** = AP's economic-transformation strategy acronym (V-I-A-D-U-C-**T = Technology**).
[109:3:10] - **Vision 2020** (1999, **N. Chandrababu Naidu**) = first long-term vision of undivided AP.
[109:3:11] # Swarna Andhra @2047 (13 Dec 2024, CM N. Chandrababu Naidu)
[109:3:12] - **10 Padi Sutralu** (ten principles): Zero Poverty; Population Management & HRD; Skilling & Employment; Water Security; Farmer-Agri Tech; Global Best Logistics; Energy & Fuel cost optimisation; Product Perfection; Swachh Andhra; Deep Tech. **Universal Basic Income is NOT one.**

## SUB 4 | Bifurcation's Impact and AP in India
[109:4:0] # What bifurcation did to the economy
[109:4:1] - AP bifurcated on **2 June 2014** (AP Reorganisation Act, 2014); residual AP had **13 districts**.
[109:4:2] - **Hyderabad alone ≈ 48%** of undivided AP's total revenue of **₹70,548 crore (2012-13)**.
[109:4:3] - **APRA, 2014 gives AP no share of Hyderabad's tax revenue**: taxes go to the State where the property is or where the tax is assessed (Sec. 50).
[109:4:4] - Loss of Hyderabad's revenue base → AP a **chronic revenue-deficit State**; capital and major institutions stayed with Telangana → new capital **Amaravati**.
[109:4:5] - Hyderabad-centred services and industry went to Telangana → **agriculture's share rose**; new IT parks at **Visakhapatnam, Tirupati, Vijayawada**.
[109:4:6] - **Assertion-reason (2017):** better governance and service delivery was stressed (R true), but there was **no turnaround in fiscal performance** (A false) – keyed "A false, R true".
[109:4:7] - **1999-2006:** AP's **primary sector ranked 2nd among States by compound growth rate**.
[109:4:8] # AP in national aggregates
[109:4:9] TABLE head: Indicator | AP
  [109:4:9.0] Area | 4.96% of India
  [109:4:9.1] Population | 4.09%, 10th (Census 2011)
  [109:4:9.2] Coastline | 1,053.07 km, 3rd (Gujarat, Tamil Nadu ahead)
  [109:4:9.3] Largest port by cargo | Visakhapatnam
  [109:4:9.4] Exports | About 5.8% of India's exports (2020-21, APEDB); 6th largest exporter, 824 commodities to 194 countries (2024-25)
  [109:4:9.5] Mines and minerals | 50% of own non-tax revenue (2024-25 RE)
[109:4:10] - **Sixth Economic Census (2013-14):** undivided AP **7.25%** of India's establishments, **5th**; in present AP **74%** of establishments are **rural**; employment in establishments grew **28%** between the 5th and 6th Censuses.
[109:4:11] # AP Economic Development Board (APEDB)
[109:4:12] - Statutory body under the **AP Economic Development Board Act, 2018**; **single point of contact for all investments**.
[109:4:13] - **Chairman = Chief Minister** (not the Governor); publishes export reports.
[109:4:14] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt) Sec. 50 (arrears of taxes), Sec. 46; AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.6; old app notes 3:109 sub 0-1; Economic Census and 1999-2006 rank figures from the printed PYQ keys, not re-checked

=== NOTES 3:110 | Ap Per Capita Income, District Disparities and Ap's Position Among States
## SUB 0 | AP Per Capita Income – Level, Growth and the Gap with India
[110:0:0] # AP vs India PCI (current prices, NSDP-based)
[110:0:1] TABLE head: Year | AP PCI (₹) | AP growth | India PCI (₹) | India growth
  [110:0:1.0] 2014-15 (bifurcation) | 93,903 | 13.31% | 86,647 | 9.5%
  [110:0:1.1] 2015-16 | 1,08,002 | 15.01% | 94,797 | 9.4%
  [110:0:1.2] 2017-18 | 1,42,054 (AE, then); 1,38,299 revised | 14.6% | 1,15,224 | 9.9%
  [110:0:1.3] 2019-20 | 1,60,341 | 4.10% (slowest) | 1,32,341 | 5.1%
  [110:0:1.4] 2021-22 | 1,93,703 (about ₹2.07 lakh in the 2023 PYQ) | 15.26% | 1,50,906 | 18.6% (fastest)
  [110:0:1.5] 2023-24 | 2,42,479 (AE) → 2,37,951 (FRE) → 2,39,311 (SRE) | 8.2-8.9% | 1,84,205 (PE) | 8.7%
  [110:0:1.6] 2024-25 | 2,68,653 (FAE) → 2,66,699 (FRE) | 11.4-12.9% | 2,00,162 | 8.7%
  [110:0:1.7] 2025-26 (FAE) | 2,94,507 | 10.43% | 2,19,575 | 6.9%
[110:0:2] - **Older AP figures:** **₹7,781 in 1960**; **₹62,912 (2010-11)** → **₹71,540 (2011-12 AE**, quoted with the 2012-13 budget).
[110:0:3] - AP's PCI has been **above India's every year since 2014-15**; 2025-26 gap ≈ **₹75,000** (AP ≈ **1.34 times** India).
[110:0:4] - **All-India PCI 2023-24 (PE) ₹1,84,205 is lower than AP's 2023-24 PCI** – both statements of the 2025 PYQ are correct.
[110:0:5] - Per capita GSDP (gross) ≠ PCI (net): 2023-24 per capita GSDP ₹2,70,295 vs India's per capita GDP ₹2,15,935.
[110:0:6] - **Telangana vs AP PCI:** 6% higher (2004-05) → **1.3 times (2013-14)** → **1.35 times (2024-25)**; pre-1956 Telangana had higher PCI and revenue (SRC).
[110:0:7] # Vision targets
[110:0:8] - **Sunrise AP Vision 2029:** PCI about **₹9.61 lakh by 2029**; **double PCI every five years**.
[110:0:9] - **Swarna Andhra @2047:** PCI **above $42,000 (about ₹55 lakh)**; **₹5.43 lakh** = **per capita GSDP** target for 2028-29 (not PCI).
[110:0:10] - **India's PCI (older series):** per capita NNI at current prices **₹74,380 (2013-14)**; **₹35,993 (2010-11, 2004-05 prices)**; about ₹6,835 (≈ ₹7,500) in 1991-92; it grew **8.6%** from 2016-17 to 2017-18.
[110:0:11] - India's first national estimate (National Income Committee, 1951): **₹225** for 1948-49. India = **lower-middle-income economy** (~$2,760).
[110:0:12] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 (src/economy/ap-socio-economic-survey-2024-25.txt) p.23-24 (Table 2.8); old app notes 3:110 sub 1-3, 8; 1960, 2011-12 AE and Vision 2029 figures from the printed PYQ keys, not re-checked
## SUB 1 | District and Regional Disparities
[110:1:0] # Inter-district disparity
[110:1:1] - **Inter-district disparity in PCI is high**: Visakhapatnam / Krishna have roughly twice the PCI of Srikakulam, Vizianagaram, Anantapur.
[110:1:2] - **Best district in GDDP (current prices), 2017-18 (13 old districts): East Godavari** (Krishna close behind).
[110:1:3] - **2023-24 GDDP:** highest **Visakhapatnam** ₹1,14,859 cr; lowest Parvathipuram Manyam. **Per capita GDDP:** highest **Visakhapatnam ₹5,45,588**; lowest **Srikakulam ₹1,59,029**.
[110:1:4] - **Visakhapatnam ranked 1st in PCI (FY 2025-26)**; drivers: Vizag Port, RINL, Hindustan Shipyard, HPCL, IT parks, **Eastern Naval Command**.
[110:1:5] - **District Domestic Product** is compiled by the **State Directorate of Economics and Statistics** (not NSO).
[110:1:6] - **Highest number of illiterates** (Vision document): **Kurnool**.
[110:1:7] - **Post offices, March 2016:** keyed **Anantapuramu** (G2 Mains 2017); the app's earlier notes said **Chittoor** – not verified. Latest: Prakasam (post offices); Visakhapatnam (bank branches, mobile connections).
[110:1:8] # Regions
[110:1:9] TABLE head: Region | Feature
  [110:1:9.0] Central and South Coastal (Krishna-Guntur belt) | Agricultural development concentrated here; historically most developed
  [110:1:9.1] Rayalaseema | Rain-shadow, drought-prone; now horticulture hub – 43% of area, 52% of output
  [110:1:9.2] North Coastal Andhra | Backward; tribal hinterland
[110:1:10] - **APRA package: 7 backward districts** = **4 Rayalaseema** (Kurnool, Kadapa, Anantapur, Chittoor) + **3 North Coastal** (Srikakulam, Vizianagaram, Visakhapatnam); **₹50 crore** per district a year.
[110:1:11] - **MPI (NFHS-5, 13 old districts):** lowest **West Godavari 2.42%**, highest Kurnool 12.84%; AP **6.06%** vs India **14.96%**.
[110:1:12] - **Aspirational Districts Programme** (NITI Aayog, January 2018, 112 districts): AP's original three **Vizianagaram, Visakhapatnam, YSR Kadapa**; after 2022 **Alluri Sitharamaraju** and **Parvathipuram Manyam** replaced Visakhapatnam and Vizianagaram.
[110:1:13] - **P4** targets the gap between high average PCI and lasting poverty.
[110:1:14] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:110 sub 5-7; 3:109 sub 9; GDDP 2017-18, illiterates and post-office keys from the printed PYQ keys, not re-checked
## SUB 2 | AP's Position Among States
[110:2:0] # PCI and GSDP ranks
[110:2:1] - **2022-23 (MoSPI):** AP's PCI (~₹2.2 lakh) is **below Haryana's**, above Punjab, Rajasthan, West Bengal; **Telangana (₹3.54 lakh)** is the richest major State, then Karnataka, Haryana, Tamil Nadu.
[110:2:2] - **2017-18 PCI rank:** app MCQ keys **5th**; the 2019 G2 PYQ (4th/5th/6th/7th) is left unkeyed – the rank depends on which States are counted.
[110:2:3] - **Per capita NSDP 2023-24 (ES 2025-26):** **Sikkim** highest (₹5,87,743), **Goa** second (₹5,85,953), **Bihar** lowest (₹62,201).
[110:2:4] - **EAC-PM working paper (Sept 2024):** share of national GDP 2023-24 **AP 4.7%, Telangana 4.9%**; five southern States **over 30%**; Telangana's economy = **96.5%** of AP's (2024-25); metro Hyderabad **47.5%** of Telangana's output.
[110:2:5] - Largest State economy = **Maharashtra**. Gini of State PCI **0.230 (1994-95) → 0.261 (2025-26)** = divergence.
[110:2:6] - **16th Finance Commission:** **42.5%** weight to **income distance** → AP's above-average PCI **lowers** its share.
[110:2:7] - **BRICS (original five): Russia** has the highest per capita income – in 2010 and still in 2024 (World Bank: Russia $14,889, China $13,303); not China.
[110:2:8] # SDG India Index (NITI Aayog)
[110:2:9] - **2020-21:** **Kerala 1st**; AP among the **top five** (shared 4th) – it did **not** "retain 2nd rank" (A true, R false).
[110:2:10] - **2023-24** (12 July 2024): **Kerala and Uttarakhand top (79)**; **AP 74** (Front Runner).
[110:2:11] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:110 sub 4; World Bank country pages (data.worldbank.org/country/ru, /china), 2024; SDG India Index 2020-21 ranks general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 3 | Per Capita Income – Concept
[110:3:0] - **PCI = NSDP ÷ population** (net); **standard of living** is shown by PCI.
[110:3:1] - PCI **rises only when income grows faster than population**; it **can fall in a developing country even as national income rises** when **population grows faster than income** (A and R true, R explains A).
[110:3:2] - PCI is an **average**: it **hides inequality** and cost-of-living differences.
[110:3:3] - Per capita GSDP and PCI are **not interchangeable** terms.
[110:3:4] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:110 sub 0

[nb94b383912a] sub3
  Which of the following is NOT correct regarding per capita GSDP and per capita income?

[n07ac5a771dd] sub3
  On which aggregate is per capita income in the Socio-Economic Survey based?

[nab65bed1149] sub3
  Per capita income rises only when national income grows in which of the following ways?

  x: PCI = income / population, so it rises only if income grows faster than population.
[nad0fc5251ac] sub3
  Per capita income alone cannot fully show economic well-being mainly because it:
  *Is an average that conceals inequality and cost-of-living differences | Covers only the agricultural sector | Is compiled only once a decade | Is measured only at constant prices
  x: Being an average, PCI hides inequality and ignores cost-of-living variation.
[f18789fc04800] sub3
  When can per capita income fall in a developing country even as national income rises?
  *population grows faster than income | inflation falls | depreciation is ignored | income grows faster than population
  x: PCI = income ÷ population, so it falls if population growth exceeds income growth.

=== NOTES 3:111 | Bifurcation and Ap's Natural Resource Endowment
## SUB 0 | Minerals and Energy Endowment
[111:0:0] TABLE head: Mineral | Where in AP
  [111:0:0.0] Limestone | Jammalamadugu, Mydukur (Kadapa basin, superior cement grade); Kurnool, Guntur, Palnadu, NTR
  [111:0:0.1] Asbestos (chrysotile) | Pulivendula, YSR Kadapa
  [111:0:0.2] Barytes | Mangampet (YSR Kadapa) – India's largest deposit
  [111:0:0.3] Uranium | Tummalapalle (YSR Kadapa)
  [111:0:0.4] Beach sand (ilmenite, rutile, zircon, garnet, monazite) | Srikakulam, Visakhapatnam, Kakinada coast
  [111:0:0.5] Bauxite | Araku, Chintapalli, Paderu
  [111:0:0.6] Apatite / ball clay | Kasipatnam (Visakhapatnam) / West Godavari
  [111:0:0.7] Manganese | Garividi (Vizianagaram, ferro-alloys); old Visakhapatnam district led world output 1892-1929
  [111:0:0.8] Mica | Nellore – India's major mica belt
  [111:0:0.9] Gold | Jonnagiri (Kurnool) – India's first private gold mine; Ramagiri (Anantapur)
[111:0:1] - **Limestone:** AP **3rd** in India by quantity (2015-16), after Rajasthan and MP; about **12%** of India's output (2019-20, Indian Minerals Yearbook 2020).
[111:0:2] - **Mining revenue 2022-23: ₹4,756 crore** (Director of Mines and Geology).
[111:0:3] # Oil, gas, coal and power
[111:0:4] - **DGH:** AP's natural resource = **oil and natural gas** of the **Krishna-Godavari basin** (~52,000 sq km), hydrocarbon resource base about **1,130 million metric tonnes**.
[111:0:5] - **Coal went mainly to Telangana** (SCCL: **Telangana 51%, GoI 49%**); the **Twelfth Schedule** keeps SCCL linkages for AP plants unchanged.
[111:0:6] - Onshore oil and gas royalty → **the producing State**.
[111:0:7] - **CGS power** split by **five years' consumption** (AP ≈ 46%), not the population ratio.
[111:0:8] - Srisailam and Nagarjuna Sagar: **left bank → Telangana**, right bank → AP; June 2021 dispute over generation below **834 feet** at **Srisailam**.
[111:0:9] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 (src/economy/ap-socio-economic-survey-2024-25.txt) p.121 (focus minerals, limestone blocks); old app notes 3:111 sub 1; limestone share/rank, mining revenue and DGH figures from the printed PYQ keys, not re-checked
## SUB 1 | Forests, Protected Areas and Forest Law
[111:1:0] # Forest cover (ISFR, FSI Dehradun, biennial)
[111:1:1] - **ISFR** is **biennial**, published by the **Forest Survey of India, Dehradun**.
[111:1:2] - **ISFR 2021: AP's forest cover rose by 647 sq km over 2019** – the largest increase among States.
[111:1:3] - ISFR 2023: **forest + tree cover 21.74%** of area; forest area incl. scrub 38,436.58 sq km (**23.59%**, includes 8,351.62 sq km scrub); **Recorded Forest Area 37,258 sq km – 8th** (ISFR 2021); forest = canopy **≥10%**.
[111:1:4] - Old districts: most cover **Visakhapatnam**; largest area **YSR Kadapa**; least **Krishna**. National Forest Policy **1988**: 33% (66% in hills).
[111:1:5] # Protected areas and forest types
[111:1:6] - **13 Wildlife Sanctuaries, 1 Tiger Reserve, 3 National Parks**; the tiger reserve = **Nagarjunasagar-Srisailam (NSTR)**, India's largest, in **Nallamala** (AP's largest forest).
[111:1:7] - Mostly **tropical dry deciduous**; **mangroves** (Coringa) at the Godavari and Krishna mouths; moist deciduous in the north-coastal hills.
[111:1:8] - **Red Sanders:** **Chittoor and YSR Kadapa** (Seshachalam, Veligonda, Lankamala, **Nallamala**, Talakona); endemic to the southern Eastern Ghats; IUCN **Endangered** (2021).
[111:1:9] # Laws, schemes and minor forest produce
[111:1:10] TABLE head: Law / scheme | Key point
  [111:1:10.0] AP Forest Act, 1967 | S.3 power to reserve; S.15 final notification; S.28 power to declare a protected forest to cease to be protected
  [111:1:10.1] FCA 1980 | Renamed Van (Sanrakshan Evam Samvardhan) Adhiniyam from 29 Dec 2023
  [111:1:10.2] PESA 1996 / FRA 2006 | Gram Sabha controls MFP / recommends diversion for public utilities
  [111:1:10.3] Green India Mission (2011, NAPCC) | 5 mha new + 5 mha better forest (not 10 mha new)
  [111:1:10.4] Nagar Van Yojana / Nagaravanam | 61 Nagar Vans / 37 city forests
  [111:1:10.5] CAMPA | Spends compensatory levies on afforestation
[111:1:11] - **Minor Forest Produce:** collected and sold mainly by the **Girijan Cooperative Corporation (GCC)**, HQ **Visakhapatnam**; runs MSP for MFP; **beedi leaf nationalised 1971**.
[111:1:12] - **JFM** (Vana Samrakshana Samithis) from **1992**; Community Forest Management from **2002**; **FRA titles 2,27,300** individual (31 Aug 2026).
[111:1:13] - **Neeru-Chettu:** making AP **drought-free** through water conservation and tree planting.
[111:1:14] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:111 sub 3-5; ISFR 2021 increase (647 sq km) general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 2 | Area, Population and Coast at Bifurcation
[111:2:0] TABLE head: Item | Residual AP | Telangana
  [111:2:0.0] Area | 1,60,205 sq km (≈ 58.2%) → 1,62,970 sq km (7 Khammam mandals added) | 1,12,077 sq km
  [111:2:0.1] Population (2011) | 4.96 crore (≈ 58%) | 3.52 crore
  [111:2:0.2] APRA "population ratio" | 58.32 | 41.68
  [111:2:0.3] Coast and ports | Entire coastline, all ports | Landlocked
[111:2:1] - AP = **8th largest State** in area (SES 2024-25: "8th largest state … 1,62,970 sq km"; some lists count it 7th after J&K became a UT).
[111:2:2] - **Coastline 1,053.07 km, 3rd** after Gujarat and Tamil Nadu (old figure 974 km).
[111:2:3] - **One major port (Visakhapatnam)**; Krishnapatnam = most cargo among non-major ports; **Ramayapatnam** most advanced of 4 greenfield ports.
[111:2:4] - About **29%** of India's fish; about **75%** of cultured-shrimp exports.
[111:2:5] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt) Sec. 2(h) (population ratio 58.32 : 41.68); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.6; old app notes 3:111 sub 0
## SUB 3 | Water and Agricultural Endowment
[111:3:0] - **Schedule XI:** tribunal allocations unchanged; priority **drinking water > irrigation > power**.
[111:3:1] - Krishna: KWDT-I **811 TMC**, ad hoc **AP 512 : Telangana 299**.
[111:3:2] - **Polavaram** = national project (**Sec. 90**); **960 MW**, **7.20 lakh acres**, **80 TMC** to the Krishna basin.
[111:3:3] - Deltas of the **Godavari, Krishna and Penna**; **Rayalaseema** rain-shadow and drought-prone; **6 agro-climatic zones**.
[111:3:4] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:111 sub 2

=== OWN MCQs of 3:111 (76)
[n98d36064bac] sub0
  After the bifurcation of Andhra Pradesh, to whom did the coal resources mainly go?
  *Telangana | Residual Andhra Pradesh | The Union Government | Both States equally
  x: Coal went mainly to Telangana, while residual Andhra Pradesh retained important coastal and basin minerals.
[nc5dbf30158f] sub0
  Mangampet in YSR Kadapa district has India's largest deposit of which mineral?
  Uranium | *Barytes | Bauxite | Limestone
  x: Mangampet has India's largest barytes deposit; Tummalapalle (YSR Kadapa) is AP's uranium site and AP's bauxite is in Araku, Chintapalli and Paderu.
