=== NOTES 3:112 | Bifurcation and Ap's State Revenue – Revenue Deficit, Loss of Hyderabad, Resource Gap
## SUB 0 | Resource Gap and Deficits of the Bifurcation Year (2014-15)
[112:0:0] # Dates
[112:0:1] - **Appointed day = 2 June 2014** (both AP and Telangana came into being); the Act got presidential **assent on 1 March 2014** – not "2 March" or "1 April".
[112:0:2] # Resource gap
[112:0:3] - **Resource gap** = **revenue deficit** (revenue expenditure minus revenue receipts) for the **first ten months** (2 June 2014 – 31 March 2015).
[112:0:4] - Method difference: the **Centre** used standardised accounting and **excluded new State schemes** (e.g. farm-loan waiver); **AP** counted its **actual expenditure**.
[112:0:5] TABLE head: Estimate by | Resource gap 2014-15
  [112:0:5.0] Union Finance Ministry | ₹4,118 crore
  [112:0:5.1] CAG (2 Jun 2014 – 31 Mar 2015) | ₹13,776 crore
  [112:0:5.2] State (AP) | ₹16,078 crore
[112:0:6] - **Central assistance to bridge the 2014-15 gap**: **₹3,979.50 crore**, released in instalments from 2 June 2014 up to **2016-17**.
[112:0:7] - Bifurcation-year gap: **more than ₹15,000 crore** on the State's count.
[112:0:8] # Fiscal deficit 2014-15
[112:0:9] - **CAG**: fiscal deficit **₹31,717 crore = 6.10% of GSDP** in 2014-15 – **slightly more than double the FRBM ceiling of 3%**.
[112:0:10] - Before 2014-15 the undivided State ran **revenue surpluses for about eight years** (2006-07 to 2013-14 – printed APPSC key; not checked in the library); bifurcation turned it into a large revenue deficit.
[112:0:11] > Exam angle: CAG figures – RD **₹13,776 cr**, FD **6.10%** (≈ double the 3% ceiling); gap grant **₹3,979.50 cr**; appointed day **2 June 2014**.
[112:0:12] ~~ Sources: Millennium Post on CAG State Finances report (FY15: RD ₹13,776 cr, FD ₹31,717 cr = 6.10% of GSDP); AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt, s.2); old app notes 3:112 sub 1, 3; revenue-surplus years general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 1 | Loss of Hyderabad – Revenue Base and Challenges
[112:1:0] - **Hyderabad region (with Rangareddy and Medak)** gave **nearly 50%** of undivided AP's **tax and non-tax revenue**.
[112:1:1] - **Srikrishna Committee (2010)**: **Hyderabad and Rangareddy's share of the State's GDP from modern services** rose from **18% (1999-2000) to about 25% (2005-06)**.
[112:1:2] - Revenue lost: **corporate taxes, stamp duties, commercial taxes** (not agricultural land revenue).
[112:1:3] - **APRA 2014 gives AP no share of Hyderabad's tax revenue** – taxes accrue to the State where the activity is (Telangana); only the 13th FC award was split by population (Sec. 46).
[112:1:4] - The bifurcation's **adverse effect on revenue fell on Andhra Pradesh State** (residual AP), not on Telangana.
[112:1:5] TABLE head: Asset lost | Effect
  [112:1:5.0] HITEC City, Financial District | Loss of IT/ITeS exports
  [112:1:5.1] Genome Valley pharma cluster; corporate and bank head offices; commercial real estate; MSME supply chains | Narrower tax base (GST, stamp duty, motor-vehicle tax)
  [112:1:5.2] Loss of Hyderabad as a whole | Structural revenue deficit
[112:1:6] - Residual AP kept its **agricultural base** – that was not lost.
[112:1:7] - Division followed the **population ratio 58.32 : 41.68** (AP : Telangana).
[112:1:8] - **Schedule IX and X institutions:** Schedule IX = government companies and corporations, divided as recommended by the expert committee headed by **Sheela Bhide**; **Schedule X = 142 institutions** (most in Telangana); AP wants assets shared in the population ratio, Telangana by location.
[112:1:9] - **Biggest challenges after 2014 (all of them)**: building **infrastructure for a new capital**, moving the **administration from Hyderabad to Amaravati**, and a **severe financial crisis with scarcity of human resources**.
[112:1:10] > Exam angle: Hyderabad ≈ **50%** of revenue; Srikrishna: Hyderabad + Rangareddy ≈ **25%** of modern-services GDP; **no share** of Hyderabad's taxes for AP.
[112:1:11] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.46, Schedules IX-X); old app notes 3:112 sub 0; Srikrishna Committee figures as printed in the PYQ explanation (not checked in the library)
## SUB 2 | Deficits After 2014-15
[112:2:0] TABLE head: Year | Revenue deficit | Fiscal deficit
  [112:2:0.0] 2022-23 BE | ₹17,036 cr ≈ ₹17,000 cr = 1.27% of GSDP; actual ₹43,487 cr (3.3%) | ₹48,724 cr (3.64%, BE)
  [112:2:0.1] 2023-24 | ₹38,683 cr | ₹62,720 cr (4.3%)
  [112:2:0.2] 2024-25 (Finance Accounts) | ₹60,285 cr (3.79%) | ₹81,362 cr (5.11%)
  [112:2:0.3] 2025-26 RE | ₹41,118 cr (2.33%) | ₹80,567 cr (4.57%)
  [112:2:0.4] 2026-27 BE | ₹22,002 cr (1.11%) | ₹75,868 cr (3.84%)
[112:2:1] - CAG monthly accounts: RD ₹54,355 crore by Nov 2025; CAG flagged a high revenue deficit three years running.
[112:2:2] - National: States' combined fiscal deficit FY25 ≈ **3.2% of GDP**; revenue balances worsened in **18 States** (FY19–FY25).
[112:2:3] > Exam angle: 2022-23 budget – RD about **₹17,000 crore = 1.27% of GSDP** (the actual was far higher).
[112:2:4] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:112 sub 4; AP Budget 2026-27 Explanatory Memorandum (2025-26 RE, 2026-27 BE); 2022-23 BE figures general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 3 | Power Sector – Reforms, Discoms and Dues
[112:3:0] - **Department of Expenditure** borrowing window (Budget 2021-22): extra borrowing of **0.5% of GSDP** linked to **power-sector reforms**.
[112:3:1] - **4 February 2021**: AP became the **second State** to complete the stipulated reforms – **DBT of electricity subsidy to farmers** (Srikakulam pilot from Sep 2020) – and could borrow an extra **₹1,515 crore**.
[112:3:2] - **UDAY**: AP signed on **24 June 2016** – the **13th State**; State took over about **₹11,000 crore** of DISCOM debt.
[112:3:3] - CAG on power subsidy: spending at **121% (2023)** and **120% (2024)** of budget estimates.
[112:3:4] - **Power dues**: under the power-sharing arrangement **Telangana has to pay Andhra Pradesh** for power supplied; on 29 Aug 2022 the Union Power Ministry (Sec. 92, APRA) ordered Telangana to pay about **₹6,756 crore** to APGENCO; the **Telangana High Court quashed** the order (Oct 2023).
[112:3:5] > Exam angle: AP = **second** State (not first) in DoE power reforms; dues flow **Telangana → AP**.
[112:3:6] ~~ Sources: The Hans India 4 Feb 2021 and Mercom India (Finance Ministry release: AP 2nd State, ₹1,515 cr, DBT); AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.92, Twelfth Schedule); old app notes 3:112 sub 7
## SUB 4 | Demands to the Centre – Special Category Status
[112:4:0] - **N. Chandrababu Naidu** demanded **Special Category Status (SCS)** citing post-bifurcation revenue loss.
[112:4:1] - SCS for **5 years** was promised on the floor of the **Rajya Sabha (2014)**.
[112:4:2] - Under SCS the **Centre bears 90%** of CSS costs (ordinary States 60%) plus tax breaks.
[112:4:3] - Centre's refusal: the **14th FC** ended the general/special category distinction and raised devolution **32% → 42%**.
[112:4:4] - Chronology: **APRA (2014) → 14th FC award (2015) → TDP leaves NDA over SCS (2018)**.
[112:4:5] TABLE head: Provision | Demand / grant
  [112:4:5.0] Sec. 94(1) | Tax incentives
  [112:4:5.1] Sec. 46 | Backward-area packages (Rayalaseema, North Coastal)
  [112:4:5.2] Sec. 94(3) | Special support for capital buildings (₹2,500 crore released)
[112:4:6] - Other demands: revenue deficit grants; full Polavaram funding at revised cost; capital-city grants.
[112:4:7] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.46, 94); old app notes 3:112 sub 2
## SUB 5 | Central Assistance and Finance Commissions
[112:5:0] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [112:5:0.0] RD grant for 2014-15 | ₹3,979.50 crore, released in instalments up to 2016-17
  [112:5:0.1] Special Assistance Measure (SAM) | Centre funds 90% of externally aided projects – alternative to SCS
  [112:5:0.2] 14th FC (from 2015-16) | Separate award for each successor State (Sec. 46 proviso); RD grants ₹22,113 crore (2015-20)
  [112:5:0.3] 16th FC | Ended RD grants for all States; AP's divisible-pool share rose to 4.22%
  [112:5:0.4] Amaravati | ₹15,000 crore special assistance fast-tracked (January 2026)
[112:5:1] - **Central institutions**: **IIT Tirupati, IIM Visakhapatnam, NIT Tadepalligudem, AIIMS Mangalagiri** (11 in all).
[112:5:2] - AP says actual deficits keep exceeding the 14th FC's static projections.
[112:5:3] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.46); old app notes 3:112 sub 3
## SUB 6 | FRBM Breaches, Debt and CAG Remarks
[112:6:0] - **Accounts at a Glance 2024-25** (Principal AG; tabled 6 Mar 2026): **RD 3.75% vs FRBM target 2.70%**; **FD 5.05% vs 4% ceiling**; borrowings ₹81,071 crore.
[112:6:1] - **Debt (2024-25 RE): ₹5,64,488 crore = 35.15% of GSDP**; 2025-26: ₹6,35,177 crore (36.04%); market loans 75% of debt.
[112:6:2] - **Off-budget borrowings** (CAG): ₹1,18,394 crore (Mar 2022) → **₹1.28 lakh crore = 9.7% of GSDP (Mar 2023)**.
[112:6:3] - **Guarantees** (2023-24): ₹1,54,797 crore (11% of GSDP).
[112:6:4] - 2024-25: 3.75% / 5.05% (Accounts at a Glance) vs 3.79% / 5.11% (Finance Accounts).
[112:6:5] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:112 sub 5
## SUB 7 | Welfare and Committed Spending
[112:7:0] - 2024-25 RE: **revenue spending ₹2,24,343 crore** vs **capital outlay only ₹24,072 crore**.
[112:7:1] - AP blames shrinking revenue base, interest, welfare and the loss of a capital city.
[112:7:2] - **Own tax revenue**: **₹85,922 crore (2023-24 actuals, 6% of GSDP)** → ₹1,09,007 crore (2025-26 BE); **State GST = largest source (37%, 2025-26 BE)**.
[112:7:3] TABLE head: Own tax head | 2023-24 actuals (₹ cr) | 2025-26 BE (₹ cr)
  [112:7:3.0] State GST | 31,130 (largest) | 40,718
  [112:7:3.1] Sales tax / VAT | 18,475 | 20,874
  [112:7:3.2] State excise | 15,998 | 27,097
  [112:7:3.3] Stamps and registration | 9,542 | 13,150
  [112:7:3.4] Electricity taxes and duties | 5,528 | 510
  [112:7:3.5] Taxes on vehicles | 4,557 | 5,664

  x: The resource gap was defined as the revenue deficit, i.e. revenue expenditure minus revenue receipts, for the first ten months (2 June 2014 to 31 March 2015). It is not the full-year fiscal deficit, a capital-outlay shortfall or a net borrowing figure.

3. The FRBM revenue-deficit target was 4% of GSDP.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: Accounts at a Glance gives 3.75% and the Finance Accounts 3.79%; the FRBM RD target was 2.70%, while 4% is the fiscal-deficit ceiling.
[fbbd77be85fd3] sub6
  How much were the guarantees given by the Andhra Pradesh government in 2023-24?
  ₹1,18,394 crore (9% of GSDP) | ₹1,28,000 crore (9.7% of GSDP) | ₹5,64,488 crore (35.15% of GSDP) | *₹1,54,797 crore (11% of GSDP)
  x: Guarantees in 2023-24 were ₹1,54,797 crore, about 11% of GSDP.
[fb57fbf342464] sub6
  Market loans make up about what share of Andhra Pradesh's debt?
  35% | *75% | 50% | 60%
  x: Market loans are about 75% of AP's debt; 35.15% is the debt-GSDP ratio.

  Against which FRBM revenue-deficit target was Andhra Pradesh's 2024-25 revenue deficit of 3.75% of GSDP compared in Accounts at a Glance?
  1.11% | *2.70% | 3.00% | 4.00%
  x: The FRBM revenue-deficit target was 2.70%; 4% is the fiscal-deficit ceiling.

  ₹41,118 crore | ₹22,002 crore | *₹24,072 crore | ₹81,071 crore
  x: Capital outlay was only ₹24,072 crore against revenue spending of ₹2,24,343 crore.

  x: State GST is the largest, about 37% of own tax revenue in 2025-26 BE.

  x: Own tax revenue was ₹85,922 crore (6% of GSDP) in 2023-24, rising to ₹1,09,007 crore in 2025-26 BE.

  x: Stamps and registration gave ₹9,542 crore, less than State excise (₹15,998 crore).

=== NOTES 3:113 | Krishna and Godavari Water Disputes – Tribunals, Apex Council, Krmb and Grmb
## SUB 0 | River Management Boards – KRMB and GRMB (Sections 85-88)
[113:0:0] # Constitution and status
[113:0:1] TABLE head: Section | Provision
  [113:0:1.0] 85(1) | Constitution and functions: two Boards within 60 days of the appointed day
  [113:0:1.1] 85(2) | HQ: KRMB in Andhra Pradesh; GRMB in Telangana
  [113:0:1.2] 85(3) | Autonomous bodies under administrative control of the Centre
  [113:0:1.3] 85(4) | Chairperson (Centre, Secretary/Additional Secretary rank) + 2 members per State (technical + administrative) + 1 central expert
  [113:0:1.4] 85(5) | Full-time Member Secretary (Chief Engineer, CWC)
  [113:0:1.5] 85(7) | CISF assists in day-to-day management of reservoirs
  [113:0:1.6] 86 | Staff on deputation equally from both States; States fund the Boards
  [113:0:1.7] 87 | Jurisdiction over projects notified by the Centre
  [113:0:1.8] 88 | Power of the Board to make regulations
[113:0:2] - **Members apart from the chairman**: 4 State members + 1 expert = **5 under Sec. 85(4)**; counting the full-time Member Secretary = **6** (most keys).
[113:0:3] - AP's petition asking the Centre to **notify KRMB's jurisdiction** was under **Section 87**.
[113:0:4] - **Jal Shakti** notified both Boards' jurisdiction on **15 July 2021**.
[113:0:5] # Functions (Sec. 85(8) and Eleventh Schedule)
[113:0:6] - **Regulate supply** of water and power from notified projects per **tribunal awards**; **administer and regulate** notified projects.
[113:0:7] - **Release water** per States' shares; **advise both States on disaster, drought and flood management** (Schedule XI para 6).
[113:0:8] - **Not** a Board function: **allocating or redetermining** shares (tribunals do that).
[113:0:9] - **Schedule XI para 9**: a State that does not implement a Board decision **bears financial and other penalties imposed by the Central Government**.
[113:0:10] - **Schedule XI para 7**: no new project on Godavari/Krishna without **Apex Council sanction**, after Board appraisal.
[113:0:11] # Committees and officers
[113:0:12] - **A.K. Bajaj Committee** (former CWC chairman; reconstituted 2017): helps KRMB prepare a **working manual and operational protocol** for sharing Krishna waters between AP and Telangana.
[113:0:13] - KRMB chairman in the PYQ key: **S.K. Haldar** (not verified – see blueprint).
[113:0:14] # Latest
[113:0:15] - KRMB to install **telemetry** meters (about **47 points**) to monitor water use by both States.
[113:0:16] - KRMB three-member committee allowed **only drinking water** from **Srisailam and Nagarjuna Sagar** (El Niño, poor inflows; 25 Aug 2026).
[113:0:17] - KRMB advised Telangana after Srisailam storage fell due to its power generation (5 Sep 2026).
[113:0:18] > Exam angle: KRMB HQ **AP**, GRMB HQ **Telangana**; CISF guards; Sec. 85 = constitution, 86 = staff, 87 = jurisdiction, 88 = regulations; allocation is **not** a Board function.
[113:0:19] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt ss.85-88, Eleventh Schedule); GKToday (A.K. Bajaj committee reconstituted); old app notes 3:113 sub 5
## SUB 1 | Apex Council (Section 84)
[113:1:0] TABLE head: Member | Role
  [113:1:0.0] Union Minister of Water Resources (now Jal Shakti) | Chairperson
  [113:1:0.1] Chief Minister of Andhra Pradesh | Member
  [113:1:0.2] Chief Minister of Telangana | Member
[113:1:1] - **3 members** in all, including the chairperson.
[113:1:2] - **Sec. 84(1)**: constituted by the Centre from the appointed day to **supervise the GRMB and KRMB**.
[113:1:3] - **Functions – Sec. 84(3)**:
[113:1:4] - 1. **Supervise** GRMB and KRMB.
[113:1:5] - 2. **Plan and approve new projects** on Krishna/Godavari after appraisal by the **Boards and CWC** – new projects need **Apex Council sanction**.
[113:1:6] - 3. **Settle disputes by negotiation** and mutual agreement.
[113:1:7] - 4. **Refer disputes** not covered by KWDT to a **tribunal** under the ISRWD Act, 1956.
[113:1:8] - **Management and development of water resources** of the two States = **Apex Council + River Management Boards** (Part IX), not the Governors.
[113:1:9] - **Not** its function: allocating shares, dividing assets, employees or capital works.
[113:1:10] - Apex Council does **not** include the PM, Home Minister or Governors.
[113:1:11] # Other APRA sections often matched
[113:1:12] TABLE head: Section | Subject
  [113:1:12.0] 20 | Duration of Legislative Assemblies
  [113:1:12.1] 46 | Distribution of revenue (13th FC award; separate 14th FC awards)
  [113:1:12.2] 84 | Apex Council for Godavari and Krishna river water resources
  [113:1:12.3] 89 | KWDT term extended
  [113:1:12.4] 90 | Polavaram national project
  [113:1:12.5] 91 | Tungabhadra Board
[113:1:13] > Exam angle: chair = **Union Water Resources (Jal Shakti) Minister**; members = **two CMs**; total **3**.
[113:1:14] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt ss.20, 46, 84, 89-91, Part IX); old app notes 3:113 sub 4
## SUB 2 | Krishna Waters – Tribunal Awards and the AP–Telangana Split
[113:2:0] TABLE head: Tribunal | Chair | Award | Key point
  [113:2:0.0] KWDT-I | Justice R.S. Bachawat | 1973, final 1976 | 75% dependability; Maharashtra 560, Karnataka 700, united AP 800 TMC
  [113:2:0.1] KWDT-II | Justice Brijesh Kumar | 2010, further report 2013 | Shared surplus and 65%-dependable flows; not notified, challenged in SC
  [113:2:0.2] GWDT | Justice R.S. Bachawat | 1980 | Based on inter-State agreements
[113:2:1] - **AP's 800 TMC + 11 TMC return flows = 811 TMC**.
[113:2:2] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [113:2:2.0] Ad hoc split (2014-15) | AP 512 TMC : Telangana 299 TMC = 66 : 34
  [113:2:2.1] Basis | Undivided AP's 811 TMC
  [113:2:2.2] Telangana's demand | 50 : 50, citing its larger basin area (not population)
  [113:2:2.3] AP's stand | Add Telangana as a separate party; redo allocations among four States
[113:2:3] - The **58:42 population ratio** was not used for water.
[113:2:4] - **Srisailam row (June 2021)**: AP protested that **Telangana was generating power below 834 ft** (the minimum drawdown level), draining water needed for AP's lift and drinking-water schemes.
[113:2:5] - AP's position: **Godavari surplus not yet quantified** under the GWDT award (31 Jan 2026).
[113:2:6] > Exam angle: KWDT-I gave united AP **800 TMC**; AP's working share = **512 TMC** (Telangana 299), **66:34**.
[113:2:7] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:113 sub 1-2; Wikipedia, Krishna River Management Board (512:299 since 2015)
## SUB 3 | Constitutional and Legal Base
[113:3:0] - **Article 262**: Parliament may make law for adjudicating **inter-State river water disputes** (use, distribution or control of waters).
[113:3:1] - **Article 262(2)**: Parliament may provide that **neither the Supreme Court nor any other court** shall exercise jurisdiction over such disputes.
[113:3:2] - **Inter-State River Water Disputes (ISRWD) Act, 1956** – enacted under Article 262.
[113:3:3]   - **Section 11**: bars the Supreme Court and other courts from disputes that may be referred to a tribunal.
[113:3:4]   - The Supreme Court held (Cauvery case, 1991) that this bar does **not** cover its appeal jurisdiction under **Article 136**.
[113:3:5]   - **Section 5(1)**: used in **October 2023** to give **KWDT-II further terms of reference**.
[113:3:6] - Articles 265 (no tax without law) and 268 (duties levied by the Union, collected by States) are tax articles – not water.
[113:3:7] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:113 sub 0; distractor articles general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 4 | Tungabhadra Board
[113:4:0] - **Tungabhadra Board**: set up under **Section 66 of the Andhra State Act, 1953**.
[113:4:1] - **Sec. 91(1), APRA**: AP and Telangana **replace undivided AP** on the Board.
[113:4:2] - **Sec. 91(2)**: the Board **continues to monitor the release of water** to the **High Level Canal, Low Level Canal and Rajolibanda Diversion Scheme (RDS)** – not CWC, CGWB or NWDA.
[113:4:3] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.91); old app notes 3:113 sub 6
## SUB 5 | KWDT-II and Section 89
[113:5:0] - **Sec. 89, APRA**: term of KWDT extended for:
[113:5:1]   - (a) **project-wise allocation** where no tribunal had made one;
[113:5:2]   - (b) **operational protocol** for releases in **deficit flows**.
[113:5:3] - Section 89 did **not** automatically divide undivided AP's share between AP and Telangana.
[113:5:4] - **October 2023**: Centre gave KWDT-II further terms under **Sec. 5(1), ISRWD Act** → divide AP's share between AP and Telangana.
[113:5:5] - **Sec. 90** = Polavaram national project (row 114).
[113:5:6] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt ss.89-90); old app notes 3:113 sub 3
## SUB 6 | Contested Projects
[113:6:0] TABLE head: Project | River | Built by | Objection
  [113:6:0.0] Pothireddypadu Head Regulator / Rayalaseema Lift | Krishna (Srisailam) | AP | TG: Srisailam is mainly a power project
  [113:6:0.1] Palamuru-Rangareddy, Dindi | Krishna | Telangana | AP: no CWC/KRMB clearance; Karnataka also complained
  [113:6:0.2] Kaleshwaram | Godavari | Telangana | TG wants national-project status
  [113:6:0.3] Polavaram-Banakacherla, now Polavaram-Nallamala Sagar Link | Godavari → Krishna → Penna | AP | TG: AP would exceed its Krishna share
[113:6:1] - AP plans to divert **200 TMC** of Polavaram **flood water** (~80 lakh people, ~7.5 lakh acres) via reservoirs at Bollapalli and Banakacherla.
[113:6:2] - **Telangana withdrew its SC petition** against the link (**Jan 2026**); a Jal Shakti committee under the **CWC Chairman** first met **30 Jan 2026**.
[113:6:3] - 31st Southern Zonal Council (Mahabalipuram, Aug 2026; chaired by Union Home Minister): Telangana sought national status for Kaleshwaram and Palamuru-Rangareddy.
[113:6:4] - Criticism: Boards lack full operational control; disputes over unapproved projects and hydel drawal continue.
[113:6:5] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:113 sub 7
## SUB 7 | Comparative Note – Cauvery
[113:7:0] - **Cauvery Water Disputes Tribunal** (set up 2 June 1990): **Justice Abhay Manohar Sapre** appointed chairman in **February 2017** (post vacant since Justice B.S. Chauhan left for the Law Commission in March 2016).
[113:7:1] TABLE head: State | SC 2018 verdict (TMC)
  [113:7:1.0] Tamil Nadu | 404.25
  [113:7:1.1] Karnataka | 284.75
  [113:7:1.2] Kerala | 30
  [113:7:1.3] Puducherry | 7
[113:7:2] ~~ Sources: LiveLaw / Deccan Herald (Feb 2017: Justice Sapre to head Cauvery tribunal); old app notes 3:113 sub 8

=== OWN MCQs of 3:113 (47)
[nb629930e47a] sub0
  Under Schedule XI of the AP Reorganisation Act, which of the following is a function of the KRMB and GRMB?
  Approving new projects on the Krishna and Godavari | *Regulating supply from notified headworks according to tribunal awards | Redetermining the shares of AP and Telangana every water year | Referring water disputes to a tribunal
  x: The Boards regulate supply from notified headworks as per tribunal awards and release water by State shares; approving projects and referring disputes belong to the Apex Council, and redetermining shares is not their function.
[n610856d327b] sub0
  Consider the following statements about the Krishna and Godavari River Management Boards:
1. They are autonomous bodies under the administrative control of the Centre.
2. Each Board has a full-time Member Secretary and one central expert.
3. Each Board has three members from each State.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?
  *1 and 2 only | 2 and 3 only | 1 and 3 only | 1, 2 and 3
  x: Under Sections 85(3) and 85(4) the Boards are autonomous bodies under Central control, with a Central Chairperson, two members per State, a central expert and a full-time Member Secretary; so statement 3 is wrong.
[n5ac8ab09075] sub0
  On which date did the Ministry of Jal Shakti notify the jurisdiction of the KRMB and GRMB?
  2 June 2014 | 15 July 2020 | *15 July 2021 | 6 October 2020
  x: Both Boards' jurisdiction was notified on 15 July 2021; under Section 87 the Boards have jurisdiction over projects notified by the Centre.
[nab748903547] sub0
  A State fails to comply with the directions applicable to a River Management Board. Under Section 92 read with Schedule XI, which consequence may follow?
  *Financial and other penalties imposed by the Centre | Automatic dissolution of the State Legislative Assembly | Cancellation of the relevant tribunal award by the Board | Transfer of all State water projects to the Supreme Court
  x: A defaulting State may face financial and other penalties from the Centre under Section 92 and Schedule XI.
[n08b81d5e554] sub0

  KRMB in Telangana and GRMB in Andhra Pradesh | Both KRMB and GRMB in New Delhi | *KRMB in Andhra Pradesh and GRMB in Telangana | Both KRMB and GRMB in Andhra Pradesh
  x: Sec. 85(2) places the KRMB headquarters in Andhra Pradesh and the GRMB headquarters in Telangana. The reversed pairing is the common trap; neither New Delhi nor a single State hosts both.

  x: Sec. 85(7) provides for CISF to guard the reservoirs.

[n3cf766f6eb0] sub1
  Which of the following is a function of the Apex Council under Section 84(3) of the AP Reorganisation Act, 2014?

  Article 32 | Article 262(2) | Article 131 | *Article 136
  x: The Supreme Court held that the bar does not cover its appeal jurisdiction under Article 136.
[g09cf29a9ec3c] sub3
  Which provision of the Constitution allows Parliament to bar the Supreme Court and other courts from inter-State river water disputes?
  Article 262(1) | *Article 262(2) | Article 263 | Article 131
  x: Article 262(2) lets Parliament provide that no court, including the Supreme Court, shall hear such disputes.
[nfb21198e20d] sub4
  The Tungabhadra Board was set up under Section 66 of which Act?
  *Andhra State Act, 1953 | Inter-State River Water Disputes Act, 1956 | Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014 | States Reorganisation Act, 1956
  x: The Board was set up under Section 66 of the Andhra State Act, 1953; under Section 91(1) of APRA, AP and Telangana replaced undivided AP on it.
[n9a91729cd98] sub4
  Polavaram was declared a national project under which section of the Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014?
  Section 85 | Section 89 | Section 94 | *Section 90
  x: Section 90 of APRA declared Polavaram a national project; it is planned for 960 MW.
[f9ced4509807d] sub4

  Pothireddypadu Head Regulator and Rayalaseema Lift | Palamuru-Rangareddy and Dindi | *High Level Canal, Low Level Canal and Rajolibanda Diversion Scheme | Srisailam and Nagarjuna Sagar
  x: Sec. 91(2) covers the High Level Canal, Low Level Canal and Rajolibanda Diversion Scheme.
[ffcea84fe602b] sub4
  Which section of APRA, 2014 makes AP and Telangana replace undivided AP on the Tungabhadra Board?
  *Section 91 | Section 89 | Section 87 | Section 90
  x: Section 91(1) puts AP and Telangana in place of undivided AP on the Board.
[n789e269ccb5] sub5
  Which of the following is NOT included among the purposes for which KWDT’s term was extended under Section 89?
  Addressing project-wise and operational issues within the scope of the reference | *Automatically dividing the undivided Andhra Pradesh share between Andhra Pradesh and Telangana | Fixing an operational protocol for releases during deficit flows | Making project-wise allocations where no tribunal had done so
  x: Section 89 covered project-wise allocation where necessary and an operational protocol for deficit flows. It did not itself automatically divide the Andhra Pradesh share between Andhra Pradesh and Telangana.
[f1065bf2511aa] sub5
  Section 89 of the AP Reorganisation Act extended the term of KWDT to frame which of the following?
  An automatic division of undivided AP's share | *An operational protocol for releases in deficit flows | The jurisdiction of the Boards | The Godavari surplus quantification
  x: Sec. 89 extended KWDT for project-wise allocation and an operational protocol for releases in deficit flows.

  x: Andhra Pradesh plans to divert 200 TMC of Polavaram flood water through the proposed link.

  x: The link envisages diversion from the Godavari through the Krishna basin to the Penna basin.

=== NOTES 3:114 | Polavaram Project – Status, Funding, National Project Status, Submergence and R&r
## SUB 0 | National Project Status – APRA Section 90
[114:0:0] TABLE head: Sub-section | What it says
  [114:0:0.0] 90(1) | Polavaram Irrigation Project declared a national project
  [114:0:0.1] 90(2) | Expedient in the public interest that the Union takes control of its regulation and development for the purposes of irrigation
  [114:0:0.2] 90(3) | Consent of Telangana deemed to have been given
  [114:0:0.3] 90(4) | Central Government executes the project and obtains environmental, forest and R&R clearances
[114:0:1] - **Polavaram is the only project declared national by the Reorganisation Act itself** – not Gundlakamma, Pulichintala or Jeedipalli.
[114:0:2] - So it is a **National Project**, executed by the Centre **in consultation with AP and Telangana** – not a mere State project.
[114:0:3] - **NITI Aayog (2016):** suggested **the State Government (AP) executes** Polavaram while the **Centre funds** the irrigation component; since then **GoAP executes it on behalf of GoI** and claims reimbursement.
[114:0:4] > Exam angle: **Section 90** (not 30, 89 or 91); "for the purposes of **irrigation**" only; by the Act the **Centre** executes, in practice **AP** does (NITI Aayog).
[114:0:5] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt s.90); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.127 (national project status under APRA); old app notes 3:114 sub 3
## SUB 1 | Project Features
[114:1:0] TABLE head: Feature | Fact
  [114:1:0.0] River / site | Godavari, 42 km upstream of the Cotton Barrage, near Ramayyapet village, Polavaram mandal – earlier West Godavari district, now Eluru district
  [114:1:0.1] Name | Indira Sagar (Polavaram) – multipurpose terminal reservoir
  [114:1:0.2] Dam | 2,467.50 m earth-cum-rock fill dam; spillway 1,121.20 m
  [114:1:0.3] FRL | +150 ft (45.72 m)
  [114:1:0.4] Reservoir | Gross 194.6 TMC; utilisation 322.73 TMC
  [114:1:0.5] Irrigation | 7.20 lakh acres – East & West Godavari, Visakhapatnam, Krishna
  [114:1:0.6] Hydel | 960 MW (12 units × 80 MW)
  [114:1:0.7] Drinking/industry | 23.44 TMC for Visakhapatnam; 28.5 lakh people in 540 villages
  [114:1:0.8] Diversion | 80 TMC of Godavari water to the Krishna – a river-linking project
[114:1:1] # Canals
[114:1:2] TABLE head: Canal | Destination
  [114:1:2.0] Right Main Canal (RMC) | Krishna delta – carries 80 TMC of Godavari water to the Krishna above Vijayawada (Pattiseema lift as interim)
  [114:1:2.1] Left Main Canal (LMC) | East Godavari and Visakhapatnam (Yeleru, Vizag water supply); LMC to Visakhapatnam targeted Mar 2027
[114:1:3] - **Polavaram-Nallamala Sagar link** (earlier the **Banakacherla link**): Telangana says it violates **APRA 2014** and the **1980 GWDT award**.
[114:1:4] - Split of the 80 TMC (GWDT, 1980): **45 TMC to AP** + **35 TMC** to upper States – **Karnataka 21, Maharashtra 14**.
[114:1:5] > Exam angle: **80 TMC** to Krishna; FRL **150 ft**; **960 MW**, **7.20 lakh acres**; site in **West Godavari** (now Eluru).
[114:1:6] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.127 (site, storage, 7.20 lakh acres, 960 MW, 80 TMC, 23.44 TMC); old app notes 3:114 sub 0, 2
## SUB 2 | Polavaram Ordinance and the Seven Mandals
[114:2:0] - **Telangana came into existence on 2 June 2014** (appointed day).
[114:2:1] - **AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Ordinance, 2014** = "**Polavaram Ordinance**" – moved the Khammam **submergence villages and mandals to AP (Seemandhra)** to ease **resettlement of those displaced** by Polavaram.
[114:2:2] - Base rule in the Act: Khammam → Telangana, excluding villages in **G.O.Ms. No. 111 (27 June 2005)**.
[114:2:3] - The Bill replacing it was passed by the **Lok Sabha on 11 July 2014** (by voice vote) – not June 2016.
[114:2:4] - **Seven mandals** moved from Khammam to AP:
[114:2:5] - 1. Kukunoor
[114:2:6] - 2. Velerupadu
[114:2:7] - 3. VR Puram (Vararamachandrapuram)
[114:2:8] - 4. Chintoor
[114:2:9] - 5. Kunavaram
[114:2:10] - 6. **Bhadrachalam (except the temple town)**
[114:2:11] - 7. Burgampadu
[114:2:12] - Telangana Assembly constituencies left with part of their territory in AP: **Pinapaka, Aswaraopeta and Bhadrachalam**.
[114:2:13] - **Bhadrachalam town** (with the Sri Rama temple) stayed in **Telangana**; it lies on the bank of the Godavari whose surrounding mandals went to **Andhra** (the "Andhra side").
[114:2:14] - Effect: AP's area **1,60,205 → 1,62,970 sq km**.
[114:2:15] > Exam angle: **7 mandals**; temple town of Bhadrachalam stayed in Telangana; Lok Sabha **11 July 2014**.
[114:2:16] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.3, Schedules of constituencies: Pinapaka, Aswaraopeta); old app notes 3:114 sub 1, 4; Bhadrachalam side general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 3 | Submergence, Inter-State Cases and R&R
[114:3:0] TABLE head: State | Concern
  [114:3:0.0] Odisha | Submergence; protective embankments; original suit in SC pending
  [114:3:0.1] Chhattisgarh | Submergence; protective embankments; SC case pending
  [114:3:0.2] Telangana | Backwater at Bhadrachalam; asked the SC to halt work and first review the project's EIA
[114:3:1] - **Maharashtra and Karnataka have not filed cases** against Polavaram (they share the 80 TMC diversion benefit).
[114:3:2] - CAG (audit to Mar 2023): settle submergence issues with neighbours with PPA; build protective embankments (due by 2014-15).
[114:3:3] - Jul 2026: ~189 villages in the submergence zone on alert during Godavari-Sabari floods.
[114:3:4] # R&R
[114:3:5] - Displaced: mainly **Koya and Kondh tribals**.
[114:3:6] - R&R follows the **RFCTLARR Act, 2013**.
[114:3:7] - **Aug 2026: 94% land acquisition, 52% relocation**; R&R colonies targeted Dec 2026.
[114:3:8] - **₹1,943 crore** paid in three phases (Mar 2026); house-site value **₹1 lakh → ₹2 lakh** (Cabinet, 1 May 2026).
[114:3:9] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:114 sub 7; Maharashtra/Karnataka not litigants general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 4 | Funding and Cost
[114:4:0] - **Cabinet decision (Special Assistance Measure, Sep 2016 / Mar 2017)**: the Centre funds **100% of the remaining cost of the irrigation component only**, **for the period starting from 1.4.2014, to the extent of the cost of the irrigation component on that date** – not drinking water or hydel, and not "as incurred".
[114:4:1] - Irrigation component at 2013-14 prices ₹20,398.61 crore; balance as on 1.4.2014 ₹15,667.90 crore (Finance Ministry note).
[114:4:2] TABLE head: Item | Figure
  [114:4:2.0] Early estimate (PYQ key) | About ₹6,400 crore (older Indira Sagar estimate; not verified)
  [114:4:2.1] 1st RCE (2011, 2010-11 prices) | ₹16,010.45 crore
  [114:4:2.2] 2nd RCE (RCC, Mar 2020, 2017-18 prices) | ₹47,725.74 crore; older RCE ₹55,548 crore
  [114:4:2.3] Spending (Dec 2025) | ₹25,700.47 crore; GoI advance ₹5,052.71 crore
  [114:4:2.4] Union Budget 2026-27 | ₹3,320 crore (storage up to 41.15 m)
  [114:4:2.5] Pending from Centre | ~₹12,000 crore (CM, 26 Jan 2026)
  [114:4:2.6] AP seeks (11 Feb 2026) | ₹32,000 crore for Phase-II + permanent lifting of MoEF stop-work order
[114:4:3] - Unresolved: clearance of the **revised cost estimate**; friction over RCE checks, land acquisition, R&R funding.
[114:4:4] > Exam angle: **100% of irrigation component, from 1.4.2014, capped at the cost on that date**.
[114:4:5] ~~ Sources: PMIndia / PIB release "Cabinet approves special assistance measure for Andhra Pradesh" (Mar 2017); CAG audit report 2025 Chapter I (1st RCE, 2nd RCE); old app notes 3:114 sub 5
## SUB 5 | Construction Milestones and Status
[114:5:0] - **8 June 2017**: CM N. Chandrababu Naidu laid the foundation stone for the **cofferdam** (and the spill-channel bridge); diaphragm wall foundation was laid in Feb 2017.
[114:5:1] - First contemplated **1941** – proposed as **"Ramapada Sagaram"** (Sir Sonti Venkataramamurthy); **GWDT set up 1969**, its **1980 award** cleared Polavaram; works **commenced 2005** under **Jalayagnam**.
[114:5:2] TABLE head: Date | Status
  [114:5:2.0] Dec 2024 | Overall 53.47%; head works 74.92%, RMC 93%, LMC 73%
  [114:5:2.1] Dec 2025 | Overall 60.8%; head works 81.61%, RMC 92.79%, LMC 73.40%
  [114:5:2.2] Aug 2026 | Civil works 90%, overall 77%
[114:5:3] - Done: spillway, radial gates, coffer dams, Gap-III concrete dam, buttress dam.
[114:5:4] - **Diaphragm wall (1,372 m)** rebuilt from 18 Jan 2025 in **440 days** (announced 2 Apr 2026), ~₹1,000 crore extra.
[114:5:5] - **Foreign expert panel** (6th visit, with CWC) reviewed Gap-1, Gap-2, D-wall (Jan 2026); **CWC cleared spillway safety** (Apr 2026).
[114:5:6] - Target moved **Dec 2027 → March 2027**, before the 2027 **Godavari Pushkaralu**.
[114:5:7] ~~ Sources: The Hans India 8 Jun 2017 (cofferdam foundation); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.127 (Dec 2024 progress); old app notes 3:114 sub 1, 8
## SUB 6 | Polavaram Project Authority (PPA)
[114:6:0] - Constituted by the **Union** after the **AP Reorganisation Act, 2014** to oversee the national project.
[114:6:1] - Role: routes national-project **funding**, assesses the irrigation-component cost, mediates **inter-State coordination**.
[114:6:2] TABLE head: Person | Post
  [114:6:2.0] Shiv Nandan Kumar | CEO as of 25 May 2024; CEO office in New Delhi
  [114:6:2.1] M. Raghuram | Member-Secretary
  [114:6:2.2] M. Ramesh Kumar | Chief Engineer, Rajamahendravaram
[114:6:3] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:114 sub 6 (not checked further)

[n89df7e8fe6a] sub0

  x: Section 90(4) says the Central Government shall execute the project and obtain environmental, forest and R&R clearances, not the AP Government.

  x: Section 90 declares Polavaram a national project. Section 30 is an old wrong key, Section 89 concerns the KWDT reference and Section 94 covers fiscal and industrial incentives.

  90(1) | 90(2) | *90(3) | 90(4)
  x: Section 90(3) deems Telangana's consent given; 90(2) has the Union take control and 90(4) has the Centre execute the project.

  x: On 11 Feb 2026 AP sought Rs 32,000 crore for Polavaram Phase-II along with permanent lifting of the MoEF stop-work order; Rs 55,548 crore is the older RCE and Rs 3,320 crore the 2026-27 Budget allocation.

=== NOTES 3:115 | Irrigation in Ap – Major and Medium Projects, Handri-neeva, Pattiseema, Galeru-nagari, Vamsadhara
## SUB 0 | Major and Medium Projects of AP
[115:0:0] TABLE head: Project | Type / river | Key facts
  [115:0:0.0] Nagarjuna Sagar | Multipurpose, Krishna | Foundation 10 Dec 1955 by Nehru (First Plan); dam completed 1967; one of the largest masonry dams; AP and Telangana
  [115:0:0.1] Srisailam | Hydel, Krishna | Upstream of Nagarjuna Sagar; Third Plan (1963)
  [115:0:0.2] Prakasam Barrage | Barrage, Krishna, Vijayawada | Construction began Feb 1954; replaced Cotton's 1850s anicut; feeds Krishna delta
  [115:0:0.3] Telugu Ganga | Krishna surplus | Srisailam-Somasila-Kandaleru-Poondi; Chennai drinking water; NTR-era (Sixth Plan)
  [115:0:0.4] Somasila | Pennar | Part of the Telugu Ganga chain, SPSR Nellore
  [115:0:0.5] Handri-Neeva (HNSS) | Lift, Krishna at Srisailam | 40 TMC; 6.025 lakh acres; Rayalaseema
  [115:0:0.6] Galeru-Nagari (GNSS) | Krishna flood waters | 38 TMC; 2.60 lakh acres; Kadapa, Annamayya, Tirupati, Chittoor, Nellore
  [115:0:0.7] Veligonda (Poola Subbaiah) | Gravity from Srisailam via Nallamala tunnels | 43.50 TMC; 4.47 lakh acres; Prakasam, Nellore, Kadapa
  [115:0:0.8] Gundlakamma (Kandula Obula Reddy) | Gundlakamma | Irrigates Prakasam district
  [115:0:0.9] Vamsadhara | Vamsadhara, Srikakulam | "Lifeline of North Coastal AP"
  [115:0:0.10] Thotapalli barrage | Nagavali | Priority project (2017)
  [115:0:0.11] Gandipalem | Pillaperu (Pillayeru) | Medium project, SPSR Nellore
  [115:0:0.12] Bhima lift | Krishna (Bhima is its tributary; Jurala backwater) | Mahabubnagar – now Telangana
  [115:0:0.13] Polavaram | Multipurpose, Godavari | National Project (row 114)
[115:0:1] - **Priority projects (2017):** **Veligonda**, **Thotapalli** and **Pattiseema** – all three were priority projects.
[115:0:2] - **31 Aug 2026**: Veligonda Phase-I inaugurated at Gantavanipalli (Markapuram), three decades after its foundation.
[115:0:3] - Jalayagnam projects still unfinished in 2025-26: Veligonda, HNSS, GNSS.
[115:0:4] - AP angle: Rayalaseema depends on Krishna-lift projects (HNSS, GNSS, Telugu Ganga) that "often run dry".
[115:0:5] - **Sunkesula barrage:** across the **Tungabhadra** near Kurnool, head of the **KC canal**.
[115:0:6] > Exam angle: **two Krishna dams = Srisailam and Nagarjuna Sagar**; Somasila on **Pennar**; Gandipalem on **Pillaperu**; Vamsadhara = lifeline of North Coastal AP.
[115:0:7] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.126 (Nagarjuna Sagar, Somasila, Vamsadhara among contributors); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 1 p.8-9 (plan-period projects); old app notes 3:115 sub 2, 5
## SUB 1 | Irrigation Data and Sources
[115:1:0] # Irrigated area
[115:1:1] TABLE head: Indicator | 2022-23 | 2023-24 (drought) | 2024-25
  [115:1:1.0] Gross irrigated area | 36.56 lakh ha | 32.71 lakh ha | 36.77 lakh ha
  [115:1:1.1] Net irrigated area | 28.56 lakh ha | 26.03 lakh ha | 30.33 lakh ha
[115:1:2] - **Irrigation potential created (31 Dec 2024)**: **106.15 lakh acres** – major 66.81, medium 5.62, minor 25.60, APSIDC lifts 8.12.
[115:1:3] - Up to **Nov 2016**: **103.5 lakh acres** created; **Krishna, Guntur and SPSR Nellore** together about **36%** (PYQ key, not checked).
[115:1:4] - **Largest irrigated area comes under the Krishna** (Krishna delta, Nagarjuna Sagar right canal, Telugu Ganga, KC canal, HLC/LLC), then the Godavari delta – not the Penna.
[115:1:5] - **Irrigation White Paper (Jul 2026)**: complete **36 projects by Dec 2028** at Rs 35,555 crore.
[115:1:6] - **GIA/GCA in AP = 52%** (AP Economy in Brief 2020); India **55.8%**.
[115:1:7] - Top district by irrigated area (2023-24): **Eluru** (2.39 lakh ha) > Ananthapuramu > Nandyal.
[115:1:8] - **Least gross irrigated area (2015-16, PYQ key):** **Prakasam**.
[115:1:9] # Source-wise share (GIA 2023-24)
[115:1:10] TABLE head: Source | lakh ha | Share
  [115:1:10.0] Wells / tube wells (together) | 15.68 | ~48% (largest)
  [115:1:10.1] Canals | 13.24 | ~40% (largest single surface source)
  [115:1:10.2] Tanks | 2.69 | ~8%
  [115:1:10.3] Others | 1.10 | ~3%
[115:1:11] - Main source in **2016-17**: **well irrigation** (bore and open wells), ahead of canals and tanks.
[115:1:12] - Older books: "**canals** are the main source in AP" (deltas); now groundwater (wells + tube wells together) leads.
[115:1:13] - About **19 lakh bore wells**; India NIA 2021-22: **tube wells 47.3%**, canals 24.7%.
[115:1:14] # Minor irrigation
[115:1:15] - **Minor irrigation**: ayacut **up to 5,000 acres** (AP); sources include **surface lift schemes, deep tube wells and dug wells** and tanks.
[115:1:16] - Undivided AP: about **78,000 tanks** serving about **46.5 lakh acres**; residual AP now **38,862 tanks** with **25.60 lakh acres** registered ayacut.
[115:1:17] - Tanks: **Coastal Andhra over 14,000** minor tanks; **Rayalaseema over 6,800** active tanks. Tanks suit the plateau's undulating relief; canals dominate the deltas.
[115:1:18] > Exam angle: largest source now **groundwater**; largest river command **Krishna**; potential **106.15 lakh acres** (Dec 2024).
[115:1:19] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.126 (106.15 lakh acres, sector-wise), p.129-130 (38,862 tanks, 25.60 lakh acres), Annexure 7.1 p.410; old app notes 3:115 sub 0; Krishna-largest general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 2 | Pattiseema Lift and Godavari-Krishna Linking
[115:2:0] - **Pattiseema** = a **lift irrigation scheme** on the Godavari at Pattiseema (West Godavari, now Eluru); **24 pumps**; first water Sept **2015**.
[115:2:1] - Objective: **link the Godavari and Krishna rivers** – Godavari → **Polavaram Right Main Canal** → Krishna at **Prakasam Barrage** = **first Godavari-Krishna link**.
[115:2:2] - Beneficiary: **Krishna delta farmers**; ~450 TMC moved.
[115:2:3] - **Temporary** until Polavaram's gravity flow starts.
[115:2:4] - The RMC runs about **174 km** – the statement "the canal traverses **100 km** through West Godavari and Krishna" is **false**.
[115:2:5] TABLE head: Link | Rivers | Status
  [115:2:5.0] Pattiseema | Godavari → Krishna | Working since 2015
  [115:2:5.1] Polavaram Right Main Canal | Godavari → Krishna (gravity) | With Polavaram
  [115:2:5.2] Polavaram-Banakacherla / Nallamala Sagar | Godavari → Krishna → Penna basin (Rayalaseema) | Proposed; 200 TMC
[115:2:6] - Telangana objects to AP's Godavari links, citing APRA 2014 and the 1980 GWDT award.
[115:2:7] - **GWDT award (1980)** permits diversion of **80 TMC** Godavari water to the Krishna.
[115:2:8] - **Polavaram**: first proposed as **"Ramapada Sagaram"** (Sir Sonti Venkataramamurthy); **started 2005**, still a **Jalayagnam** project; May 2026: 88% civil works done.
[115:2:9] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Geography - APPSC Exams Part 2 p.16; Rocket AP Geography p.36 (Pattiseema LIS: Godavari → Polavaram RMC → Krishna); old app notes 3:115 sub 3, 4; 174 km canal length general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 3 | Water Harvesting, Neeru-Chettu and Watersheds
[115:3:0] TABLE head: Programme | Year | Feature
  [115:3:0.0] Neeru-Meeru ("water and you") | 2000, AP (Naidu) | Rainwater harvesting, raising groundwater
  [115:3:0.1] Neeru-Chettu | 2 Oct 2014 | Aim: make AP drought-proof (water-secure) in five years; core works = desilting of minor irrigation tanks and channels, check dams, plantation; 1,00,171 works (2024-25)
  [115:3:0.2] NTR Jalasiri | April 2015 (renamed from Indira Jala Prabha) | Bore wells for small farmers' dry lands; aim to irrigate 10 lakh (1 million) acres
  [115:3:0.3] Jaladhara-Jalaharathi | 7 Apr 2026 | 100-day plan with Water Users Associations; launched at Yadiki
[115:3:1] - **Ralegan Siddhi** (Maharashtra, Anna Hazare): beat chronic drought through **community efforts** (watershed, check dams).
[115:3:2] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 2 p.6 (Neeru-Chettu 2 Oct 2014; NTR Jalasiri April 2015); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 (Neeru-Chettu); old app notes 3:115 sub 6; Jalasiri target and Ralegan Siddhi general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 4 | Groundwater Regulation and Water Policy
[115:4:0] - **APWALTA, 2002** (AP Water, Land and Trees Act): checks random borewell digging; the authority (Panchayat Secretary in villages) may require **rainwater harvesting structures on premises of 200 sq m or more**.
[115:4:1] - **National Water Policy 2012**: **Water Users Associations** to be given **statutory powers** to collect and retain water charges.
[115:4:2] - **Maharashtra (April 2016)**: banned digging bore wells **deeper than 200 feet** during drought.
[115:4:3] - **APFAMGS**: farmers collect rainfall and well data, prepare **crop-water budgets**, voluntarily limit pumping (FAO-supported).
[115:4:4] - 40,000-50,000 new bore wells a year: area irrigated per well falling.
[115:4:5] - **National Hydrology Project** (2016-17, 100% grant) set up AP's real-time groundwater monitoring.
[115:4:6] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.127 (NHP); old app notes 3:115 sub 8; NWP 2012 and Maharashtra 2016 ban general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 5 | Persons, Plan Periods and Jalayagnam
[115:5:0] TABLE head: Person / event | Fact
  [115:5:0.0] Nehru | Nagarjuna Sagar foundation, 10 Dec 1955 (First Plan)
  [115:5:0.1] Naidu | Veligonda foundation, 5 Mar 1996; Neeru-Meeru 2000
  [115:5:0.2] Y.S. Rajasekhara Reddy | Jalayagnam, 2004 (Tenth Plan)
  [115:5:0.3] NTR | Telugu Ganga
  [115:5:0.4] Project | Plan period
  [115:5:0.5] Prakasam Barrage | First-Second
  [115:5:0.6] Srisailam (1963) | Third
  [115:5:0.7] Telugu Ganga (1983) | Sixth
[115:5:1] - **Jalayagnam (2004)** envisaged **86 irrigation projects** (major and medium, flood banks, modernisation) mainly on the Krishna, Godavari and Pennar – aim: irrigate **1 crore acres**, stabilise 21 lakh acres.
[115:5:2] - Old app notes count **52 major and medium projects** in it (NextGen: 41 major + 25 minor proposed; counts differ by source).
[115:5:3] - Residual AP now counts **40 Jalayagnam projects** (23 major + 7 medium + 4 flood banks + 6 modernisation) for 37.63 lakh acres new potential.
[115:5:4] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 1 p.15 (Jalayagnam 1 crore acres, 21 lakh acres); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.126 (40 projects); "86 projects" general knowledge, not checked; old app notes 3:115 sub 5
## SUB 6 | Classification of Projects
[115:6:0] TABLE head: Basis | Rule
  [115:6:0.0] AP minor irrigation | Ayacut up to 5,000 acres
  [115:6:0.1] National (CCA) | Minor up to 2,000 ha; medium 2,000-10,000 ha; major above 10,000 ha

## SUB 7 | Micro-irrigation and PMKSY
[115:7:0] - **PMKSY (Pradhan Mantri Krishi Sinchai Yojana, 2015)** – the scheme formulated to boost irrigation = AIBP + Har Khet Ko Pani + Watershed + Per Drop More Crop.
[115:7:1] - **APMIP launched 2003**; now run as **RKVY-PDMC**.
[115:7:2] - 37.90 lakh acres, 13.49 lakh farmers since inception; **75% drip, 25% sprinkler**.
[115:7:3] - **AP 1st** nationally in micro-irrigation (2024-25); **100% subsidy for SC/ST**.
[115:7:4] - Per Drop More Crop (2015-16; under RKVY since 2022-23): **55%** small/marginal, **45%** others.
[115:7:5] - **Micro Irrigation Fund**: Rs 10,000 crore via **NABARD**; AP among eight States.
[115:7:6] - AIBP (PMKSY): 8 of 99 priority projects in AP; HKKP-RRR: 100 water bodies approved (60% central share).
[115:7:7] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.128 (AIBP, HKKP-RRR); NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 1 p.15 (APMIP 2003); old app notes 3:115 sub 7
## SUB 8 | Boards, Tribunals and Multipurpose Projects
[115:8:0] TABLE head: Project | River | State
  [115:8:0.0] Nagarjuna Sagar | Krishna | AP / Telangana
  [115:8:0.1] Hirakud | Mahanadi | Odisha (one of the longest earthen dams)
  [115:8:0.2] Damodar Valley | Damodar | Jharkhand / West Bengal
  [115:8:0.3] Tungabhadra | Tungabhadra | Karnataka-AP joint
[115:8:1] - **Tungabhadra Board** releases water to AP's **High Level Canal (HLC)** and **Low Level Canal (LLC)**, and to Telangana's **Rajolibanda Diversion Scheme (RDS)**.
[115:8:2] - **KRMB and GRMB**: under **APRA 2014**, Schedule XI principles.
[115:8:3] - **APRA Eleventh Schedule**: Handri-Neeva, Telugu Ganga, Galeru-Nagari, Veligonda, Kalwakurthy, Nettempadu to be completed with existing water sharing.
[115:8:4] TABLE head: Tribunal | Chair / year | Award
  [115:8:4.0] KWDT-I | Bachawat, set up 1969, award 1976 | 2,130 TMC at 75% dependability; combined AP 811 TMC
  [115:8:4.1] KWDT-II | Brijesh Kumar, set up 2004, report 2010 | 2,293 TMC at 65%; not notified
  [115:8:4.2] GWDT | 1980 | Polavaram: 80 TMC to Krishna
[115:8:5] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt s.91, Eleventh Schedule); old app notes 3:115 sub 9

=== NOTES 3:117 | Infrastructure Initiatives – Power Sector Position, Transport (ports, Airports, Highways, Railways)
## SUB 0 | Installed Power Capacity and Generation Mix
[117:0:0] TABLE head: Figure | Meaning
  [117:0:0.0] 25,726.89 MW (~25.7 GW) | Installed capacity, March 2022 (PYQ figure)
  [117:0:0.1] 27,392 MW | Overall installed capacity in SES 2024-25 (thermal, hydel, gas, renewables incl. captive/private)
  [117:0:0.2] 21,297.03 MW | Dec 2025 installed/contracted (different basis)
  [117:0:0.3] 33,240 MW | Pumped-storage potential (29 sites)
[117:0:1] - Mix: **thermal, hydel, gas, renewable – no nuclear** (the State has only asked for a 100 MW share of BHAVINI's proposed plant).
[117:0:2] - India as a whole: **thermal** (coal) gives the largest share of power generation.
[117:0:3] - Renewables FY 2024-25: **7,522 MW** (solar 3,755.6, wind 3,636.5); later SES figure 9,068 MW; State RE (Dec 2025) 10,639.75 MW with **solar 5,614.52 MW (largest)**, wind 4,397.67 MW.
[117:0:4] - APGENCO + APPDCL 8,789.03 MW = 46.37% of grid: thermal 6,610, hydel 1,773.60, solar 405.43.
[117:0:5] - **Simhadri** (NTPC, Visakhapatnam): **2,000 MW** (as of April 2025).
[117:0:6] - Share of generation (APPSC G2 Mains 2017): **AP GENCO : private : Central = 41 : 47 : 12**.
[117:0:7] - **Pranahita-Chevella** lift scheme (Telangana region, undivided AP): needs **3,466 MW**, "almost **one-third**" of the then installed capacity of 16,300 MW (Down to Earth's wording; arithmetically about one-fifth).
[117:0:8] - Peak demand **13,712 MW** (FY 2024-25) → **15,016 MW** (21 May 2026); transmission losses 2.63%, T&D losses 9.32% (FY 2024-25; 12.10% in 2014-15).
[117:0:9] - **9 hours** free daytime power to farm pump sets (22.36 lakh in app notes; 21.87 lakh in SES 2024-25); aqua farmers **Rs 1.50/unit**; nodal agency **APRAPSCom**.
[117:0:10] > Exam angle: capacity figures with a date, mix (no nuclear), Simhadri 2,000 MW, GENCO:private:central ratio.
[117:0:11] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.131-137; downtoearth.org.in "Half-baked mega project" (web search result); app notes 3:117
## SUB 1 | Power Reforms, DISCOMs and Energy Efficiency
[117:1:0] TABLE head: DISCOM | Full form | HQ | Areas (old districts)
  [117:1:0.0] APEPDCL | AP Eastern Power Distribution Company Ltd | Visakhapatnam | Srikakulam, Vizianagaram, Visakhapatnam, East and West Godavari
  [117:1:0.1] APCPDCL | AP Central Power Distribution Corporation Ltd | Vijayawada | Krishna, Guntur, Prakasam
  [117:1:0.2] APSPDCL | AP Southern Power Distribution Company Ltd | Tirupati | Nellore, Chittoor, Kadapa, Anantapur, Kurnool
[117:1:1] - **AP Electricity Reform Act 1998**: APSEB unbundled; World Bank loan (APL I, 1999) – AP went ahead alone with power reform (APPSC G1 1999).
[117:1:2] - **2000 unbundling**: four DISCOMs – EPDCL (Visakhapatnam), CPDCL (**Hyderabad**), NPDCL (Warangal), SPDCL (Tirupati).
[117:1:3] - **After 2014 (APRA)**: **Anantapur and Kurnool** moved from APCPDCL to **APSPDCL**.
[117:1:4] - Present Vijayawada APCPDCL **carved out of APSPDCL in 2020**, not one of the 2000 four.
[117:1:5] - **UDAY** (approved **Nov 2015**) → replaced by **RDSS (2021)**: reforms-based, results-linked; smart metering and loss-reduction works; AP's RDSS aim (PYQ): save about **₹2,000 crore** of T&D losses.
[117:1:6] - **World Bank** ranked AP **No. 1 in Energy Efficiency Implementation Readiness** (Nov 2016; score 42.01, then Rajasthan, Karnataka).
[117:1:7] - **ChandraKanthi**: AP programme to **distribute LED lamps** (AP led the DELP/UJALA LED drive).
[117:1:8] - **APSECM** (2012): India's first standalone State Designated Agency of BEE; first prize NECA-2024; SEEI 2024 score 87.25.
[117:1:9] - Smart meters and feeder segregation cut AT&C losses; APEPDCL and APSPDCL among top performers (Jan 2026).
[117:1:10] > Exam angle: APCPDCL = "Corporation", the other two = "Company"; Anantapur–Kurnool shift; World Bank EE ranking.
[117:1:11] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.132, p.135; deccanchronicle.com 5 Nov 2016 "World Bank declares AP No 1 in energy efficiency" (web search result); app notes 3:117
## SUB 2 | Ports and Maritime Policy
[117:2:0] TABLE head: Port | District | Category / model
  [117:2:0.0] Visakhapatnam | Visakhapatnam | Only major port (Union)
  [117:2:0.1] Gangavaram | Visakhapatnam | Non-major, private (Adani); PPP BOOT (2003)
  [117:2:0.2] Krishnapatnam | Nellore | Non-major, private (Adani); PPP BOST (2004); India's largest private-sector port at commissioning
  [117:2:0.3] Kakinada Deep Water | Kakinada | Non-major, PPP (OMST)
  [117:2:0.4] Kakinada SEZ / Gateway | Kakinada | Captive → commercial port via Swiss Challenge; GMR SPV
  [117:2:0.5] Ramayapatnam | Nellore/Prakasam | Greenfield, landlord model, 34.04 MTPA
  [117:2:0.6] Machilipatnam | Krishna | Greenfield, landlord model
  [117:2:0.7] Mulapeta (Bhavanapadu) | Srikakulam | Greenfield, landlord model
  [117:2:0.8] Nakkapalli | Visakhapatnam | Declared minor port (alumina, ANRAK)
  [117:2:0.9] Vodarevu / Nizampatnam | Prakasam / Guntur | BOOT (PPP)
[117:2:1] - Count: one major port + **14 non-major ports** (Port Policy 2015); now **16 notified ports** under GoAP (SES 2024-25).
[117:2:2] - **Coastline: 1,053.07 km – 3rd in India** after Gujarat and Tamil Nadu (revised 2025; old figure 974 km).
[117:2:3] - APRA 2014: Union to examine a major port at **Duggirajupatnam**.
[117:2:4] - **Visakhapatnam port:** natural deep harbour sheltered by **Dolphin's Nose**; exports **Bailadila** iron ore (Kirandul-Kottavalasa line) mainly to **Japan**.
[117:2:5] - **AP Maritime Policy 2024-29**: make AP India's leading **maritime gateway**; +145 MTPA by 2030; **300 MTPA by 2047**; one port in world's top 20; **20% of all-India cargo by 2047**; 25% of shipbuilding target. **AP Maritime Board (APMB)** = nodal body for **non-major ports**.
[117:2:6] > Exam angle: statements on port models (Swiss Challenge, BOOT, minor-port status); Krishnapatnam is NOT a major port.

## SUB 4 | Solar, Pumped Storage and Clean Energy Policy
[117:4:0] - **AP Integrated Clean Energy Policy 2024** (June 2024): absorbed the **AP Green Hydrogen and Green Ammonia Policy 2023**; targets **160 GW+**, **Rs 10 lakh crore**, **7.5 lakh jobs**, **Net Zero by 2047** (not 2030).
[117:4:1] - **Green Hydrogen & Ammonia Policy 2023:** **0.5 MTPA** green hydrogen or **2 MTPA** green ammonia in five years; nodal **NREDCAP**.
[117:4:2] - **Green Hydrogen Valley Declaration**, Amaravati, **19 July 2025**: "Green Hydrogen Capital"; nodal **SRM University**. National hydrogen hubs **outside AP**: Paradip (**Odisha**), Kandla, Tuticorin.
[117:4:3] - Progress: approvals Rs 2.88 lakh crore; Partnership Summit, **Visakhapatnam** (14-15 Nov 2025).

  [117:4:4.0] Kurnool | Ultra Mega Solar Park (Panyam ~1,000 MW)

  [117:4:4.2] Pathikonda + Kanekallu | Tata Power 800 MW solar-wind hybrid

  [117:4:4.5] Upper Sileru PSP | 1,350 MW, APGENCO-NHPC JV (Sep 2024)

  x: APRA 2014 provided for upgrading Visakhapatnam, Vijayawada and Tirupati airports; Kadapa was not among them.
[nindian-economy-68-11] sub6
  Which of the following pairs is correctly matched?
  Industrial Corridor – Movement of goods, services and economic activity | NIMZ – Industrial development along with connectivity | *Dedicated Freight Corridor – Dedicated railway freight infrastructure | Economic Corridor – Large areas with a world-class manufacturing ecosystem
  x: A Dedicated Freight Corridor is dedicated railway freight infrastructure. An industrial corridor combines industry with connectivity, an economic corridor moves goods and services, and an NIMZ is a large manufacturing area.
[nf44d31ac434] sub6
  Where is the headquarters of South Coast Railway, India's 18th railway zone?
  Vijayawada | *Visakhapatnam | Guntur | Guntakal
  x: SCoR is headquartered at Visakhapatnam, with Guntakal, Guntur, Vijayawada and Visakhapatnam divisions; it became operational on 1 June 2026.

=== NOTES 3:118 | Tourism, Logistics and Other Services in Andhra Pradesh
## SUB 0 | AP Retail Trade Policy 2015-20
[118:0:0] - Issued by G.O.Ms. No. 11, Industries & Commerce, **11 Jan 2016**; valid **5 years**; purpose: **development of the retail sector**.
[118:0:1] - Objectives: make AP a **preferred retail destination**; hub for **retail logistics** (warehouses); accelerate investment to underdeveloped regions; skills and jobs; retail as a tool of socio-economic development; promote **e-commerce**.
[118:0:2] - Targets: **Rs 5,000 crore** new investment and **20,000 additional jobs by 2020**; train **1 lakh youth** in 5 years.
[118:0:3] - **Applies to** retail enterprises with **turnover Rs 1 crore or more** OR **10 or more full-time employees** – so a **family kirana shop with fewer than 10 people is outside it** (single-desk and simplification apply to all).
[118:0:4] - Mega retail status: investment Rs 100 crore+ or 2,000 direct jobs.
[118:0:5] > Exam angle: 5 PYQs (Panchayat Secretary, Group-1) – aim, job target, investment target, applicability.
[118:0:6] ~~ Sources: G.O.Ms. No. 11 (11-01-2016), static.investindia.gov.in/s3fs-public/2019-01/2016INDS_MS11.pdf p.1-2 (web, fetched); app notes 3:118
## SUB 1 | Tourism – Economic Role, Policy and Bodies
[118:1:0] - Tourism gives **direct employment**, **indirect employment much higher than direct**, and **foreign exchange** (APPSC G2).
[118:1:1] - **AP Tourism Policy 2024-29**: linked to **Swarna Andhra @2047**; **industry status** to tourism and hospitality across all segments; tailor-made packages for **Mega and Ultra Mega** projects; targets: AP among India's **top three destinations**, tourism **above 20% of GVA** and 20% of workforce.
[118:1:2] - **Seven anchor hubs** (hub-and-spoke): Visakhapatnam, Tirupati, Araku, Rajahmundry, Amaravati, Srisailam, Gandikota; **25+ circuits** (2 Buddhist, 10 temple, 5 beach, 4 river cruise, 3 eco, 2 sea cruise, seaplane).
[118:1:3] - **Budget hotels**: promoted to **attract tourists**. **Swarna Andhra Tourism Vision 2047** launched at the **FAITH conclave**; AP won **Best Tourism Promotion State** at **GTM 2026**.
[118:1:4] - **APTDC**: fully State-owned operational arm – **Haritha hotels**, boating, sound-and-light, **Srisailam ropeway**; revenue **Rs 106.80 crore** (FY 2024-25); in **2000-05** its revenue rose **more than six times** (APPSC G2 2011). **APTA** = nodal tourism authority.
[118:1:5] - **Arrivals**: 36.42 crore (2025-26). Rank in domestic visits: **4th in 2024** (Ministry of Tourism: after UP, TN, Karnataka); SES 2024-25 calls AP the third most visited. **France** = most visited country (2024).
[118:1:6] - **Incredible India 2.0** (2017): promotion of **tourism and employment**.
[118:1:7] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.157-161; app notes 3:118
## SUB 2 | Central Schemes – Swadesh Darshan, PRASHAD and AP Projects
[118:2:0] TABLE head: Scheme | AP project | Cost
  [118:2:0.0] Swadesh Darshan 2.0 | Borra Caves, Araku | Rs 29.87 crore
  [118:2:0.1] PRASHAD | Simhachalam temple (pilgrimage amenities) | Rs 54.04 crore
  [118:2:0.2] PRASHAD | Annavaram temple | Rs 25.32 crore
  [118:2:0.3] SASCI | Gandikota Fort and Gorge | Rs 77.91 crore
  [118:2:0.4] SASCI | Havelock Bridge + Pushkar ghat, Rajahmundry | Rs 94.44 crore
  [118:2:0.5] CBDD (sub-scheme of SD 2.0) | Ahobilam, Nagarjunasagar | Rs 25 crore each
[118:2:1] - **Swadesh Darshan** (Jan **2015**): theme-based circuits – **15 circuits** incl. Buddhist, Coastal, Heritage, Ramayana, Krishna, Spiritual, **Sufi**, **Tirthankar** (Lok Sabha replies 2019, 2021). **SD 1.0**: 76 sanctioned, **75 completed**; **SD 2.0 = 52**; **PRASHAD = 54** (national).
[118:2:2] - **SASCI** = Special Assistance to States for Capital Investment; **CBDD** = Challenge Based Destination Development.
[118:2:3] - **PRASHAD** = Union **Ministry of Tourism**; **ITDC** = **1966**.
[118:2:4] - Before 2014, centrally assisted tourism projects of undivided AP were in **Chittoor, Warangal and Nalgonda** (APPSC G2 2011).
[118:2:5] > Exam angle: "Sufi circuit is not in Swadesh Darshan" is the old key (UPPCS 2017) – it is one of the 15 circuits now.
[118:2:6] ~~ Sources: Lok Sabha USQ replies 2019/2021 on Swadesh Darshan circuits (tourism.gov.in, web search result); Rajya Sabha reply Aug 2023 / The Hindu 8 Mar 2024 on Simhachalam PRASHAD (web search result); app notes 3:118
## SUB 3 | Tourism Places, Heritage and Circuits
[118:3:0] - AP = birthplace of **Mahayana Buddhism**; about **40** Buddhist monuments. Buddhist sites: Amaravati, Thotlakonda, Bavikonda, Bojjannakonda, Sankaram, Salihundam, Ghantasala (**Srisailam not** a Buddhist site).
[118:3:1] - **Rushikonda Beach** (Visakhapatnam): **Blue Flag**; 21 more beaches proposed.
[118:3:2] - Vizag beach-shack policy modelled on **Goa**.
[118:3:3] - **Visakhapatnam-Araku train:** glass-roofed **Vistadome** coach; Araku: coffee, **Borra caves**.
[118:3:4] - **Gandikota Fort** (YSR Kadapa): anchor hub; a recently found **16th-century copper plate** inscription relates to Gandikota's history (APPSC Panchayat Secretary).
[118:3:5] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.159-161; app notes 3:118
## SUB 4 | Logistics, Medical and Education Services
[118:4:0] - Multimodal logistics parks: **Visakhapatnam** and **Ananthapur**. **LEADS**: AP **Achiever** in 2022; **Fast Mover** in 2024.
[118:4:1] TABLE head: Hub | Feature
  [118:4:1.0] AMTZ (AP MedTech Zone) | Visakhapatnam; medical devices
  [118:4:1.1] Amaravati | Eye Tourism medical zone
  [118:4:1.2] AIIMS Mangalagiri | Leads eSanjeevani
  [118:4:1.3] Amaravati education hub | VIT, SRM, Amrita
  [118:4:1.4] IITTM Nellore | Trains hospitality staff
[118:4:2] - Union Budget 2026-27: **five regional medical hubs**; **five university townships** in industrial corridors.
[118:4:3] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:118

=== NOTES 3:119 | Information Technology and E-governance in Andhra Pradesh – It Policy, Rtgs, E-pragati
## SUB 0 | E-governance Milestones, Projects and Awards
[119:0:0] TABLE head: Year | Platform / event
  [119:0:0.0] 1998 | CARD (Computer-aided Administration of Registration Department): electronic delivery of all registration services
  [119:0:0.1] 1998-99 | TWINS (Twin cities network services, Hyderabad)
  [119:0:0.2] 2001 | eSeva: India's first integrated citizen service centres, under N. Chandrababu Naidu
  [119:0:0.3] 2002 | e-Procurement
  [119:0:0.4] Nov 2010 | APSWAN
  [119:0:0.5] 4 Nov 2011 | MeeSeva: single-window citizen service portal and centres
  [119:0:0.6] 2016 | e-Pragati launched; 2017-18 declared "e-Pragati year"
  [119:0:0.7] 2017 | RTGS
  [119:0:0.8] 2 Oct 2019 | Village and Ward Secretariats
  [119:0:0.9] 30 Jan 2025 | Mana Mitra (WhatsApp Governance), minister Nara Lokesh
[119:0:1] - **Raj-SWIFT** is **Rajasthan's** State intranet – not an AP project (Prajavani, CARD, TWINS are AP).
[119:0:2] - MeeSeva grew out of **eSeva** (not APSWAN); first-year MeeSeva training through television by the IT&C Department with **MANA TV**.
[119:0:3] - **CARD**: one of ten finalists, International Innovation awards of **CAPAM** (Commonwealth Association for Public Administration and Management).
[119:0:4] TABLE head: Award | Winner
  [119:0:4.0] 20th National e-Gov Conference, Visakhapatnam (Jan 2017) – Gold, Government Process Re-engineering | Loan Charge Creation Module, CCLA, Vijayawada (AP)
  [119:0:4.1] Same – Gold, Innovative Use of Technology | State Pension Portal, Madhya Pradesh
  [119:0:4.2] Same – Gold, Citizen-centric service delivery | Aadhaar-enabled PDS, Civil Supplies, Hyderabad (Telangana)
  [119:0:4.3] CSI-Nihilent e-Governance award 2017-18 | D-Krishi – AP app for farmers
[119:0:5] - **DIGIT** (eGov Foundation urban platform) is used by **Municipal Administration and Urban Development**.
[119:0:6] - **CFMS** (Comprehensive Financial Management System): integrated finance platform; primary objective **single source of truth** for State finances.
[119:0:7] > Exam angle: project ↔ purpose / award (CARD, MeeSeva, D-Krishi, 20th NCeG); "which is NOT an AP project".
[119:0:8] ~~ Sources: mentor-be.blogspot.com "20th good governance awards announced" (Jan 2017 award list, web, fetched); dqindia.com "Rajasthan: Swift path to E" and IIMA case "TWINS project" (web search results); app notes 3:119
## SUB 1 | E-pragati, RTGS and Grievance Platforms
[119:1:0] - **e-Pragati**: **whole-of-government** State Enterprise Architecture; developed under **CM N. Chandrababu Naidu** (2014-19); **14 packages / 72 projects / 33 departments**; core hubs **People, Land, Entity, GIS, IoT** (**no Finance Hub**); goal **faceless, paperless, cashless**.
[119:1:1] - Campaign aims: AP Fibre Grid to every gram panchayat; State Data Centre hosting all data and apps; **People Hub** database of citizens for welfare delivery. **Computerising land registration is NOT an e-Pragati aim** (that was CARD, 1998).
[119:1:2] - **RTGS** (Real Time Governance Society, **2017**): real-time monitoring, analytics; Command and Control Centre; **AWARE 2.0** disaster early warning; **AP DigiVerify** blockchain certificates; AI4PGRS.
[119:1:3] - **State Data Lake**: single source of truth for **40+ departments**; concerns **privacy, algorithmic bias**. **Mana Mitra**: **865 services** (SES 2025-26).
[119:1:4] - **Grievance platforms:** **Prajavani** → **Meekosam** (online forum, 2014-19) → **Spandana** (2019-24) → **PGRS**.
[119:1:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:119
## SUB 2 | Connectivity – Fiber Grid, Digital Village, APSFL
[119:2:0] - **AP Fiber Grid Phase-I**: **24,000 km** **IP-MPLS ring** (not GPON), all **13 districts**; **NOC at Visakhapatnam**.
[119:2:1] - **Mori** (Sakhinetipalli mandal, **East Godavari**): pilot fibre-grid smart village – AP's **first digital village** (Fibernet launched there **29 Dec 2016**).
[119:2:2] - **APSFL**: incorporated Oct 2015, launched 27 Dec 2017; **India's first State-owned ISP**.
[119:2:3] - **BharatNet Phase-II** (APSFL): **54,502 km, 11,254 GPs**. APSWAN (2010) → APSDWAN (1 May 2021).
[119:2:4] ~~ Sources: thehansindia.com 28 Dec 2016 "AP gets its first digital village" and business-standard.com "Mori gets smart digital village tag" (web search results); app notes 3:119
## SUB 3 | IT Policies and Definitions
[119:3:0] - **AP IT Policy 2021-24**: approved 30 June 2021; in force **1 April 2021 – 31 March 2024**; **55,000 jobs** aim.
[119:3:1]   - **Local procurement:** IT needs **up to Rs 1 crore** procured **exclusively** from IT companies/start-ups **operating in AP**, provided quality is comparable.
[119:3:2]   - **Entry-level IT job**: CTC **up to Rs 5 lakh**; high-end above Rs 10 lakh; an **MNC** has facilities in at least one country other than home.
[119:3:3] - **IT & GCC Policy 4.0 (2024-29)**: in force **12 Dec 2024**; GCC = a multinational's **own offshore unit**; co-working spaces in Tirupati, Visakhapatnam, Vijayawada.
[119:3:4] - **LIFT Policy 4.0** = **Land Incentive for Tech Hubs** (land for GCCs and IT infra developers).
[119:3:5] - **Innovation and Startup Policy 4.0**: by **ITE&C Department**; **20,000 startups**, **1 lakh jobs**; implemented by **AP Innovation Society**.
[119:3:6] - **Data Center Policy 4.0**: up to **1 GW**. **Drone Policy 4.0**: 100 companies, 25,000 pilots, **15,000 direct** jobs.
[119:3:7] - Legal: IT Act 2000 (MeitY; last amended **2008**); **AP IT (Electronic Service Delivery) Rules 2011** (electronic delivery mandatory); AP Cyber Security Policy 2017 → APCSOC (2018).
[119:3:8] > Exam angle: policy clauses (local procurement, CTC definitions) – asked in Polytechnic/NTRUHS papers.
[119:3:9] ~~ Sources: thehindu.com via inkl "Govt notifies IT policy for 2021-24" and businesstoday.in 30 Jun 2021 (web search results); app notes 3:119

[119:4:2] - New IT parks after 2014: Tirupati, Vijayawada, **Visakhapatnam** (Madhurawada-Rushikonda; RMZ GCC Park at **Kapuluppada**).
