=== NOTES 3:120 | Approaches and Initiatives in Ap Agriculture – Apcnf/zbnf, Horticulture, Aquaculture, Allied Sectors
## SUB 0 | Farmer Schemes, Extension and Institutions
[120:0:0] # Rythu Bharosa Kendras → Rythu Seva Kendras
[120:0:1] - **RBK = Rythu Bharosa Kendram**: launched **30 May 2020**; **10,778** village-level single-window centres beside village secretariats (SES 2023-24; asked twice): inputs, advisory, market linkage.
[120:0:2] - Now **Rythu Seva Kendras (RSKs)**: **10,606** RSKs in the State (SES 2024-25); rationalised to **8,489** functional (2026).
[120:0:3] - RSK services: **soil testing, e-Panta, CCRC issue, input subsidies, crop insurance, drone awareness, procurement** (**not industrial licences**).
[120:0:4] - **Integrated Call Centre (ICC)**: set up **30 May 2020** at **Gannavaram** (Krishna); toll-free **155251**; RBK Channel and Studio there (Mar 2021).
[120:0:5] # Income Support
[120:0:6] TABLE head: Scheme | Period | Amount / family / year | Coverage
  [120:0:6.0] YSR Rythu Bharosa-PM Kisan | 2019-24 | Rs 13,500 in three instalments (incl. Rs 6,000 PM-KISAN) | Owners, tenants, RoFR cultivators
  [120:0:6.1] Annadata Sukhibhava-PM Kisan (Super Six) | 2024-25 on | Rs 20,000 = Rs 14,000 State + Rs 6,000 PM-KISAN | Landholders; CCRC-holding tenants
[120:0:7] - **YSR Rythu Bharosa allocation, Budget 2019-20 (new govt): Rs 8,750 crore**.
[120:0:8] - Annadata Sukhibhava: **3 instalments** (Kharif Rs 7,000, Rabi Rs 7,000, Summer Rs 6,000); 2025-26: 46.86 lakh families, Rs 6,309 crore in 2 instalments.
[120:0:9] - **Vaddi Leni Runalu / Pavala Vaddi**: **4% subvention up to Rs 1 lakh; 1% for Rs 1-3 lakh**.
[120:0:10] # Scheme → Domain
[120:0:11] TABLE head: Scheme | Domain
  [120:0:11.0] Panta Sanjeevani | Farm ponds (rainwater harvesting), via MGNREGS convergence; launched with about Rs 100 crore
  [120:0:11.1] Polam Pilustondi (Aug 2014) | Field campaign to upgrade farmers' skills through technology transfer
  [120:0:11.2] Rythu Ratham (2017) | Tractors to farmers at a subsidy
  [120:0:11.3] Chandranna Rythu Kshetralu (2016-17) | Demonstration plots of best practices, about 51,000 ha
  [120:0:11.4] Bhuchetana | Soil-health (micronutrient) mission with an ICRISAT-led consortium
  [120:0:11.5] Jala Yagnam (YSR, 2004-09) | Irrigation: 52 major/medium projects
  [120:0:11.6] Jeeva Kranthi | Sheep and goat units
  [120:0:11.7] Paala Velluva | Dairy units for women; milk procured by Amul
  [120:0:11.8] Pasu Bhima Padhakam | Livestock insurance
  [120:0:11.9] GAP Polambadi | Farmer Field Schools, Ind.GAP
[120:0:12] # Missions and Partners
[120:0:13] - **Primary Sector Mission** (one of seven missions, 2014; later "Rythu Kosam"): agriculture and allied sectors; **ICRISAT** = knowledge partner for double-digit growth; growth engines identified (23 in the 2018-19 Agriculture Action Plan).
[120:0:14] - **District convergence committees** for agriculture: chaired by the **District Collector**.
[120:0:15] - **AP Agricultural Commission**: headed by **R. Radhakrishna**.
[120:0:16] - **AP State Agriculture Mission** (G.O.Ms.No.57, **1 July 2019**): **Chairman = Chief Minister**; Vice-Chairman **M.V.S. Nagi Reddy**.
[120:0:17] - **Free power to farms**: introduced by **Y.S. Rajasekhara Reddy** (**2004**); DBT pilot **Srikakulam (2020)** with smart meters; 22.36 lakh pump sets get 9 hours free daytime power.
[120:0:18] - **Aadhaar-enabled Fertiliser Distribution (DBT pilot)**: **Krishna district, 2016**.
[120:0:19] # Agriculture Budget
[120:0:20] - **First separate agriculture budget: undivided AP, 2013**.
[120:0:21] - **Agriculture and Allied Budget 2026-27: ~Rs 53,753 crore**, presented by **K. Atchannaidu** for the **third time**: irrigation Rs 14,277 crore, power subsidy Rs 13,722 crore, Price Stabilisation Fund Rs 500 crore.
[120:0:22] - Credit plan 2025-26: Rs 2,96,764 crore = 97% of target.
[120:0:23] # Rythu Bazars
[120:0:24] - **Started 1999**; farmers sell **directly to consumers**; **129** functioning; run by an **Estate Officer** (not the District Collector); prices ~**25% above wholesale, below local retail**.
[120:0:25] # Extension
[120:0:26] - **Padi Pantalu** = Telugu monthly of the **AP Agriculture Department**; **Vyavasayam** = ANGRAU's farm monthly.
[120:0:27] - **Farmers portal** (Agriculture Dept): **crop-wise best management practices** and advisories.
[120:0:28] - **ATMA**: centrally sponsored **60:40**; district body; prepares **SREP**; all 26 districts.
[120:0:29] - **Polambadi = Farmer Field Schools**; **GAP Polambadi** linked to **Ind.GAP** certification.
[120:0:30] - **Soil Health Cards** (RKVY): **judicious use of fertilisers**; **e-Crop booking** at RSKs = basis for insurance, MSP procurement, input subsidy.
[120:0:31] - Soil samples tested 5.52 lakh (2025-26); fertiliser use fell to about **38.1% of allotment** (2016-17 AE).
[120:0:32] - Debate: APRS wants free crop insurance back; gap between CCRCs issued and tenants actually financed; **New AP Tenancy Act, 2024** only planned; 8,24,620 active CCRCs (2025-26).
[120:0:33] # Crop Insurance and Tenancy
[120:0:34] - **Dr YSR Free Crop Insurance** from Kharif 2019; AP left PMFBY (Kharif 2020) and **rejoined PMFBY from Kharif 2022**.
[120:0:35] - **CCRC** = Crop Cultivator Rights Card (**AP Crop Cultivator Rights Act, 2019**); **valid 11 months**; gives tenants loans, insurance, MSP; **no ownership rights**. 9,13,283 CCRCs issued (3 Jan 2025).
[120:0:36] > Exam angle: scheme ↔ purpose (Panta Sanjeevani = farm ponds), RBK full form and count, Rs 13,500 split, ICC at Gannavaram.
[120:0:37] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.8, p.72; NextGen AP Economy STATIC-1 Part 2 p.6 (Polam Pilustondi Aug 2014); app notes 3:120; general knowledge for scheme amounts and dates (not checked)
## SUB 1 | Land Use, Holdings and Crops
[120:1:0] # Land Use
[120:1:1] - Agriculture supports about **60% of population**; agriculture and allied = **33% of GVA** (2025-26).
[120:1:2] - Geographical area **162.97 lakh ha**; net sown area **49.55 lakh ha = 30.40%** (2023-24, drought); **59.14 lakh ha = 36.29%** (2024-25).
[120:1:3] - NSA fell from 67.26 lakh ha (2008-09) to 49.55 lakh ha (2023-24). Barren and uncultivable land 13.35 lakh ha (8.19%).
[120:1:4] - **Older land-use keys:** least NSA **Vizianagaram**, largest barren land **YSR Kadapa** (2015-16).
[120:1:5] - **NITI Aayog: cultivated area 10.1 million ha (2020-21) "= 37%" of geographical area** (keyed; 37% actually fits the net sown area).
[120:1:6] - **Common Property Resources** (NSS 1998): about **9%** of AP's area (20.3 lakh ha) vs India's 15%.
[120:1:7] - **Cropping intensity** (GCA / NSA): **1.25** (2016-17); **1.21** (2023-24).
[120:1:8] # Operational Holdings
[120:1:9] - Average holding **0.94 ha (2015-16) → 0.86 ha (2021-22)**; holdings **85.24 lakh → 96.90 lakh**; nationally **small and marginal (0-2 ha)** holdings dominate (**86.21%**).
[120:1:10] - **Marginal holdings: about 49.84 lakh** (Agriculture Census 2010-11). Most holdings (2010-11, present AP): **Guntur** (keyed).
[120:1:11] # Food Grains and Paddy
[120:1:12] - **Paddy = largest area of any crop**: **20.97 lakh ha of 37.51 lakh ha** food-grain area (~56%, 2024-25); output **127.15 lakh t** (**kharif 78.79 > rabi 48.36**); yield ~6.1 t/ha.
[120:1:13] - Food-grain area: **41.44 lakh ha (2021-22)** → 33.24 (2023-24) → 37.51 (2024-25); output 161.86 lakh t (2024-25).
[120:1:14] - **Food-grain output 2018-19: 151 lakh t** (SES 2018-19; Ministry final figures lower).
[120:1:15] - Area under food crops has been **growing more slowly / declining** relative to non-food crops (shift to commercial crops).
[120:1:16] - Rain-fed yields are low: about **1 tonne/ha**.
[120:1:17] - **Cotton ("white gold")**: commercial crop with the **largest area expansion**; **2nd crop in GVA after paddy**.
[120:1:18] - Rice yield above national average: AP **3,846 kg/ha** vs India 2,844 (2022-23).
[120:1:19] - Rice yield 2021-22: AP **3,392 kg/ha** vs India 2,798. Food-grain area **up 12.85%** in 2024-25 after an **11.7% rainfall surplus** (previous year 16.5% deficit).
[120:1:20] - **Second food grain after paddy:** **jowar** in older keys; **maize** is now AP's second cereal. Largest food-crop area (undivided AP, key): **West Godavari**.
[120:1:21] # Cropping Pattern Shifts
[120:1:22] - Shift to **fruits, vegetables, flowers, spices** (profitability, exports); Godavari delta **paddy land converted to aquaculture**.
[120:1:23] - Debate: paddy is a major **methane** source; experts want diversification with procurement support.
[120:1:24] - Kharif 2026: only ~61% of normal area sown by 19 Aug (groundnut 34%, paddy 62%); El Nino action plan (Jul 2026): control room, RSK advisories, **one Mobile Rythu Bazaar per district** (no compulsory shift to sugarcane).
[120:1:25] # Crop → Region
[120:1:26] TABLE head: Region / district | Crop
  [120:1:26.0] Godavari delta (East + West Godavari) | Main rice area and aquaculture hub
  [120:1:26.1] Krishna zone (Krishna, Guntur, Prakasam) | Paddy, chillies, cotton
  [120:1:26.2] Anantapuramu (scarce rainfall) | Groundnut
  [120:1:26.3] Guntur | Spice hub: chillies, Duggirala turmeric, Spices Park; Tobacco Board of India HQ
  [120:1:26.4] Prakasam (main) + Nellore | FCV tobacco
  [120:1:26.5] Kurnool, YSR Kadapa | Onion
  [120:1:26.6] Palasa (Srikakulam) | Cashew
  [120:1:26.7] East Godavari + Kakinada | Oil palm
[120:1:27] - **Agri Export Zones** in AP: **Chittoor** (mango pulp, vegetables), **Krishna** (mango), **Guntur** (chillies).
[120:1:28] - **Bondalu paddy** (Kakinada, Dec 2019): farmers advised to stop because **Kerala**, the earlier buyer, began growing it.
[120:1:29] - **Marigold** is grown around **chilli** fields to attract insects (pollination, trap crop).
[120:1:30] - **Millet revival**: Agriculture Department initiative linking millet farming systems with household consumption.
[120:1:31] # Growth Engines (Swarna Andhra @2047)
[120:1:32] - APSES 2024-25: **30% growth over five years**; **17 growth engines**.
[120:1:33] - **11 productivity crops**: paddy, maize, jowar, bajra, ragi, redgram, blackgram, bengalgram, groundnut, sunflower, sesamum (**not sugarcane**).
[120:1:34] - Kharif 2026: all 28 districts in deficit; El Nino plan: shift to red gram, black gram, castor; **Highest Kharif MSP 2026-27: sesamum Rs 10,346/qtl**.
[120:1:35] > Exam angle: largest crop (paddy), district ↔ crop, land-use percentages by year and report.
[120:1:36] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.8, p.48-50; app notes 3:120; general knowledge for 2010-11 census and district keys (not checked)
## SUB 2 | Natural Farming – ZBNF to APCNF
[120:2:0] # Origin and Agency
[120:2:1] - **ZBNF** developed **mid-1990s** by **Subhash Palekar** (Padma Shri); "zero budget" = **no purchased fertilisers or pesticides**.
[120:2:2] - AP launched ZBNF in **2016**; renamed **APCNF = Andhra Pradesh Community-managed Natural Farming**. **2018** = AP's Year of Natural Farming.
[120:2:3] - **RySS = Rythu Sadhikara Samstha**: **not-for-profit company** of the **AP Department of Agriculture**; incorporated **October 2014**; implements APCNF.
[120:2:4] - **Target (2018)**: make AP **India's first 100% natural farming (ZBNF) State by 2024** (6 million farmers); now "100% natural farming" by 2047.
[120:2:5] - **ZBNF won 3rd place** (top 10 of 120 entries) at the **Paris Peace Forum, Nov 2018**.
[120:2:6] # Practices
[120:2:7] TABLE head: Practice | Meaning
  [120:2:7.0] Beejamrutham | Seed treatment
  [120:2:7.1] Jeevamrutham | Bio-fertiliser: cow dung, urine, jaggery, pulse flour, soil
  [120:2:7.2] Ghanajeevamrutham | Solid form of Jeevamrutham
  [120:2:7.3] Mulching (Acchadana) | Crop residue cover: saves moisture, checks weeds
  [120:2:7.4] PMDS | Pre-Monsoon Dry Sowing on residual moisture
  [120:2:7.5] 365-day green cover | Soil covered all year by cover crops
[120:2:8] - Trap crops: marigold, castor. PMDS on 92,000 acres in Kurnool (Jul 2026).
[120:2:9] # Coverage and Targets
[120:2:10] - Now: **10.20 lakh farmers, 4.89 lakh ha**, 4,116 GPs. Targets: all GPs by 2026-27; 41 lakh farmers on 19 lakh ha by 2028-29.
[120:2:11] - Unit = **cluster of about 5 Gram Panchayats/villages** (~2,000 farmers; 1,500-2,500 acres).
[120:2:12] - **Pesticide use** falling: 2,650 MT (2014-15) → 1,990 MT (2023-24); **266.166 MT a.i. reduction in 2023-24** and 193.248 MT in Kharif 2024-25, credited to **awareness** by the Agriculture Department.
[120:2:13] # Funding, Awards and Replication
[120:2:14] - **KfW** (German bank) loan; **Azim Premji Foundation** grants.
[120:2:15] TABLE head: Award | Detail
  [120:2:15.0] M.S. Swaminathan Award for Environment Protection | To RySS (2022-23)
  [120:2:15.1] Gulbenkian Prize for Humanity (shared) | 2024; Lisbon, 11 July 2024
  [120:2:15.2] Food Planet Prize | Sweden (Curt Bergfors Foundation); launched with US$1 million prizes; 2026 prize US$1.5 million, Bastad, June 2026; APCNF first Indian finalist
[120:2:16] - APCNF = "**world's largest agro-ecological transition**"; copied in **Zambia and Sri Lanka**.
[120:2:17] - **NMNF** = natural farming (no external inputs); **PKVY** = **organic**, clusters of **50 farmers / 50 acres**, PGS-India certified.
[120:2:18] - NMNF action plans: **7.78 lakh ha, 19.75 lakh farmers**; PKVY: 200 tribal clusters in Visakhapatnam. RySS earlier ran the **Debt Redemption Scheme 2014**; ZBNF features: minimum tillage, livestock integration, crop diversity, no chemicals. Kenyan and Gujarat delegations visited Guntur (2026).
[120:2:19] - **ISHA Foundation awards (28 July 2024)**: four APCNF paddy farmers from **Guntur, Krishna, West Godavari and Srikakulam** honoured at Sripuram (Vellore), Save Soil campaign.
[120:2:20] > Exam angle: RySS full form and nature, ZBNF founder, 2024 target, Paris Peace Forum, natural vs organic.
[120:2:21] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.8, p.58; app notes 3:120; general knowledge for Paris Peace Forum and 2024 target (not checked)
## SUB 3 | Livestock and Dairy
[120:3:0] # Product Ranks
[120:3:1] TABLE head: Product | AP rank (2023-24, SES 2024-25) | Output
  [120:3:1.0] Eggs | 1st | 2,548.74 crore
  [120:3:1.1] Meat | 5th | 10.68 lakh t
  [120:3:1.2] Milk | 7th | 139.94 lakh t
[120:3:2] - SES 2024-25 heading: **"Milk, Meat and Eggs – Key Growth Drivers"** (fur, skins and hides are not).
[120:3:3] - Eggs **1st in 2018-19** and every year since; 2024-25: eggs 1st, meat 4th (11.37 lakh t).
[120:3:4] - **White Paper on Agriculture (Dec 2018)**: AP 1st in poultry, 2nd in eggs, 4th in meat, 5th in milk; named **livestock and horticulture** as growth engines.
[120:3:5] - Milk fell **9.41%** in 2023-24; supported by **crossbred cattle** and **graded Murrah buffaloes**.
[120:3:6] - **Older district keys**: milk – **Krishna** topped (2016-17; Sept 2016); meat – **Kurnool** (Sept 2016). **Chittoor** = top dairy district in the Amul tie-up.
[120:3:7] - **Undivided AP (2007-08)**: **1st in sheep**; **1st in poultry** during the 2000s.
[120:3:8] # Livestock Census (20th, 2019)
[120:3:9] TABLE head: Species | AP rank | Population (lakh)
  [120:3:9.0] Poultry | 2nd | 1,078.63
  [120:3:9.1] Sheep | 2nd | 176.27
  [120:3:9.2] Buffalo | 6th | 62.19
  [120:3:9.3] Goat | 11th | 55.22
  [120:3:9.4] Cattle | 14th | 46.00
[120:3:10] - Breeds: **Ongole, Punganur** – cattle; **Godavari** – buffalo; **Nellore** – sheep; **Aseel** – poultry.
[120:3:11] - **Rashtriya Gokul Mission**: **National Kamadhenu Breeding Centre, Chintaladeevi** (Nellore).
[120:3:12] # Schemes
[120:3:13] TABLE head: Scheme | Benefit
  [120:3:13.0] Sunandini | Feed subsidy for rearing female calves (cows and buffaloes)
  [120:3:13.1] Mana Kodi (backyard poultry) | Better nutrition of the rural poor (eggs, meat)
  [120:3:13.2] SC Corporation animal husbandry scheme | Purchase of graded Murrah buffaloes
  [120:3:13.3] Pasu Bhima Padhakam | Insurance, premium 40:40:20 (Centre:State:beneficiary) for BPL/SC/ST
  [120:3:13.4] Pashu KCC | Collateral-free up to Rs 1.60 lakh; effective 4% on timely repayment
[120:3:14] - **VBRI (Veterinary Biological Research Institute)**: **Vijayawada** (residual AP).
[120:3:15] - **Amul-AP tie-up (APDDCF)**: 2020. Mobile veterinary clinics: **340 = 2 per constituency**.
[120:3:16] - Mobile clinics: 175 in Phase 1 (19 May 2022); livestock units for women 2,15,626; **Pasu Bhima** 25:25:50 for APL; Pashu KCC up to Rs 3 lakh under bank norms (3% subvention).
[120:3:17] - **Credit Guarantee Scheme for livestock**: first ever, by **DAHD** (2023) under AHIDF; Rs 750 crore fund. NADCP merged into **LHDCP**.
[120:3:18] - India 2023-24: milk **239.30 MT**, **471 g/day** per capita; eggs **103 per capita/year**; **2nd in world** egg production (142.77 billion). India: **world's largest livestock population** (19.46% of cattle, 59.29% of buffalo); 21st Livestock Census Nov 2024-Feb 2025.
[120:3:19] - Descending AP livestock order: **poultry, sheep, buffalo, cattle**. Gokul Gram farm **Chadalawada** (Prakasam) ⚠ APPSC key (2025) gave Nellore.
[120:3:20] - **AHIDF** (DAHD): dairy/meat processing, feed plants, breed farms (**not irrigation canals**); **NADCP** (Sep 2019): 100% central, FMD-free by 2030; **Pashu Aadhaar** = 12-digit ear tag; **1962 app** replaced e-Gopala; **NLM** (2014-15, revised 2021-22).
[120:3:21] > Exam angle: product ranks (eggs 1st), census ranks, scheme ↔ animal.

## SUB 4 | Fisheries and Aquaculture
[120:4:0] # Coastline, Output and Ranks
[120:4:1] - **Coastline 1,053.07 km – 3rd in India** after Gujarat and Tamil Nadu (APSES 2025-26; PIB Dec 2025); older figure **974 km, "2nd longest"**.
[120:4:2] - AP ≈ **30% of India's fish** and **31% of shrimp exports** (SES 2024-25); inland fisheries dominate (~82%).
[120:4:3] - **Shrimp / cultured prawn**: AP **India's largest producer** (~**70%** of cultured prawns, Budget 2017-18).
[120:4:4] - Aquaculture = core of AP's **Blue Economy**; 5 lakh+ families.
[120:4:5] # Seafood Exports
[120:4:6] TABLE head: Share | Base
  [120:4:6.0] 45% | India's seafood exports (AP Budget 2017-18)
  [120:4:6.1] 36% | India's seafood export value, end of 2018-19
  [120:4:6.2] 31% | India's shrimp exports (2024-25)
  [120:4:6.3] 75% | India's cultured-shrimp exports (2025-26)
[120:4:7] - **2019**: AP shrimp exports hit by a **fall in international shrimp prices**.
[120:4:8] - **MPEDA**: under **Ministry of Commerce**; arms RGCA, NETFISH, NaCSA.
[120:4:9] # Policy, Regulation and Infrastructure
[120:4:10] - **AP Fisheries Policy 2015-20**: MI/Gram Panchayat tanks leased to fishermen cooperative societies for **3 years**.
[120:4:11] - **APSADA Act, 2020**: licenses **freshwater** aquaculture (e-Matsyakar); farms **within 2 km of HTL** → **Coastal Aquaculture Authority (CAA Act, 2005)**.
[120:4:12] - **India's first operational Mega Aqua Food Park**: **Godavari Mega Aqua Food Park, Tundurru (Bhimavaram), West Godavari**, commissioned Feb 2019.
[120:4:13] - **10 fishing harbours** under **PMMSY** (not Sagarmala). Aqua-power tariff **Rs 1.50/unit** up to 10 acres in notified zones.
[120:4:14] - Fishing harbours cost Rs 3,699.09 crore (CSS-PMMSY); aqua tariff **Rs 3.85** for bigger farms or outside zones (10-acre cap lifted Aug 2026).
[120:4:15] - **PMMSY subsidy**: **40% general** (24% GoI + 16% GoAP), **60% women/SC/ST** (36% + 24%); PM-MKSSY covers institutional credit, aquaculture insurance, traceability (**no compulsory trawler replacement**).
[120:4:16] - **China = 2nd-largest** seafood destination (2025-26, +20.1% volume); record exports Rs 73,890 crore; output 55.39 lakh t (2024-25), inland ~82% / marine ~18% (2017-18); exports depend on the US market.
[120:4:17] - **PMMSY (2020)**: **Rs 20,050 crore**; **PM-MKSSY**: central sector sub-scheme, 2023-24 to 2026-27, **Rs 6,000 crore**; FIDF corpus Rs 7,522 crore.
[120:4:18] # Fishermen Welfare
[120:4:19] - **Marine fishing ban: 61 days, 15 April – 14 June**; relief **Rs 20,000 per family** (from 2024-25; Rs 10,000 from 2019-20, Rs 4,000 before).
[120:4:20] - Diesel subsidy **Rs 9/litre**; ex-gratia **Rs 10 lakh**; **AP Fisheries University**: **Narsapur, West Godavari**.
[120:4:21] - Ex-gratia 2024-25: Rs 315 lakh to 63 families; KCC to fishers since 2018-19. Fisheries University is **not** in East Godavari.
[120:4:22] > Exam angle: coastline length and rank (now 3rd), shrimp shares by base, first aqua mega food park.
[120:4:23] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.76; PIB Release 2198800 (4 Dec 2025, via RUNBOOK settled facts); swarajyamag.com/amp/story/insta/union-minister-harsimrat-kaur-badal-commissions-first-aqua-mega-food-park-in-andhra-pradesh (web search, Feb 2019); app notes 3:120
## SUB 5 | Horticulture and Oil Palm
[120:5:0] # Size and Ranks
[120:5:1] - **AP = largest fruit producer in India** (**1st**, 15.6% of national horticulture output); 18.23 lakh ha, 365.92 lakh MT (SES 2024-25). Budget 2017-18 placed AP **2nd** in fruits.
[120:5:2] - **1st in productivity**: **oil palm, papaya, lime, cocoa, tomato, chillies**; **2nd**: **mango, sweet orange, turmeric** (SES 2024-25).
[120:5:3] - **Rayalaseema** = horticulture hub: 45% of area, 52% of output, 71% of micro-irrigation area.
[120:5:4] - Growth-engine crops: **11 crops in 24 clusters** (banana, mango, chilli, oil palm, cocoa, dragon fruit, onion, cashew, coconut, tomato, coffee).
[120:5:5] - Horticulture GVA Rs 1,52,950 crore: AP **3rd** after UP and Maharashtra; target Rs 3 lakh crore by 2029-30.
[120:5:6] # Oil Palm
[120:5:7] - AP **1st in area, production, productivity** of oil palm: 2.27 lakh ha, 17.63 lakh MT FFB, 3.10 lakh MT CPO (2023-24); 2.49 lakh ha = 48% of India, FFB **22.81 lakh MT** (2025-26).
[120:5:8] - **Palm oil production 17.81 lakh tonnes** (2017-18 agriculture statistics).
[120:5:9] - **Palm oil = most consumed edible oil in India** (mostly imported).
[120:5:10] - **NMEO-OP (2021)**: reach **10 lakh ha**; earlier Oilseeds Technology Mission (1986) → NMOOP (2014).
[120:5:11] # MIDH, Micro-irrigation, Post-harvest
[120:5:12] - **MIDH** (2014-15): NHM + HMNEH; **60:40** general, 90:10 NE/Himalayan. **NHM** from 2005-06.
[120:5:13] - **APMIP** since **2003**: 37.90 lakh acres, 13.49 lakh farmers; targets **35% of GSA by 2030**, **50% by 2047**.
[120:5:14] - Post-harvest target 2025-26: **1,030 units**. **Operation Greens (TOP)**: tomato, onion, potato.
[120:5:15] - Operation Greens is modelled on **Operation Flood**; value-chain study crops: **mango, banana, tomato, pomegranate, sweet lime, papaya** (**not oil palm**).
[120:5:16] - **NHM**: started **May 2005**; selected districts of **18 States and 5 UTs** (incl. UP); **NE and Himalayan States not under NHM**; objectives: high growth, post-harvest management, human resources (**financial assistance not an objective**). MIDH: **100% central for NHB, CDB, CIH**.
[120:5:17] - APMIP: **75% drip**; target 15 lakh ha in five years. Oil palm: NMEO-OP adds **6.5 lakh ha**; CPO **11.20 lakh t by 2025-26**, **28 lakh t by 2029-30**; edible-oil import dependence **63.2% (2015-16) → 56.25% (2023-24)**. Coconut: AP 4th (1.06 lakh ha).
[120:5:18] - Horticulture area **46.43 lakh acres**, output **406.38 lakh MT** (Survey 2025-26); 21% GVA growth (2024-25); **22 growth-engine crops**; Rayalaseema ~7.8 lakh ha; chillies 2nd (2025-26); 1st in productivity also coconut.
[120:5:19] > Exam angle: productivity ranks (oil palm 1st, mango 2nd), palm-oil figures, fruits rank by year.
[120:5:20] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.8, p.68-70; app notes 3:120

=== NOTES 3:123 | Institutional Credit to Agriculture in Andhra Pradesh – Banks, Nabard and Shgs
## SUB 0 | SHG Movement – SERP, Velugu/IKP, Stree Nidhi, MEPMA
[123:0:0] TABLE head: Year | Programme | Fact
  [123:0:0.0] Sep 1982 | DWCRA | Development of Women and Children in Rural Areas; Centre:State 75:25
  [123:0:0.1] 2000 | Velugu | Women's groups against poverty; World Bank-aided; SERP registered 2000
  [123:0:0.2] 2004 | Pavala Vaddi | Interest subsidy for SHGs (3% effective)
  [123:0:0.3] 2004-05 | Indira Kranthi Patham (IKP) | Velugu + DWCRA merged – all anti-poverty programmes in one integrated scheme; flagship of SERP
  [123:0:0.4] 2011 | Stree Nidhi | Credit cooperative federation promoted by Mandal Samakhyas + GoAP
[123:0:1] - **SERP** = Society for Elimination of Rural Poverty (rural); **MEPMA** = Mission for Elimination of Poverty in Municipal Areas (**urban** SHGs).
[123:0:2] - **SHG registration:** **no registration is mandatory** (informal groups of 10-20).
[123:0:3] - **NRLM "Institutions of the Poor":** **SHGs and their federations** (VOs, MS) – not PACS or RRBs.
[123:0:4] - **AP leads India** in SHG-bank linkage: **36%** national share, **99.66%** recovery (2024-25). Members lack individual **CIBIL** histories.
[123:0:5] - **MEPMA limits:** SHG bank linkage up to **Rs 20 lakh**; individual loans **Rs 50,000-2 lakh**.
[123:0:6] # Stree Nidhi
[123:0:7] - **Streenidhi Credit Cooperative Federation Ltd** (2011): **banking institution promoted by SHG women**; a **community-based micro-finance** body of the Mandal Samakhyas with GoAP; registered under the AP Cooperative Societies Act, 1964.
[123:0:8] - **Purpose:** **timely and affordable credit** to SHG members through VOs/SLFs and MS/TLFs; also deposits, loan insurance, Business Correspondent.
[123:0:9] - **Rate:** **13%** at launch (2011); **11%** now (livelihood loans up to Rs 1 lakh, APSES 2024-25). Loans Rs 21,123.71 crore (2011-2024).
[123:0:10] - Stree Nidhi describes itself as a "**historic necessity**" – SHG women needed their own credit body.
[123:0:11] - **PS-SBG** (Pattabhi Sitaramayya-Self Business Group) for rural women entrepreneurs: launched by **Andhra Bank**.
[123:0:12] > Traps: MEPMA urban, SERP rural; IKP merged Velugu and DWCRA (not Indira Prabha – that is land development); SHGs need no registration.
[123:0:13] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy Static-1 01 p.14-15 (Velugu 2000, Pavala Vaddi 2004, IKP); NextGen AP Economy Static-1 02 p.2, p.6 (Stree Nidhi 2011, AP Coop Act 1964); AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.283 (Streenidhi 11%, loans); microcapital.org (13% at launch, web search); app notes 3:123 sub 6-7
[123:0:14] - **Average bank credit per SHG (AP, 2016-17):** about **Rs 2.89 lakh**.
[123:0:15] - **Stree Nidhi loans 2018-19:** about **Rs 4,313 crore** to SHG women.
## SUB 1 | NABARD – Establishment and Functions
[123:1:0] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [123:1:0.0] Recommended by | CRAFICARD – Committee to Review Arrangements for Institutional Credit for Agriculture and Rural Development, chaired by B. Sivaraman
  [123:1:0.1] Act / set up | NABARD Act 1981; set up 12 July 1982
  [123:1:0.2] Took over | RBI's ACD and RPCC and the whole of ARDC
  [123:1:0.3] Capital / HQ | Rs 100 crore; Mumbai; fully Government-owned since 2019
[123:1:1] - **Role:** apex **refinancing** institution for rural credit – **term loans/refinance to State Cooperative Banks**, RRBs and commercial banks; **long-term loans to State Governments** to contribute to the **share capital** of cooperative credit societies.
[123:1:2] - **Does NOT lend short-term credit directly** to farmers.
[123:1:3] - **Supervision:** inspects **RRBs, State and District Central Cooperative Banks** – **not PACS** (inspected by the Registrar/DCCB).
[123:1:4] - **SHG-Bank Linkage** (1992-93); **KCC** model scheme (**1998**, R.V. Gupta Committee). Same year as NABARD: **EXIM Bank** (1982).
[123:1:5] - Order: RRBs (1975) → NABARD (1982) → SHG linkage (1992-93) → KCC (1998).
[123:1:6] > Trap: NABARD does not inspect primary cooperative societies.
[123:1:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 07 p.10; Rocket Indian Economy p.44 (NABARD 12 July 1982, Sivaraman); NextGen AP Economy Static-1 01 p.14 (KCC 1998); app notes 3:123 sub 0
[123:1:8] - **SIDBI:** set up **2 April 1990**; apex bank for MSME finance (HQ Lucknow).
## SUB 2 | RIDF, Indira Prabha and Other NABARD Programmes
[123:2:0] - **RIDF:** created by GoI **in NABARD** in **1995-96**; corpus **Rs 2,000 crore** (not Rs 20,000 crore); **NABARD** is the nodal agency.
[123:2:1] TABLE head: Activity | RIDF category
  [123:2:1.0] Rural drinking water | Social sector
  [123:2:1.1] Rural roads | Rural connectivity
  [123:2:1.2] Minor irrigation | Agriculture and related
[123:2:2] - Loans to States: **irrigation** largest (30%), roads 26%, social 21%; RIDF XXVIII (2023-24) Rs 40,475 crore.
[123:2:3] - AP: **68 lift irrigation schemes** modernised at Rs 74.22 crore to stabilise **1.03 lakh acres** (2024-25).
[123:2:4] - **Indira Prabha** (2004, Sirigandayapalem, Guntur): State programme with **NABARD** support for **land development** of **assigned land distributed to the poor**. (Indira Kranthi = SHG programme; Bhubharati = land records; Janmabhoomi = people's participation.)
[123:2:5] TABLE head: Fund | Purpose
  [123:2:5.0] LTIF | State share of PMKSY irrigation projects
  [123:2:5.1] Micro Irrigation Fund | AP among 8 States funded
  [123:2:5.2] Warehouse Infrastructure Fund (2013-14) | Scientific storage, APMC facilities
[123:2:6] - Share-sale (disinvestment) money goes to **NIF**, not RIDF.
[123:2:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy Static-1 01 p.15 (Indira Prabha 2004); app notes 3:123 sub 4
## SUB 3 | Sources of Agricultural Credit and RRBs in AP
[123:3:0] - **Commercial banks** = **major source** of institutional farm credit in AP (as in India: SCBs over 70% of formal farm credit).
[123:3:1] - **2007-08:** **cooperative credit societies** gave about **one-fifth** of AP's crop loans; commercial banks most of the rest.
[123:3:2] - Institutional: cooperatives, commercial banks, RRBs, Government. **Non-institutional:** moneylenders.
[123:3:3] - Exclusion: **tenant, tribal, women and oral-lessee** farmers – no **land records**.
[123:3:4] TABLE head: Loan term | Period
  [123:3:4.0] Short-term | Up to 1 year, maximum 15 months
  [123:3:4.1] Medium-term | 15 months to 3 (or 5) years
  [123:3:4.2] Long-term | Above 3 (or 5) years
[123:3:5] # RRBs
[123:3:6] - Set up **2 October 1975** on the **Narasimham Working Group** (1975) recommendations; first = **Prathama Bank** (Moradabad); RRB Act 1976; shares Centre 50 : State 15 : sponsor bank 35.
[123:3:7] - **One State, One RRB:** AP's four RRBs merged into **Andhra Pradesh Grameena Bank** from **1 May 2025**; sponsor **Union Bank of India**; RRBs in India 43 → **28**.
[123:3:8] - **Andhra Bank:** founded **1923**, Machilipatnam, by **Bhogaraju Pattabhi Sitaramayya**; merged into **Union Bank of India** (1 April 2020).
[123:3:9] - **Narasimham Committee I (1991):** began banking reforms (different from the 1975 Working Group).
[123:3:10] ~~ Sources: NextGen Indian Economy - APPSC 07 p.8 (Narasimham Working Group, 2 Oct 1975, Prathama); Rocket Indian Economy p.44; app notes 3:123 sub 1-2 (as of 2026)
## SUB 4 | Crop Loan Schemes – Zero Interest, Pavala Vaddi, KCC
[123:4:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Provision
  [123:4:0.0] Vaddi Leni Runalu / YSR Zero Interest Crop Loan (payout 20 Apr 2021) | Crop loans up to Rs 1 lakh repaid on time; State pays interest
  [123:4:0.1] Pavala Vaddi (farmers) | Crop loans above Rs 1 lakh and up to Rs 3 lakh at 3% ("quarter-rupee" interest) for prompt repayers
  [123:4:0.2] Modified Interest Subvention Scheme (GoI) | Up to Rs 3 lakh at 4% for prompt repayers
  [123:4:0.3] KCC (RBI Directions, 19 Jun 2026) | Loans from 1 Jan 2027; max tenure 6 years
[123:4:1] - India farm credit 2024-25: Rs 28.69 lakh crore vs target Rs 27.50 lakh crore = **104%**.
[123:4:2] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy Static-1 01 p.15 (Pavala Vaddi 2004); app notes 3:123 sub 5 (as of 2026)
## SUB 5 | Credit Planning – PLP, State Focus Paper, SLBC
[123:5:0] - Chain: district **Potential Linked Credit Plans (PLPs)**, prepared by **NABARD** → NABARD **State Focus Paper** → **SLBC Annual Credit Plan**.
[123:5:1] TABLE head: SFP 2026-27 (5 May 2026) | Amount
  [123:5:1.0] Total credit potential | Rs 5.11 lakh crore (about 20% above Rs 4.24 lakh crore)
  [123:5:1.1] Agriculture and allied | Rs 2.55 lakh crore
  [123:5:1.2] of which crop loans | Rs 1.66 lakh crore
[123:5:2] - **2022-23** AP credit plan (NABARD, March 2022): agricultural credit raised by **10%**.
[123:5:3] - **ACP 2025-26:** Rs 2.96 lakh crore to agriculture + allied; Rs 1,490 crore to tenant farmers. **236th SLBC** (16 Sep 2026): MSME lending below expectations; lend for **PM Surya Ghar**.
[123:5:4] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:123 sub 3 (as of 2026)

=== NOTES 3:124 | Agricultural Marketing in Andhra Pradesh – Market Yards, Rythu Bazaars and E-marketing
## SUB 0 | Rythu Bazars
[124:0:0] - **Concept:** **direct marketing between farmers and consumers**, no middlemen → **better price to farmers and fair price to consumers**.
[124:0:1] - **Started 1999**; first Rythu Bazars **functioning from January 1999**.
[124:0:2] - **Ryot Bazar idea first in Punjab (Apni Mandi)**; **AP was the second** State.
[124:0:3] - Run day-to-day by an **Estate Officer**; district coordinating officer = **Joint Collector** (not the District Collector); outside the purview of Agricultural Market Committees.
[124:0:4] - **Pricing:** about **25% above wholesale** and **25% below local retail** prices.
[124:0:5] - **Number:** 124 functioning, 28 under construction (2024-25); 129 (2025-26).
[124:0:6] - **Price intervention:** the State's quickest channel – government procures **perishables** through them when prices swing.
[124:0:7] TABLE head: Feature | Regulated market yard | Rythu Bazar
  [124:0:7.0] Basis | AP (AP&L) Markets Act, 1966 | Executive scheme, 1999
  [124:0:7.1] Managed by | Agricultural Market Committee | Estate Officer
  [124:0:7.2] Seller | Farmers sell to licensed traders | Farmer sells directly to consumer
[124:0:8] > Trap (APPSC): "District Collector is the coordinating officer" is the wrong statement – it is the Joint Collector.
[124:0:9] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.95 (Rythu Bazars 124, perishables); OnlyIAS NCERT Economy 05 p.21 (direct farmer-to-consumer); app notes 3:124 sub 3
[124:0:10] - **Department:** Rythu Bazars come under the **Revenue Department** (Estate Officer; Joint Collector coordinates).
## SUB 1 | Market Yards, Market Committees and the 1966 Act
[124:1:0] - Farmers selling alone face **bargaining imbalance**: forced sales, false weights, unrecorded prices, middlemen margins.
[124:1:1] TABLE head: Act / rule | Year | Provision
  [124:1:1.0] AP (Agricultural Produce and Livestock) Markets Act | 1966 | Act No. 16 of 1966; crops, livestock and products
  [124:1:1.1] AP (AP&L) Markets Rules | 1969 | Under Section 33 of the Act; licensing
  [124:1:1.2] Three central farm laws | 2020 | Repealed Nov 2021 → 1966 framework remains operative
  [124:1:1.3] Model APMC Act | 2003 | Single market fee, registration in place of licensing
[124:1:2] - **1966 Act mechanisms:** open auction, standard weights, recorded prices, **licensed buyers**.
[124:1:3] - **Setting up:** notified area → Market Committee → notified market (yard). District supervision: **DATMO**.
[124:1:4] - **Market fee: 1%**; Rs 433.39 crore (2024-25 to Dec 2024). AMCs pay **25%** of income (10% before **Oct 2015**) into the **Central Market Fund**.
[124:1:5] - **Adoni** = second largest market yard; **Guntur Mirchi Yard** = Asia's largest chilli market; **Agri Export Zone for red chillies** in Guntur.
[124:1:6] - **Pulpwood trading (Feb 2016):** **Subabul, Eucalyptus and Casuarina** sold to paper mills through market committees – **not bamboo**.
[124:1:7] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.95 (market fee); app notes 3:124 sub 1-2
## SUB 2 | Cooperative Marketing – MARKFED and Price Stabilisation
[124:2:0] - **AP MARKFED** (AP State Cooperative Marketing Federation Ltd) = **apex organisation of the Cooperative Marketing Societies**; supplies inputs, cattle feed, fertilisers; works through RSKs and PACS.
[124:2:1] - **13 DCMSs** procure produce at minimum administered prices, also on behalf of MARKFED.
[124:2:2] - **Price Stabilisation Fund:** **Rs 3,000 crore** (2019-20); **MARKFED** played the key role; protects MSP for **turmeric, minor millets, sweet orange, banana, onion**; 2019 – groundnut farmers of 7 districts compensated.
[124:2:3] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.64-65; app notes 3:124 sub 5
[124:2:4] - **Purpose of the PSF:** intervention in **agricultural and horticultural crops** to secure farmers a just price.
## SUB 3 | Pledge Loans, Storage and Cold Storage
[124:3:0] - **Rythu Bandhu Pathakam** (Raitu Bandhu): **pledge loan** scheme – farmer stores produce in **market-committee godowns** and borrows against it (hypothecation); avoids distress sale.
[124:3:1] - **Capital Intensive Subsidy Scheme for cold storages:** introduced **July 1999**.
[124:3:2] - **APSWC:** set up **August 1958**; share capital **50:50 GoAP and CWC**.
[124:3:3] TABLE head: Market committee godowns | 2024-25 | 2025-26
  [124:3:3.0] Total | 1,052; 9.65 lakh MT | 1,052
  [124:3:3.1] Operational | 626 | 643
[124:3:4] - Gap: limited operational storage, need for **cold storage**. **AIF**: farm-gate infrastructure at **RBKs/RSKs**.
[124:3:5] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP Economy Static-1 01 p.14 (cold storage July 1999); app notes 3:124 sub 6
## SUB 4 | e-Marketing – e-NAM and eFarmarket
[124:4:0] - **e-NAM:** 1,656 mandis in 23 States and 4 UTs; **AP 33 markets**; each AP mandi got free software + **Rs 30 lakh**; 39-52 FPOs registered.
[124:4:1] - **Mostly intra-mandi:** most e-NAM trades are within one mandi; AP: only **11 mandis** in inter-mandi trade (Rs 13.98 crore, Dec 2024). Helped **price discovery**, not yet payments or inter-mandi trade.
[124:4:2] - **Model APLM Act 2017:** makes the **whole State a single market**, private markets, e-trading, unified licence – i.e. pushes **inter-mandi and inter-State** trade, not intra-mandi.
[124:4:3] - **eFarmarket:** AP = **first State** with an e-marketing platform linking its farmers to traders across India (APFEVCL SPV, 2021). **CM APP** (Continuous Monitoring of Agricultural Prices and Procurement): farm-gate prices collected **daily from every RBK** – AP the only State.
[124:4:4] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.95 (e-NAM in AP); bureaucratsindia.in (eFarmarket, web search); business-standard.com and enam.gov.in (intra-mandi, APLM Act, web search); app notes 3:124 sub 4
## SUB 5 | FPOs and Collective Marketing
[124:5:0] - **Central Sector Scheme for 10,000 FPOs** (budget Rs 6,860 crore); implementing agencies **SFAC, NABARD, NCDC, NAFED**; AP: 455 FPOs registered.
[124:5:1] - **Farmer Producer Companies** get financial assistance from **NABARD and SFAC** (equity grant, credit guarantee).
[124:5:2] TABLE head: Problem of the farmer | Channel
  [124:5:2.0] Middlemen margin on vegetables | Rythu Bazar
  [124:5:2.1] False weights, unrecorded prices | Regulated market yard
  [124:5:2.2] Forced sale after harvest | Rythu Bandhu pledge loan
  [124:5:2.3] Price crash | Price Stabilisation Fund
  [124:5:2.4] Small, scattered lots | FPOs
  [124:5:2.5] Limited buyers in one mandi | e-NAM
[124:5:3] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.62 (10,000 FPOs, SFAC/NABARD/NCDC/NAFED); app notes 3:124 sub 7

=== NOTES 3:125 | Approaches and Initiatives in Ap Industry – Industrial Policy, Single Desk, Msmes, Industrial Nodes
## SUB 0 | Industrial Development Policies – Editions, Targets and Incentives
[125:0:0] # Editions → period
[125:0:1] TABLE head: Policy | Period | What is asked
  [125:0:1.0] Industrial Development Policy (IDP) | 2015-20 | With Single Desk Policy, MSME Policy 2015-20 and sector policies (aerospace, textiles, food)
  [125:0:1.1] IDP | 2020-23 | 10 thrust areas; micro unit = plant and machinery ≤ Rs 1 crore, turnover ≤ Rs 5 crore; mega = 2,000 jobs; 30 skill development centres; 2 skill universities (one for AI, robotics, VR, Industry 4.0); immersion programmes with international agencies; not applicable within Vijayawada Municipal Corporation limits
  [125:0:1.2] IDP | 2023-27 | In force 1 April 2023 – 31 March 2027; aims to make AP an "unchallenged" global investment destination; 33% of land in new industrial parks for MSMEs
  [125:0:1.3] IDP (4.0) | 2024-29 | Notified 26 Oct 2024, G.O.Ms.68; flagship for Sub-Large, Large, Mega, Ultra-Mega units
[125:0:2] - **IDP 2020-23 incentives:** **SGST reimbursement for medium, large and mega units for 5 years, linked to employment**; **16.2%** of APIIC plots for **SC** entrepreneurs; APIIC 25% land rebate capped at **Rs 20 lakh**.
[125:0:3] - **IDP 2023-27 aims (G1 2024):** a **minimum 30%** (not 25%) industry share in GSDP; port-led growth; APIIC land allotment and fast-track clearances in **21 days** (not 2 weeks); Single Desk Portal and **YSR AP One** for end-to-end support → statements I, II and V of the 2024 question are wrong.
[125:0:4] - **The (4.0) 2024-29 suite (all 26 Oct 2024):** IDP (4.0) **G.O.68**, MSME & Entrepreneur Development (4.0) **G.O.69**, Electronics Manufacturing (4.0) **G.O.70**, Food Processing (4.0) **G.O.71**; also Private Industrial Parks ("Plug and Play"), Sustainable Electric Mobility, Textile & Garments 2024-29, IT & GCC Policy (4.0).
[125:0:5] # IDP (4.0) targets and sectors
[125:0:6] TABLE head: Target (2024-29) | Value
  [125:0:6.0] Manufacturing GVA | Rs 3.4 → 7.3 lakh crore
  [125:0:6.1] Investment | Rs 30 lakh crore
  [125:0:6.2] FDI | At least USD 10 billion
  [125:0:6.3] Jobs | 5 lakh first-time manufacturing jobs
  [125:0:6.4] Industrial parks | 175+
  [125:0:6.5] Exports | USD 20 → 40 bn
[125:0:7] - **Motto:** **cut the cost of production and raise the Speed of Doing Business**.
[125:0:8] - **10 Sustenance** sectors (automobile, engineering, chemicals, petrochemicals, food processing, pharma, textiles, metals …) and **10 Propelling** sectors (aerospace-defence-drones, EV and battery, toys, medical devices, **blue economy**, furniture, biotech, green energy, semiconductors, ports-logistics). Textiles = sustenance, not propelling.
[125:0:9] - **P4** = Public-Private-People Partnership; "**One Family, One Entrepreneur**" by **2030**.
[125:0:10] - **Investment Facilitation Cell:** liaison officers, handholding, approval tracking, resolution of delays.
[125:0:11] # IDP (4.0) categories and incentives (FCI = **Fixed** Capital Investment, land excluded)
[125:0:12] TABLE head: Category | Threshold | Investment subsidy
  [125:0:12.0] Sub-Large | Rs 51-500 crore | 12% (+5% women/SC/ST/BC/specially-abled); 5 instalments
  [125:0:12.1] Large | Rs 501-1,000 crore | Up to 12% (6% if only 50% capacity installed); 7 instalments
  [125:0:12.2] Mega | Rs 1,001-5,000 crore or 2,000+ jobs | Up to 15%, tailor-made
  [125:0:12.3] Ultra-Mega | Above Rs 5,000 crore | Up to 15%, tailor-made
[125:0:13] - **Early Bird:** **30%** for the first **200** projects with CFE in 18 months; **40%** for PLI sectors with CFE in 24 months.
[125:0:14] TABLE head: Standard incentive | Rate
  [125:0:14.0] Net SGST | 100% for 5 years from commercial production
  [125:0:14.1] Stamp and transfer duty | 100% reimbursement on purchase/lease of land
  [125:0:14.2] Land-conversion fee | 100% reimbursement
  [125:0:14.3] Fixed power cost | Rs 1 per unit for 2 years
  [125:0:14.4] De-carbonisation | Up to 6% of FCI
  [125:0:14.5] PLI top-up | 10% of GoI PLI, cap 5% of FCI
  [125:0:14.6] Local procurement | 1% of export turnover, 3 years
  [125:0:14.7] Employment Creation Subsidy | Up to 10% of FCI (more than 5 workers)
[125:0:15] - **Not a standard incentive (G2 2025):** "15% capital subsidy on **Foreign** capital investment".
[125:0:16] - **Escrow Guarantee** = promised subsidies paid on time. **Pavala vaddi:** State repays interest above **3%** a year → unit pays **25 paise per Rs 100 per month**.
[125:0:17] - **Local jobs:** AP Employment of Local Candidates in Industries/Factories Act, **2019** – **75%** for locals.
[125:0:18] > Number traps: mega = 2,000 jobs; SGST 5 years; 30 skill centres; 16.2% SC plots; 33% MSME land; IDP 2023-27 ends 2027; (4.0) = 2024-29.
[125:0:19] ~~ Sources: mondaq.com/pdf/1358986.pdf (Nexdigm summary of IDP 2023-27: 1 Apr 2023–31 Mar 2027, minimum 30% industry share, 21-day land allotment and clearances); AP SES 2024-25 p.103-105 (IDP 4.0 incentives, Single Desk); app notes 3:125 sub 1, 2
## SUB 1 | Single Desk Portal, APIIC, APEDB and Other Promotion Bodies
[125:1:0] # Single window → Single Desk
[125:1:1] TABLE head: Item | Year | Provision
  [125:1:1.0] AP Industrial Single Window Clearance Act | 2002 | Statutory backing; deemed approval if a department misses its deadline
  [125:1:1.1] Single Desk Portal (SDP) | April 2015 | Under the Single Desk Policy 2015-20; one-stop G2B platform for clearing investment proposals
  [125:1:1.2] AP Omnibus (Speed of Doing Business) Bill | 2026 | Amends the 2002 Act; passed Aug 2026
[125:1:2] - **SDP:** **93+ clearances** from **19 departments**; **pre-establishment and pre-operation approvals within 21 days**; online application, payment, tracking, certificate – **no physical visits** to regulators. Since 2015: 1,20,634 applications, 1,17,954 approved (to 1 Jan 2025).
[125:1:3] - **Single Desk integrates all departments on one platform** → investors need not approach each department (A and R true, R explains A).
[125:1:4] - **National:** **e-Biz** portal (Ministry of Commerce, **28 Jan 2013**) = **single window for approach to government services** (G2B). PARIVESH 3.0 = environmental clearances; E-Samudra = maritime administration.
[125:1:5] - BRAP (DPIIT) reform areas include the **single window system**.
[125:1:6] # Bodies
[125:1:7] TABLE head: Body | Fact
  [125:1:7.0] APIIC (AP Industrial Infrastructure Corporation) | Incorporated 26 Sept 1973, authorised capital Rs 20 crore; develops industrial parks and allots land to industry; land bank 41,998 acres; may allot 50 acres without Cabinet (Jun 2026); SIA for MSE-CDP
  [125:1:7.1] APIDC / APSFC | Finance industry (not land allotment)
  [125:1:7.2] SIPB | Chaired by the CM; final say on mega projects
  [125:1:7.3] SIPC | Chief Secretary; screens proposals
  [125:1:7.4] YSR AP One | One-stop resource and support centre for industries/investors
  [125:1:7.5] DICs | District single window; implement the MSMED Act
  [125:1:7.6] AP MSMEDC (Mangalagiri) | World Bank RAMP (2022-23 to 2026-27)
  [125:1:7.7] NSIC | SRI Fund Rs 50,000 crore
[125:1:8] # AP Economic Development Board (APEDB)
[125:1:9] - **APEDB Act, 2018**; opened **APEX Korea (Busan)** – first overseas facilitation centre; signed MoU with the **JSW Group**.
[125:1:10] TABLE head: Division | Function
  [125:1:10.0] External Engagement Division (EED) | Engages Governments, multilateral and bilateral agencies, development banks, trade associations; promotes AP abroad
  [125:1:10.1] Resource Mobilisation Division (RMD) | Administers the Investment Facilitation, Innovation, Viability Gap, Project Development and Capacity Development Funds (no "Capacity investment project fund")
  [125:1:10.2] Strategic Planning and Policy Division (SPPD) | Analyses national and global development trends for a high-growth ecosystem

## SUB 2 | AP Industrial Base, Investments and Rankings
[125:2:0] # Profile
[125:2:1] - **Endowments:** coastline **1,053.07 km – 3rd longest** (after Gujarat and Tamil Nadu; revised Dec 2025, earlier "974 km, 2nd"); **six operational ports**, minerals, farm base, surplus power. A national surplus of coal is **not** an AP strength.
[125:2:2] - **Manufacturing ≈ 11% of GSDP** (APSES 2024-25); manufacturing share **22.11% in 1987-88** (combined AP); large and medium industries grew **18.83%** a year in 1957-2006.
[125:2:3] - **Rank:** AP **7th in industrial output** (RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States 2020-21).
[125:2:4] - **Corporate investment (RBI study, Sept 2011, for 2010-11):** **Chhattisgarh 1st, AP 2nd**, Gujarat 3rd.
[125:2:5] - **Sunrise sector** identified by AP: **agro-food processing**.
[125:2:6] - After bifurcation the MSME profile shifted to **capital-intensive** units. APEDB: AP = **5.8%** of India's exports (2020-21).
[125:2:7] - National frame (2026): IIP base **2022-23**; core index **nine** industries (**iron ore** added), weight **32.88%**.
[125:2:8] TABLE head: District | Main sectors
  [125:2:8.0] Srikakulam | Agro and food processing, chemicals, petrochemicals
  [125:2:8.1] East Godavari | Chemicals, petrochemicals
  [125:2:8.2] Guntur | Food processing, cement
  [125:2:8.3] Prakasam / Kurnool | Mineral-based industries
  [125:2:8.4] Nellore | Leather, electronics, auto components
  [125:2:8.5] Kadapa / Anantapur | Textiles (Anantapur also electronics)
[125:2:9] # Major units
[125:2:10] - **Visakhapatnam Steel Plant (RINL):** India's **first shore-based** integrated steel plant; **USSR** collaboration; dedicated **1 August 1992**.
[125:2:11] - **Pharma:** Jawaharlal Nehru Pharma City, **Parawada** (Anakapalli) – bulk drugs/APIs. **Cement:** **26** operational plants (2014-15), in **YSR Kadapa, Kurnool, Guntur**.
[125:2:12] - **Minerals:** **Mangampet** (YSR Kadapa) = world's single largest **barytes** deposit, mined by **APMDC**; Aug 2026: NGRI found **lithium and gallium** at **Tummalapalle**.
[125:2:13] - **ONGC U-field Onshore Deepwater Block** (KG basin): **Rs 2,917 crore**, dedicated by PM Modi in **Nov 2022**.
[125:2:14] - **Employment in large and mega projects:** highest in **Visakhapatnam** district (G2 2017).
[125:2:15] # Investments and announcements

## SUB 3 | MSME Policy 2015-20, Sectoral Policies and MSME (4.0)
[125:3:0] # MSME Policy 2015-20
[125:3:1] - Targets: new jobs for **at least 3.5 lakh people** and new investment of **at least Rs 15,000 crore by 2020**; MSME Revival Fund **Rs 100 crore** (2015-16).
[125:3:2] - **25% rebate in land cost, limited to Rs 10 lakh**, in industrial estates/parks.
[125:3:3] - AP Budget 2017-18: **Rs 126 crore** for rejuvenation of MSME units.
[125:3:4] - **M-Parks Policy:** **200 MSME parks by 2023**, about **30,000 MSMEs**, **Rs 45,000 crore** investment.
[125:3:5] # Sectoral policies
[125:3:6] - **Aerospace and Defence Manufacturing Policy 2015-20:** at least **5,000 highly skilled jobs** and **Rs 20,000 crore** investment by 2020, plus 4 anchor units – two separate targets (A and R both true, R does not explain A).
[125:3:7] - **Textile, Apparel and Garments Policy 2018-23:** capital subsidy caps – **100% of FCI** (garmenting, made-ups, integrated, processing, technical textiles), **75%** (weaving, knitting), **50%** (spinning, ginning) → "all of the above"; policy never got operational guidelines and was replaced by (4.0) 2024-29.
[125:3:8] - **Textile (4.0) 2024-29:** 2 lakh+ jobs. **Food Processing (4.0):** Large Rs 50-100 crore, **Mega above Rs 100 crore**. **Electronics (4.0):** clusters at Kopparthy, Tirupati, **Sri City**. **Tourism (Mar 2026):** 5-10% FCI subsidy up to Rs 40 crore.
[125:3:9] # MSME & Entrepreneur Development Policy (4.0) 2024-29
[125:3:10] - **G.O.Ms.69**; target **Rs 50,000 crore**; **175+ MSME parks – one per assembly constituency**; **Rs 500 crore** corpus.
[125:3:11] TABLE head: Capital subsidy | General | Special (women, SC/ST/BC, minority, PwD)
  [125:3:11.0] Micro | 25%, cap Rs 25 lakh | 45%, cap Rs 45 lakh
  [125:3:11.1] Small | 25%, cap Rs 1.5 cr | 45%, cap Rs 4.5 cr
  [125:3:11.2] Medium | 25%, cap Rs 7 cr | 35%, cap Rs 7 cr
[125:3:12] - **Profile (IDP 4.0 baseline):** about **9 lakh** Udyam MSMEs (Feb 2024; **3.23%** of India); **79% services**; MSMEs ≈ **33.5% of GSDP**. "**28** exclusive MSME parks" is **not** the policy figure.
[125:3:13] TABLE head: MSME limits (investment / turnover) | 2020 | From 1 April 2025
  [125:3:13.0] Micro | Rs 1 cr / Rs 5 cr | Rs 2.5 cr / Rs 10 cr
  [125:3:13.1] Small | Rs 10 cr / Rs 50 cr | Rs 25 cr / Rs 100 cr
  [125:3:13.2] Medium | Rs 50 cr / Rs 250 cr | Rs 125 cr / Rs 500 cr
[125:3:14] - **India:** MSMEs ≈ **30% of GDP, 46% of exports**; women own about **20%**; Stand Up India and MUDRA (Tarun) for women entrepreneurs.
[125:3:15] - **Credit and relief:** **APIIC-Union Bank** MoU (**July 2022**) – loans in **39 industrial parks**; **ReSTART (May 2020)** – COVID relief, mainly cleared **incentive arrears**; **MSME Samadhaan** – delayed payments, **45-day** rule.
[125:3:16] - **Clusters:** MSE-CDP (SIA = APIIC); AP Cluster Development Programme 2026-30 – **45 clusters**, up to **80%** of cost. **ODOP:** **99** products in 26 districts (Raja Rani dolls – Annamayya).
[125:3:17] - **Large and mega units (APSES 2024-25):** **1,074** operational, Rs 3.01 lakh crore, **5.33 lakh** jobs; SIPB FY25: **8** projects, Rs 2,45,275 crore. Jun 2026: Cabinet ratified 87 SIPB projects, **Rs 34,000 crore**.
[125:3:18] ~~ Sources: AP MSME Policy 2015-20 (static.investindia.gov.in "MSME Policy (2015-20).pdf", p.1, p.7: 3.5 lakh jobs, Rs 15,000 crore, 25% land rebate up to Rs 10 lakh); AP Aerospace & Defence Manufacturing Policy 2015-20 (static.investindia.gov.in, p.3, p.8); AP SES 2024-25 p.103-105; app notes 3:125 sub 5, 6; M-Parks, textile caps and 2017-18 budget: general knowledge, not checked

=== OWN MCQs of 3:125 (162)
[n710852df951] sub0

=== NOTES 3:126 | Social Sector Initiatives in Ap – Education, Health, Nutrition and Their Outcome Indicators
## SUB 0 | Education Schemes – Amma Vodi, Thalliki Vandanam, Nadu-Nedu and Others
[126:0:0] # Mother-aid schemes
[126:0:1] TABLE head: Scheme | Fact
  [126:0:1.0] Jagananna Amma Vodi (January 2020) | Rs 15,000 a year to poor mothers (or guardians) of school- and college-going children; an education scheme of Andhra Pradesh (one child per mother)
  [126:0:1.1] Thalliki Vandanam (2025-26, Super Six; replaced Amma Vodi) | Rs 15,000 a year for every school-going child (Classes I-XII, govt and private), paid to the mother; Rs 13,000 by DBT + Rs 2,000 for school upkeep
[126:0:2] # School infrastructure and learning
[126:0:3] - **Mana Badi Nadu-Nedu** (launched **November 2019**): **school education** – modernising government school infrastructure to make government schools **competitive institutions**; not Amma Vodi.
[126:0:4] - **Vidya Kanuka** (2020): free kit (uniforms, shoes, bag, books, dictionary) for Class 1-10. **Badikosta** (2017): **bicycles** to Class IX girls. **Akshara Andhra:** adult literacy. **Andhra Model of Education:** 100% enrolment. **TaRL:** language 21% → 47%, maths 20% → 50%. **LEAP:** responds to **ASER** learning gaps. **Jagananna Gorumudda:** enriched mid-day meal under **PM POSHAN**.
[126:0:5] - **Happy Classroom:** **Isha Foundation**, slow learners, about **3,000** schools of **Chittoor**.
[126:0:6] - **Zero-enrolment schools (U-DISE):** about **1,000**.
[126:0:7] # Higher education and regulation
[126:0:8] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [126:0:8.0] AP State Council of Higher Education (APSCHE) | Convenes the meetings of Vice-Chancellors; coordinates universities
  [126:0:8.1] Jawahar Knowledge Centres (2006) | Employability-enhancement platform in degree colleges
  [126:0:8.2] AP School Education Regulatory and Monitoring Commission Act, 2019 | Published in English, Telugu and Urdu; regulates private-school fees
  [126:0:8.3] Dr Abdul Haq Urdu University | Kurnool (2016)

## SUB 1 | Health Insurance and Health Schemes
[126:1:0] # Aarogyasri → Dr NTR Vaidya Seva
[126:1:1] - **Origin:** **Rajiv Aarogyasri** (**April 2007**, BPL families) → **Dr YSR Aarogyasri** → **Dr NTR Vaidya Seva**.
[126:1:2] - **2014-19 (NTR Vaidya Seva):** cover **up to Rs 2.5 lakh per BPL family** on a **floater** basis.
[126:1:3] - **Now:** **BPL families and families with income up to Rs 5 lakh**; cover **up to Rs 25 lakh per family per year**, **no limit for cancer**; **3,257 procedures**; **1.43 crore** families benefited. Budget 2026-27: Rs 4,000 crore to the Trust.
[126:1:4] - **Aarogya Raksha** (**7 April 2017**): for families **above the poverty line** (outside Aarogyasri), premium **Rs 1,200** per member a year, cover Rs 2 lakh.
[126:1:5] - **YSR Aarogya Aasara:** **post-operative sustenance allowance** during recovery.
[126:1:6] - **Universal Health Policy (2026):** up to **Rs 2.5 lakh per citizen** through insurers.
[126:1:7] # Scheme → domain
[126:1:8] TABLE head: Scheme | Domain
  [126:1:8.0] YSR Nethanna Nestham | Handlooms and textiles (weavers)
  [126:1:8.1] Nadu-Nedu | School education
  [126:1:8.2] Dr YSR Kanti Velugu | Health (eye screening)
  [126:1:8.3] Anna Sanjeevini | Branded generic medicines at affordable prices
  [126:1:8.4] Swasthya Vidya Vahini | Medical students sent to villages to gather health data and spread health awareness
  [126:1:8.5] 104 programme | Primary health care by Medical Officers at village level (mobile medical units); 108 = emergency ambulance
[126:1:9] > G1 2024: telemedicine in all AB-HWCs ✓; Family Doctor as a **standalone** project ✗ (it works through PHC doctors and Village Health Clinics); Aarogya Aasara ✓; the "income limit Rs 2.5 lakh → Rs 5 lakh" statement is disputed (Rs 2.5 lakh was the earlier **cover**, eligibility was BPL).
[126:1:10] ~~ Sources: AP SES 2024-25 pdf p.225 (Vaidya Seva Rs 5 lakh income limit, Rs 25 lakh cover, 3,257 procedures), p.227 (Aarogya Raksha for APL, 7 Apr 2017); app notes 3:126 sub 1; NTR Vaidya Seva 2.5 lakh, Swasthya Vidya Vahini, Anna Sanjeevini: general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 2 | Health Facilities, Services and Spending
[126:2:0] # Network
[126:2:1] TABLE head: Facility | Number
  [126:2:1.0] Village Health Clinics (village secretariat level) | 10,032
  [126:2:1.1] Rural PHCs / urban PHCs | 1,293 / 559
  [126:2:1.2] CHCs | 173
  [126:2:1.3] Area hospitals | 54
[126:2:2] - **Family Physician / Family Doctor Programme:** **6 April 2023**; PHC Medical Officers visit Village Health Clinics every 15 days – integrated with PHCs. Telemedicine runs in AB-HWCs.
[126:2:3] - **New medical colleges (Sept 2023):** **Vizianagaram, Eluru, Machilipatnam, Rajamahendravaram, Nandyal** – first of **17**; **Pulivendula and Adoni later**.
[126:2:4] - **ESI:** **seven** new ESI hospitals approved **Feb 2021**, incl. a **100-bed** hospital at **Vizianagaram**.
[126:2:5] - **Palasa** (Srikakulam): Kidney Research Centre for the **Uddanam** belt. **Talli Bidda Express:** free drop-home for mothers and newborns.
[126:2:6] - **Doctors (2001-02, combined AP):** highest share after Hyderabad – **Visakhapatnam**.
[126:2:7] - **Digital health:** ABDM (2021, **Ministry of Health and Family Welfare**); AP **tops** in ABHA coverage; **Sanjeevani** statewide from Aug 2026 – digital health profile + **ABHA ID**.
[126:2:8] # Spending
[126:2:9] - **Health 2021-22:** **Rs 13,830.44 crore**, up **21.1%**. Feb 2026 budget: health up **9.5%**.
[126:2:10] - **Budget 2026-27:** total **Rs 3,32,205 crore**; **School Education Rs 32,308 crore = largest**; education **11.7%** (all-States average 14.5%); Health **Rs 19,306 crore = 4th**; debt service **18 paise** of each rupee.

[126:2:13] - **ABHA accounts:** about **5.04 crore** generated in AP.

=== NOTES 3:127 | Urbanisation and Smart Cities in Ap – Amaravati Capital Region, Visakhapatnam, Kakinada, Tirupati
## SUB 0 | Amaravati Capital – APCRDA, Foundation and Building
[127:0:0] # APCRDA and the capital city
[127:0:1] - **APCRDA** = AP Capital Region Development Authority, under the **APCRDA Act, 2014** (authority set up **2014**); HQ **Rayapudi**.
[127:0:2] - **Amaravati** = a **greenfield capital** on the **Krishna**, between Vijayawada and Guntur, in **Thullur** mandal; "India's first smart capital"; **water-centric**, 51% green/blue space.
[127:0:3] TABLE head: Unit | Area
  [127:0:3.0] Capital city (notified) | 217.23 sq km, 29 villages
  [127:0:3.1] Capital region | 8,352.69 sq km (expanded Aug 2026, from 7,068.20)
[127:0:4] - **Master plan:** **Surbana Jurong (Singapore)**. **Amaravati Development Partner** = Singapore consortium (**Ascendas-Singbridge, Sembcorp**) with **Amaravati Development Corporation** – **no Japanese** partner.
[127:0:5] - **Land pooling:** farmers sent to **Singapore** to learn best practice; Phase I **93.1% of 37,921 acres**, **70,550** returnable plots; LPS 2.0 about 16,666 acres in 7 villages (Apr 2026); **6,212.50 acres** for the **Inner Ring Road** (Sep 2026).
[127:0:6] - **"My Brick – My Amaravati"** (2015): donors bought e-bricks at **Rs 10 per brick**.
[127:0:7] - **Interim Legislative building** (Velagapudi): completed in **192 days** (AP Budget 2017-18).
[127:0:8] - **Justice City:** India's first, planned in Amaravati (High Court, judicial academy, legal institutions).
[127:0:9] - **Works and funds:** 96 works, ₹57,592.71 crore; World Bank + ADB ₹15,000 crore; HUDCO ₹11,000 crore; Amaravati Sangamam at Sakhamuru.
[127:0:10] # Timeline
[127:0:11] TABLE head: Event | Date
  [127:0:11.0] Foundation stone | 22 October 2015, PM Narendra Modi, Uddandarayunipalem
  [127:0:11.1] APCRDA (Repeal) Bill, 2020 + AP Decentralisation and Inclusive Development Bill, 2020 passed by Assembly | 20 January 2020
  [127:0:11.2] Repeal of both 2020 Acts; APCRDA Act revived | 22 Nov 2021
  [127:0:11.3] AP High Court upholds Amaravati | 3 March 2022
  [127:0:11.4] "Restart Amaravati" relaunch | 2 May 2025
  [127:0:11.5] AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2026 – sole capital (Section 5) | effective from 2 June 2024
[127:0:12] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:127 sub 1, 2, 4; brick price, 192 days, ADP partners and Repeal Bill date: general knowledge, not checked
## SUB 1 | Three-capitals Proposal (2020)
[127:1:0] TABLE head: Seat | City
  [127:1:0.0] Executive | Visakhapatnam
  [127:1:0.1] Legislative | Amaravati
  [127:1:0.2] Judicial | Kurnool
[127:1:1] - Laws: **AP Decentralisation and Inclusive Development Bill, 2020** and **AP Capital Region Development Authority (Repeal) Bill, 2020**; basis: **G.N. Rao Committee** and **Boston Consulting Group** study.
[127:1:2] - South African model: **Pretoria** administrative, **Cape Town** legislative, **Bloemfontein** judicial.
[127:1:3] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:127 sub 3
## SUB 2 | Swachh Survekshan, Ease of Living and City Rankings
[127:2:0] TABLE head: Survey | AP result
  [127:2:0.0] Swachh Survekshan 2017 | Visakhapatnam 3rd in India – AP's cleanest city
  [127:2:0.1] Swachh Survekshan 2022 | Visakhapatnam = 'Top Impact Creator' (million-plus) and clean big city (10-40 lakh); Tirupati MC = 1st in 'Safaimitra Suraksha Shehar'; Vijayawada MC = 'cleanest capital'; Pulivendula – innovation, 50,000-1 lakh; Salur – innovation, 25,000-50,000
  [127:2:0.2] Swachh Survekshan 2023 | Visakhapatnam 4th (AP's cleanest)
  [127:2:0.3] Swachh Survekshan 2024-25 | Super Swachh League: Vijayawada (10 lakh+), Guntur, Tirupati; Rajahmundry = Promising Swachh Shehar
  [127:2:0.4] Ease of Living Index 2018 (111 cities) | AP cities: Vijayawada, Kakinada, Tirupati, Visakhapatnam, Amaravati (not Nellore; Warangal is Telangana); Tirupati 4th
  [127:2:0.5] Ease of Living 2020 / MPI 2020 | Kakinada 4th (below 1 million) / Tirupati 2nd
  [127:2:0.6] Ease of Living (AMRUT State ranking, 2018) | AP 1st
[127:2:1] > G1 2023 (SS 2022): I, II and V true; III and IV swap the towns – **Pulivendula** = 50,000-1 lakh, **Salur** = 25,000-50,000.
[127:2:2] ~~ Sources: telanganatoday.com "Andhra Pradesh bags six awards in Swachh Survekshan 2022" (Vijayawada cleanest capital, Tirupati Safaimitra, Pulivendula and Salur categories); app notes 3:127 sub 8
## SUB 3 | Urban Bodies, Smart Cities and Urban Schemes
[127:3:0] # Development authorities and corporations
[127:3:1] TABLE head: Body | Fact
  [127:3:1.0] VUDA → VMRDA | Visakhapatnam (Urban → Metropolitan Region) Development Authority
  [127:3:1.1] TUDA | Tirupati Urban Development Authority
  [127:3:1.2] GUDA (2017) | Rajamahendravaram–Kakinada
  [127:3:1.3] Special Development Authority (2011) | For the petroleum sector (PCPIR, Visakhapatnam-Kakinada)
  [127:3:1.4] AP Urban Greening and Beautification Corporation (2015) | Greening and beautifying cities under the Swachh Andhra mission
[127:3:2] - **1996 urban reform:** urban land used as a resource by **streamlining the levy of development charges**.
[127:3:3] - **CLAP** = **Clean Andhra Pradesh** (Jagananna Swachh Sankalpam); "OWMS – Online water management services" is not the expansion.
[127:3:4] # Smart Cities Mission
[127:3:5] - AP's **4 smart cities**: **Visakhapatnam, Kakinada** (first round, Jan 2016), **Tirupati**, **Amaravati** (third round, June 2017); not Vijayawada or Guntur.
[127:3:6] - AP Budget **2022-23: Rs 1,000 crore** for the Smart Cities Mission.
[127:3:7] # Urbanisation and schemes
[127:3:8] - **Census 2011** (residuary AP): urban **29.5%** (India 31.2%); most urbanised district **Visakhapatnam**, then Krishna; largest UA **Visakhapatnam**.
[127:3:9] - **AMRUT** (water, sewerage): 1.0 – 174 projects; **2.0 – 506 projects**. PMAY-U 2.0 = pucca houses. YSR Jagananna Colonies under **Navaratnalu – Pedalandariki Illu**.
[127:3:10] - **City Economic Regions (2026):** three – Visakhapatnam (10 districts, NITI Aayog pilot, US$750-800 bn by 2047), Amaravati, Tirupati. Criticism: land-pooling farmers' grievances.
[127:3:11] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:127 sub 0, 5, 6, 7, 8; SDA, 1996 reform, CLAP and Rs 1,000 crore: general knowledge, not checked

=== NOTES 3:128 | Skill Development and Employment in Ap – Apssdc, Employment Schemes
## SUB 0 | Local Candidates Act and Employment Guarantee
[128:0:0] TABLE head: Law / scheme | Year | Key provision
  [128:0:0.0] AP Employment of Local Candidates in Industries/Factories Act | July 2019 | 75% of private-sector jobs in industries/factories reserved for locals – AP the first State to do so
  [128:0:0.1] MGNREGS (NREGA 2005) | launched 2 Feb 2006 from Bandlapalli, Anantapur district (AP) | 100 days of wage work; Centre paid 100% of unskilled wages
  [128:0:0.2] VB-G RAM G (125-day Act, replaces MGNREGA) | began 1 Jul 2026; national launch at Mukkavaripalli (Tirupati district) | 125 days; pause up to 60 days in peak farm season; 60:40 Centre:State
[128:0:1] - Local-jobs Act criticised nationally as a **deterrent to investors**.
[128:0:2] - **MGNREGS 2025-26:** AP **3rd** nationally – **1,635.10 lakh person-days** (labour budget 1,800 lakh); **women 60.54%**; 1.06 lakh households got 100 days; wage rate **₹307/day** (from ₹300).
[128:0:3] - **GP buildings in MGNREGS convergence:** funding **90:10**, unit value **₹19 lakh in tribal areas** (APPSC key; not verified).
[128:0:4] - **PLFS 2023-24:** AP unemployment **4.1%** (all ages); **youth unemployment ~17.5%**, far **above** the national level.
[128:0:5] > Exam angle: "75% / 2019 / first State" is asked again and again; "125 days, not 100; 60:40".
[128:0:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:128 sub 2-3; Praveen CA360 Jul 2026 magazine p.21 (Bandlapalli); APPSC keys
## SUB 1 | National Skilling Schemes and Days
[128:1:0] - **Union Budget 2023-24:** **PMKVY 4.0** – Industry 4.0 courses (**coding, AI, robotics, mechatronics, IoT, 3D printing, drones**, soft skills); **30 Skill India International Centres** to skill youth for jobs abroad.
[128:1:1] - **World Youth Skills Day = 15 July** (UNGA, 2014). Themes: **2021 "Reimagining Youth Skills Post-Pandemic"**; 2024 "Youth Skills for Peace and Development"; 2025 "Youth Empowerment through AI and Digital Skills".
[128:1:2] - **Indian Institute of Skills:** foundation stone laid by the PM at **Kanpur** (Dec 2016), on the Singapore ITE model.
[128:1:3] TABLE head: Scheme | Skill scheme?
  [128:1:3.0] STRIVE – Skills Strengthening for Industrial Value Enhancement (ITIs, apprenticeship) | Yes
  [128:1:3.1] SANKALP – Skill Acquisition and Knowledge Awareness for Livelihood Promotion | Yes
  [128:1:3.2] SHREYAS – Scheme for Higher Education Youth in Apprenticeship and Skills | Yes
  [128:1:3.3] STRIDE (UGC) – Trans-disciplinary Research for India's Developing Economy | No – research support
[128:1:4] - **PM-SETU:** all **83 Govt ITIs** of AP to be upgraded as **16 hubs + 67 spokes**.
[128:1:5] > Exam angle: odd-one-out among skill schemes (STRIDE); Budget numbers (30 centres).
[128:1:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:128 sub 0-1; Union Budget 2023-24 (as in the PYQ explanation); general knowledge, not checked (IIS Kanpur, STRIVE/SANKALP/SHREYAS)
## SUB 2 | APSSDC and AP Skill Programmes
[128:2:0] - **APSSDC** (AP State Skill Development Corporation): set up **2014** as a not-for-profit company on the lines of **NSDC**; **nodal agency for placement-linked training** via **Skill Hubs, Skill Spokes**, colleges and **Sector Skill Councils**; 50 skill hubs in rural colleges (Jan 2026).
[128:2:1] - **70% placement** is the minimum for all State skill schemes.
[128:2:2] - **Siemens Centres of Excellence:** APSSDC with **Siemens** in engineering colleges (e.g. **JNTU Anantapur**).
[128:2:3] - **Merger (Apr 2026):** all skill departments into one **Commissionerate of Skill, Employment and Training** – motto "**One District – One Skill**".
[128:2:4] - **FY 2025-26:** 1,11,159 trained in Employability Skill Centres; **PM Internship Scheme 72,481** enrolments.
[128:2:5] TABLE head: Programme | Purpose
  [128:2:5.0] Rajiv Udyogasri (2007) | skill training that placed rural youth in private-sector jobs
  [128:2:5.1] Naipunyam | AI portal: skilling, certification and job matching; goal 20 lakh by 2029
  [128:2:5.2] Skill International | German (A1–B2) training for overseas jobs, mainly nurses; LoI (6 May 2026) with Japan's FVCC (not German)
  [128:2:5.3] Skill Census 2024 | household survey of the skills, qualifications and jobs of 100% of youth
  [128:2:5.4] Nirudyoga Bruthi (Super Six) | ₹3,000/month to unemployed youth
  [128:2:5.5] Yuva Nestham | allowance for educated youth, 2014–19 TDP government
[128:2:6] - **Super Six** employment target: **20 lakh jobs in 5 years**.
[128:2:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:128 sub 0-1
## SUB 3 | Livelihood Missions, Recruitment and Overseas Jobs
[128:3:0] TABLE head: Mission | Implementing society in AP
  [128:3:0.0] DAY-NRLM (rural) | SERP – AP 1st in India in SHG-bank linkage
  [128:3:0.1] DAY-NULM (urban) | MEPMA – 2.74 lakh SHGs
  [128:3:0.2] DDU-GKY (rural youth placement) | SEEDAP
[128:3:1] - **Streenidhi:** loans to SHG members up to **₹1 lakh at 11%**; **20.31 lakh Lakhpati Didis**.
[128:3:2] - **Mega DSC-2025:** **15,941** teachers; upper age limit for govt jobs **44 years till 2027**.
[128:3:3] - Village/ward secretariats renamed **Swarna Gramam and Swarna Ward** department; staff as Village Skill Officers.
[128:3:4] - AP **top State on iGOT Karmayogi** (1 crore+ enrolments).
[128:3:5] - **OMCAP** (**Overseas Manpower Company AP Ltd**): agency under the **Emigration Act, 1983**; Gulf migration heavy among women from **Konaseema**; housemaids must go via OMCAP – **over 90%** of abuse complaints come from those who did not.

=== NOTES 3:129 | Social Welfare Programmes in Ap – Sc/st/bc/minority Welfare, Pensions, Housing, Women and Child Welfare
## SUB 0 | SC, ST, BC and Minority Welfare
[129:0:0] # Education support
[129:0:1] - **Jagananna Vidya Deevena** (2019-20): **100% fee reimbursement** beyond Intermediate for **SC, ST, BC, Minorities, Kapu, EBC and Differently Abled** students; **maintenance support ₹20,000** a year; about **15.5 lakh students**. **Vasathi Deevena** = **hostel** costs. ₹4,900 cr dues pending (Jan 2026).
[129:0:2] - **NTR Videshi Vidya Adarana** (overseas study aid): **SC, ST, BC, minority and Kapu/EBC** (open-category poor) students – i.e. **all** categories; replaced by **Jagananna Videshi Vidya Deevena**.
[129:0:3] - **NTR Vidyonnathi:** civil-services coaching for SC/ST candidates – the empanelled institute is paid up to **₹1,50,000** per candidate.
[129:0:4] - **Post-matric scholarship (SC):** family income limit **₹2,50,000** a year.
[129:0:5] - **Portals:** **Jnanabhumi** – scholarships and fee reimbursement (Aadhaar-linked); **Pedarikampai Gelupu** – welfare corporations' bank-linked loans (2014-19); **OBMMS** = **Online Beneficiary Monitoring and Management System** of the welfare corporations.
[129:0:6] - **APSWREIS** residential schools: results **higher** than the State average.
[129:0:7] # SC Corporation (APSCCFC) schemes
[129:0:8] - **Land Purchase Scheme:** for **landless poor SC women**; unit cost up to **₹15 lakh per acre**; **75% subsidy : 25% loan**.
[129:0:9] - Vehicle scheme (four-wheelers for SC youth): about **35% subsidy**.
[129:0:10] - **Inter-caste marriage incentive:** **₹50,000** a couple.
[129:0:11] - **Indiramma Kalalu:** the State's financial commitment for **SC and ST** welfare (Amma Hastam = PDS kit; Ahar, Akankshya = Odisha).
[129:0:12] - AP was the **first State** to give **statutory status** to the **SC/ST Sub-Plan** (Act of **2013**).
[129:0:13] - **AP Social Welfare Fund:** 1974-75 (APPSC key; not verified).
[129:0:14] # BC and minorities
[129:0:15] - **YSR Jagananna Badugu Vikasam** is **not** a scheme for OBCs and was **not** launched on 26 October 2022 (it gave industrial incentives to SC/ST entrepreneurs).
[129:0:16] - **Dudekula:** Muslim community in AP's **BC** list; special cover in the **2021-22** budget.
[129:0:17] - **Dukan-O-Makan** (shop-cum-house) and **Roshni**: minority schemes, **fully State-funded**.
[129:0:18] - **Nai Roshni** (Ministry of Minority Affairs): **leadership development of minority women**.
[129:0:19] - Minorities: **Imams/Mouzams** get ₹5,000–10,000 honoraria (Mar 2026).
[129:0:20] TABLE head: Budget 2026-27 head | Allocation
  [129:0:20.0] BC welfare (total) | ₹51,021 cr (department ₹23,650.88 cr)
  [129:0:20.1] SC welfare | ₹20,644 cr
  [129:0:20.2] Minorities | ₹6,090 cr (₹3,133 cr sub-plan)
[129:0:21] > Exam angle: amounts and eligibility (₹50,000, ₹1.5 lakh, ₹2.5 lakh, 75:25, ₹15 lakh) and "which categories" questions.
