## SUB 1 | Welfare Packages and Livelihood Schemes
[129:1:0] - **Navaratnalu** (YSRCP, 2019–24): **nine** welfare pillars delivered by **DBT** and village/ward **volunteers** – Rythu Bharosa, Aarogyasri, Amma Vodi, Pension Kanuka, housing for all poor, fee reimbursement, Jalayagnam, **liquor prohibition (in phases)**, Aasara. **Not** among them: Vahana Mitra, **zero hunger**.
[129:1:1] TABLE head: Scheme | Who / what
  [129:1:1.0] Jagananna Chedodu | ₹10,000/yr to tailors, rajakas (dhobis), nayee brahmins (barbers)
  [129:1:1.1] Jagananna Thodu | interest-free working-capital loan up to ₹10,000 to petty vendors
  [129:1:1.2] YSR Navodayam | one-time debt restructuring of MSMEs (not transport jobs)
  [129:1:1.3] Jagananna Jeeva Kranthi | sheep/goat units to BC, SC, ST women
  [129:1:1.4] Chandranna Bima | insurance for unorganised workers against death and disability
  [129:1:1.5] Divya Darshanam | poor Hindus: free bus travel and darshan at select temples
  [129:1:1.6] NTR Sujala | RO drinking water at ₹2 per 20 litres
  [129:1:1.7] Deepam (2014-19) | LPG connections to poor women; AP declared smoke-free State on 8 June 2017
  [129:1:1.8] Rajiv Aarogyasri (2007) | flagship health-insurance scheme of CM Y.S. Rajasekhara Reddy
[129:1:2] - 2019-20 promise: ₹10,000 to about **23,000 barbers and 1,92,000 dhobis** (APPSC key; not verified).
[129:1:3] - **Kutumba Vikasam – Samaja Vikasam:** programme to cut AP's poverty ratio from **9.2% to 0% by 2029**; today's zero-poverty drive is **P4** (Public-Private-People Partnership).
[129:1:4] - **Super Six** (TDP-JSP-BJP, 2024): **Talliki Vandanam** (₹15,000 per school child), **Annadata Sukhibhava** (₹20,000/yr farmers), **Deepam-2** (3 free LPG cylinders), **free bus travel for women**, **Aadabidda Nidhi** (₹1,500/month women), **₹3,000/month unemployment allowance** + 20 lakh jobs.
[129:1:5] - Fiscal strain: welfare (**17 paise per rupee**) now matches debt service; shift from rights to cash transfers ("new welfarism"). AP **MPI** fell **0.053 → 0.025** (SES 2025-26).
[129:1:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:129 sub 6; APPSC keys; testbook.com (Kutumba Vikasam; search result); general knowledge, not checked (Divya Darshanam, Aarogyasri, Indiramma Kalalu)
## SUB 2 | Women's SHGs, DWCRA and Livelihoods
[129:2:0] TABLE head: Programme | Full form / purpose
  [129:2:0.0] DWCRA (1982-83) | Development of Women and Children in Rural Areas; sub-scheme of IRDP; self-help and income generation
  [129:2:0.1] Velugu (2000) | SHG-based poverty removal; merged with DWCRA as Indira Kranthi Patham, run by SERP
  [129:2:0.2] Stree Nidhi | credit to SHG members up to ₹1 lakh at 11%; ₹26,540 cr lent
  [129:2:0.3] Pasupu Kumkuma | ₹10,000 per DWCRA woman as capital infusion in phases – to strengthen their economic capacity
  [129:2:0.4] YSR Aasara | repaid SHG loans outstanding on 11 Apr 2019 in four instalments
  [129:2:0.5] YSR Cheyutha | yearly aid to SC/ST/BC/minority women aged 45-60
  [129:2:0.6] AP Rural Inclusive Growth Project (2015) | World Bank-aided, run by SERP; 150 most backward mandals
[129:2:1] - SERP mobilises **84.47 lakh** rural women; AP **1st in SHG-bank linkage**; about **42.15%** of SHGs availed bank loans in 2016-17 (APPSC key).
[129:2:2] - **Integrated Rural Energy Programme** is **not** an income programme for women – it gives basic home energy (cooking, heating, lighting).
[129:2:3] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:129 sub 1; APPSC keys
## SUB 3 | Public Distribution System
[129:3:0] TABLE head: Indicator (SES 2025-26) | Value
  [129:3:0.0] Fair Price Shops | 29,796
  [129:3:0.1] Monthly rice allocation | 2.36 lakh MT
  [129:3:0.2] Beneficiaries (Apr–Dec 2025) | 1.84 crore; ₹1,554.06 cr
[129:3:1] - **FPS density:** one shop per about **1,376** persons (national norm **2,000**); most ration shops in **Anantapuram** (Feb 2017).
[129:3:2] - **White ration card ceiling (2017):** **Rs 75,000** a year **urban**, Rs 60,000 rural.
[129:3:3] - **e-PDS:** **food grains to BPL families** through fair price shops, with **biometric authentication** and real-time stock tracking.
[129:3:4] - **Mana Biyyam (2013):** fine rice at **₹1/kg** to BPL, **max 20 kg** per family.
[129:3:5] - **Door delivery (2021):** **9,260 Mobile Dispensing Units** (₹539 cr); discontinued **1 May 2025**.
[129:3:6] - Entitlements: free **Sortex fortified rice**; **PHH 5 kg per person**, **AAY 35 kg per card**; **ragi and jowar up to 3 kg free**; wheat atta ₹20/kg; sugar ₹13.50 (AAY), ₹17 (PHH).
[129:3:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:129 sub 0
## SUB 4 | Women, Child and Marriage Schemes
[129:4:0] - **Bangaru Thalli** (2013, by Act): supports the **girl child of BPL families from birth till graduation**.
[129:4:1] - **Anna Amrutha Hastam** (2013): one full meal for pregnant and lactating women at **Anganwadi** centres (ICDS).
[129:4:2] - **AP Disha Bill, 2019:** 7-day investigation + 14-day trial + death penalty; **never got assent**; withdrawn **Aug 2026** (after BNS/BNSS). **Disha app** = SOS app linked to Disha police stations.
[129:4:3] TABLE head: Scheme | Feature
  [129:4:3.0] Mission Shakti | One Stop Centres in all districts + 181 helpline
  [129:4:3.1] Stree Shakti (15 Aug 2025) | free bus travel for women and transgender persons; 71 crore+ journeys; women's share 40% → 65%
  [129:4:3.2] Divyang Shakti (Mar 2026) | free travel for persons with disabilities
  [129:4:3.3] YSR Kalyanamasthu | marriage aid: ₹1 lakh SC/ST; ₹50,000 BC; also disabled, construction workers
  [129:4:3.4] YSR Shaadi Tohfa | minority brides (₹1 lakh)
  [129:4:3.5] Dulhan | ₹50,000 to poor Muslim brides via AP State Minorities Finance Corporation
[129:4:4] - Kalyanamasthu/Shaadi Tohfa: bride and groom must pass **SSC**; ages 18 and 21. **Chandranna Pelli Kanuka** (2014-19): **₹75,000** for SC inter-caste marriages.
[129:4:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:129 sub 5; general knowledge, not checked (Bangaru Thalli, Amrutha Hastam)
## SUB 5 | Housing Schemes
[129:5:0] TABLE head: Scheme | Launch / detail
  [129:5:0.0] INDIRAMMA – Integrated Novel Development in Rural Areas and Model Municipal Areas | April 2006; living standards, chiefly housing for the poor
  [129:5:0.1] NTR Housing (Gramin) | TDP 2014–19; unit cost ₹1,50,000 (2016-17)
  [129:5:0.2] YSR Jagananna Housing (APSHCL) | 3 June 2021; Jagananna colonies
  [129:5:0.3] PMAY convergence (PMAY-U + PMAY-G) | target 7.60 lakh houses by 2026
[129:5:1] - APTIDCO: HUDCO loan to finish **2.61 lakh** houses; PMAY-U 2.0: **61,084** approved; **PMAY-NTR Nagar** ~**1.93 lakh** houses; star-rated appliances to **5 lakh PMAY households** (APSHCL + EESL + APSEEDCO, Mar 2026).

[129:6:0] - **YSR Pension Kanuka** renamed **NTR Bharosa** pensions (**July 2024**); about **65.31 lakh** beneficiaries in 28 categories.

  [129:6:1.2] Severe health conditions | ₹10,000
  [129:6:1.3] Fully disabled | ₹15,000

[130:1:1] - Close options trap: 32.60 (2022-23) / 33.71 (2023-24 RE) / 34.14 (2024-25 BE) / 36.04 (2025-26 RE).
[130:1:2] - **Composition (2024-25 RE):** **market loans ₹4,24,070 cr (~75%) – largest**; central loans ₹42,910 cr (8%); provident fund ₹31,188 cr; small savings ₹5,506 cr (1%); others ₹60,814 cr (11%). Market borrowings ₹4,83,093 cr (2025-26 RE).
[130:1:3] - **Total liabilities incl. PSUs: ₹9,74,556 cr (12 June 2024)**.
[130:1:4] # Interest and debt service
[130:1:5] TABLE head: Year | Interest (₹ cr)
  [130:1:5.0] 2017-18 RE | 14,783
  [130:1:5.1] 2022-23 | 25,492 (~16% of receipts)
  [130:1:5.2] 2023-24 | 29,481
  [130:1:5.3] 2024-25 | 32,967 (~17%)
  [130:1:5.4] 2025-26 RE | 37,233
  [130:1:5.5] 2026-27 BE | 37,281 (16%)
[130:1:6] - **Budget 2017-18:** debt servicing (interest + repayments) ≈ **14.5%** of expenditure. **Budget 2023-24:** interest = **14% of revenue receipts** (pension 10%).
[130:1:7] - Interest = **charged item (not voted)**; fixed, non-developmental; more than doubled since 2017-18. Sustainability signs: **debt-GSDP** and **interest/revenue receipts**; **golden rule** – borrow only for capital assets. AP runs a **primary deficit** → borrowing also funds **current spending**.
[130:1:8] # Benchmarks and bifurcation
[130:1:9] - **N.K. Singh Committee (2017):** general government debt **60%** = **Centre 40% + States 20%**; **FRBM (amended 2018)**: Central 40% by **31 March 2025**. **16th FC:** States' FD **3% of GSDP** (Union 3.5%); **Net Borrowing Ceiling** 3% of GSDP.
[130:1:10] - Comparators (2023-24): **AP 34.6%**, Punjab 42.9%, West Bengal 38.3%, Rajasthan 37.9%; all States 29.2%.
[130:1:11] - **APRA 2014 Section 54:** Public Debt and Public Account liabilities **split by population ratio**; loans re-lent for a project go to the State where it lies.
[130:1:12] - **Centre (2016-17 RE):** outstanding liabilities about **46.7% of GDP** (APPSC key).
[130:1:13] # Off-budget borrowings and guarantees
[130:1:14] - Off-budget = loans by State PSUs/corporations **repaid from the State budget**, outside reported debt, used to **get around FRBM caps**; **₹1.28 lakh cr, 9.7% of GSDP (Mar 2023, CAG 2024)**; ₹1,40,470 cr (31 Mar 2024).
[130:1:15] - **APSDC** escrowed **liquor revenue** to borrow; AP High Court questioned tax revenue going to APSDC instead of the **Consolidated Fund**. Centre adjusts them against **NBC** (e.g. Kerala's **KIIFB**); **16th FC**: bring them **on budget**; they **obscure true debt** and may hurt **sovereign ratings**.
[130:1:16] - **Guarantees** = **contingent liabilities**, outside outstanding debt, due only **when invoked**: **₹1,77,328 cr (10% of GSDP)** in 2026-27; ₹1,54,797 cr (11%) in 2023-24; exposure ₹3.11 lakh cr (Dec 2025).
[130:1:17] TABLE head: Fund | Purpose
  [130:1:17.0] Consolidated Sinking Fund | RBI-run; amortise liabilities; invested in GoI securities
  [130:1:17.1] Guarantee Redemption Fund | RBI-run; meet invoked guarantees
[130:1:18] > Exam angle: debt-GSDP by year (19.4 → 32 → 34.14), largest debt component (market loans).
[130:1:19] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.36 and Annexures 3.5-3.6 p.358; PRS AP Budget Analysis 2023-24; app notes 3:130 sub 9-10
[130:1:20] - **2014-15 debt-GSDP (first year after bifurcation):** sources differ – a CAG table ~28.2%, a Lok Sabha reply 23.9%; the APPSC options (28.19–29.63%) remain unsettled.
## SUB 2 | Revenue Receipts and Own Tax Revenue
[130:2:0] - **Revenue receipts** = income the government **need not pay back** = **own tax + own non-tax + share in central taxes + grants-in-aid from the Centre**. Corporation tax = tax revenue; interest, dividends, fees = non-tax; **market borrowings = capital receipt**; pensions = revenue expenditure. Centre's gross tax order (2009 question): customs > corporation tax > income tax > Union excise.
[130:2:1] TABLE head: Head (₹ cr) | 2022-23 | 2023-24 | 2024-25 BE | 2026-27 BE
  [130:2:1.0] Own tax | 78,026 | 85,922 | 1,09,789 | 1,25,846
  [130:2:1.1] Own non-tax | 5,417 | 7,432 | 10,576 | –
  [130:2:1.2] Share in central taxes | 38,177 | 45,711 | 50,475 | 64,362
  [130:2:1.3] Grants-in-aid | 36,149 | 34,702 | 30,334 | 32,458
  [130:2:1.4] Revenue receipts | 1,57,768 | 1,73,767 | 2,01,174 | 2,34,190 (+19%)
[130:2:2] - **2018-19 revenue receipts: ₹1,57,599 cr** (not to be confused with ₹1,57,768 cr of 2022-23).
[130:2:3] - **Grants-in-aid share:** **23.6%** of revenue receipts in **2016-17**; 15% in 2024-25 BE. CAG 2015-16: own tax ≈ 45%.
[130:2:4] - **2024-25 RE:** ₹1,91,142 cr = own tax ₹94,967 + non-tax ₹7,018 + Centre ₹89,157. Own tax ~**half**, Centre **45–47%**, non-tax **~4%**; order: **Own Tax > Central Transfers > Own Non-Tax**. **2026-27:** own resources **59%** + central transfers **41%**.
[130:2:5] - CAG: only **₹1.05 lakh cr** (48% of estimate) collected by **Nov 2025**; non-tax under **one-fifth** of BE.

## SUB 3 | Deficits and Expenditure Pattern
[130:3:0] - **2014-15** (first post-bifurcation year): **revenue, fiscal and primary deficits all increased**. **2016-17 BE**: AP had **revenue, fiscal and primary deficits**. Revenue grew about **16%** in 2015-16 (APPSC key).
[130:3:1] - 2023-24: RD ₹38,683 cr, FD ₹62,720 cr; 2024-25 RE: RD ₹48,311 cr, FD ₹73,362 cr.
[130:3:2] - Revenue expenditure creates no asset (salaries, pensions, interest, **welfare/DBT**); capital expenditure creates assets or cuts liabilities. Welfare = revenue expenditure → widens the revenue deficit (ES 2025-26: conditional transfers or sunset clauses).
[130:3:3] TABLE head: Year | Revenue exp (₹ cr) | Capital outlay (₹ cr) | Share
  [130:3:3.0] 2014-15 | – | – | capex 8.95% of total expenditure (APPSC key)
  [130:3:3.1] 2022-23 | 2,01,256 | 7,244 | ~3.4%
  [130:3:3.2] 2023-24 | 2,12,450 | 23,331 | ~9.9%
  [130:3:3.3] 2024-25 RE | 2,24,343 | 24,072 | ~9.7%
  [130:3:3.4] 2025-26 RE | 2,38,022 | 33,135 | ~11.9%
  [130:3:3.5] 2026-27 BE | 2,56,143 | 48,698 | ~15.7%
[130:3:4] - **2016-21:** AP capex **below 15%** of total expenditure; capex reflects the **commitment to infrastructure creation**. 2024-25 actual ₹16,141 cr vs BE ₹32,713 cr (~51% short). All States 2014-24: capex 13–20%. Capital outlay mainly **irrigation, roads, water supply, urban development**.
[130:3:5] TABLE head: Developmental | Non-developmental
  [130:3:5.0] Social services: education, health, water, housing, SC/ST/BC welfare, nutrition | General services: organs of State, police, defence, tax collection, administration
  [130:3:5.1] Economic services: agriculture, rural development, irrigation, energy, industry, transport | Interest payments, pensions
[130:3:6] - Dev/non-dev split **cuts across** revenue/capital (teacher salary = dev revenue; dam = dev capital).
[130:3:7] - **Committed (2026-27):** **interest ₹37,281 cr (16% of receipts)**; **pensions ₹23,713 cr (10%)** → **fiscal rigidity**. All States: committed + subsidies = **51.76%** of revenue exp (2023-24).
[130:3:8] - **AP Guaranteed Pension System Act, 2023:** **50% of last basic pay**; **12th PRC** set up.

## SUB 4 | Own Non-tax Revenue and Minerals
[130:4:0] - Non-tax = receipts not raised by taxing the public: **interest receipts, dividends and profits of PSUs, fees, fines, user charges, royalties** (GST is a tax, not non-tax). AP's 2023-24 BE non-tax: **₹15,400 cr**. **User charges** = a fiscal consolidation tool; State PSU profits/dividends are in "Others" (₹2,680 cr, 2024-25 RE).
[130:4:1] TABLE head: Head (₹ cr) | 2022-23 | 2023-24 | 2024-25 RE
  [130:4:1.0] Mines and Minerals | 3,024 | 3,060 | 3,518
  [130:4:1.1] Medical and Health | 177 | 211 | 313
  [130:4:1.2] Interest receipts | 9 | 21 | 27
  [130:4:1.3] Total non-tax | 5,417 | 7,432 | 7,018
[130:4:2] - **Mines and Minerals = largest non-tax source every year** (2015-16, 2016-17 RE PYQs; **50% in 2024-25 RE**; 37% in 2025-26 RE, ahead of Medical and Health 34%); forests and interest receipts among the smallest.
[130:4:3] - AP = "**Ratna Garbha**": barytes (**Mangampet, YSR Kadapa**), beach sands, limestone (Kadapa–Kurnool), dolomite, **mica (Nellore)**, **uranium**, iron ore, gold, diamonds, granite (**platinum not listed**). AP's share in India's mineral output ~**6%** (2016-17, APPSC key).
[130:4:4] - Mining revenue **₹4,756 cr (2022-23)**. **Royalty** = **ad valorem** on **Average Sale Price** under the **MMDR Act, 1957** (⚠ APPSC key 2017 gave 1948). Ad valorem = % of value; specific = fixed per unit.
[130:4:5] - **Minor minerals:** **sand** is a minor mineral; **States** frame the rules for granting minor-mineral concessions (MMDR s.15) and to **prevent illegal mining** (s.23C).
[130:4:6] - **MADA v. SAIL (2024):** **royalty is not a tax**; States may tax mineral-bearing land; auction revenue goes to **States**. **DMF** = non-profit trust, **Section 9B** (MMDR Amendment 2015); **Section 9D** called unfair to mineral-rich States. MMDR amended 2023; India in **Minerals Security Partnership**; not rich in all **30 critical minerals**.
[130:4:7] - **Free Sand Policy (8 July 2024):** buyers pay only operation cost, seigniorage and taxes.
[130:4:8] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.34 (Chart 3.3); app notes 3:130 sub 5; MMDR Act provisions (general knowledge, not checked)
## SUB 5 | Budget Basics, CFMS and Reform-linked Borrowing
[130:5:0] TABLE head: Article | Subject
  [130:5:0.0] 112 | Annual Financial Statement of the Union
  [130:5:0.1] 202 | Annual Financial Statement of a State
  [130:5:0.2] 110 / 114 / 116 | Money Bill / Appropriation Bills / Vote on account
  [130:5:0.3] 246A / 269A / 279A | GST power / IGST / GST Council (three-fourths majority)
  [130:5:0.4] 270 / 275 / 280 | distribution of taxes / grants / Finance Commission
  [130:5:0.5] 293(3) | State needs Centre's consent to borrow while it owes the Centre
[130:5:1] - **101st Amendment, 2016**; GST from **1 July 2017**; GST compensation ended **June 2022**. GST introduction was first announced in the **Union Budget 2006-07**.
[130:5:2] - Union model: **Gender Budget = Statement 13 (since 2005-06)**; **Child Budget = Statement 12 (since 2009-10)**.
[130:5:3] - **Socio-Economic Survey**: tabled before the budget speech; **not a constitutional duty**; dropped in the 2026 Budget session.
[130:5:4] - **CFMS (2018):** Comprehensive Financial Management System centralised treasury and payments online; criticised for **hurting civic bodies' autonomy**.
[130:5:5] - **Reform-linked borrowing (2020-21):** four reforms – **One Nation One Ration Card, ease of doing business, urban local body reforms, power sector**; AP got ₹2,525 cr for ONORC (Sep 2020); **AP and MP first to complete three of four**; Feb 2021: ₹1,515 cr for **power-sector reform** (DBT of farm power, Srikakulam) – 2nd after MP.
[130:5:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:130 sub 0, 11; general knowledge, not checked (GST announcement 2006-07)

  [130:6:4.2] Annadata Sukhibhava (with PM-Kisan) | ₹6,600 cr
[130:6:5] - **P4** (Public-Private-People Partnership): **zero poverty in five years**, **family as unit**. **Harita AP:** green cover ~30% → 37% by 2030 → 50% by 2047. AP has the **highest per-capita social security pension spending** (₹5,061, 16th FC).
[130:6:6] - **Budget 2025-26 BE:** Rural Development ₹18,140 cr (+50%); Social Welfare & Nutrition ₹9,071 cr (−38%); **Agriculture & Allied ₹14,138 cr (+128%)** over 2024-25 RE ₹6,192 cr – partly a low base (2024-25 budgeted ₹12,514 cr).
[130:6:7] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:130 sub 1-2 (from AP Budget 2026-27 documents)

=== NOTES 3:131 | Central Assistance to Andhra Pradesh – Finance Commission Transfers and Centrally Sponsored Schemes
## SUB 0 | Central Sector vs Centrally Sponsored Schemes
[131:0:0] TABLE head: Feature | Central Sector (CS) | Centrally Sponsored (CSS)
  [131:0:0.0] Subjects | Union List | State/Concurrent
  [131:0:0.1] Funding | 100% Centre, run by Central Ministries | 60:40 general; 90:10 special category/NE/Himalayan
  [131:0:0.2] Examples | PM-KISAN, Ayushman Bharat, MPLADS | NHM, ICDS, PMAY, PMKSY
  [131:0:0.3] Union spending 2026-27 | 17% | 8%
[131:0:1] - **Rationalisation (2016):** on the CMs' sub-group report, CSS were merged into **28 umbrella schemes** (core-of-the-core + core + optional).
[131:0:2] - **Bharatmala Pariyojana** (2017): umbrella programme for **holistic highway development**.
[131:0:3] - **PFMS** (Public Financial Management System, CGA): online system through which the Centre **tracks fund flow to States** under schemes. **SNA-SPARSH:** **just-in-time** release of CSS funds via **State IFMIS, RBI e-Kuber and PFMS**; 28 States + 3 UTs, 66 CSS.
[131:0:4] - **AP ranked first** among States in **28 CSS and central sector schemes** (up to Nov 2018).
[131:0:5] - After the **Plan/non-Plan** merger, the **Tribal Sub-Plan** continues as the **Scheduled Tribe Component** – **commensurate allocation** within schemes.
[131:0:6] - **VB-G RAM G Act** replaced MGNREGA (Centre paid 100% of unskilled wages) with **60:40**. New 5-year CS/CSS appraisal cycle from 1 Apr 2026; **16th FC wants outcome-linked CSS**. CSS matching shares (**rigid co-financing**) squeeze States' fiscal space; **CSS ≈ 40%** of central transfers.
[131:0:7] - Central funds sent **directly to State implementing agencies** in 2016-17: **₹1,033.19 cr** (APPSC key).
[131:0:8] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:131 sub 0; general knowledge, not checked (28 umbrella schemes, PFMS, Bharatmala, ST Component)
## SUB 1 | Finance Commission Channels and AP's Share
[131:1:0] TABLE head: Article | Channel
  [131:1:0.0] 280 | Finance Commission – recommends distribution of tax proceeds between the Centre and States
  [131:1:0.1] 270 | tax devolution (distribution of Union taxes)
  [131:1:0.2] 275 | statutory grants – revenue deficit, local body, disaster
  [131:1:0.3] 282 | discretionary grants
  [131:1:0.4] 280(3)(bb),(c) | local bodies
[131:1:1] # States' and AP's share by Finance Commission
[131:1:2] TABLE head: FC | States' share | AP's share
  [131:1:2.0] 13th | 32% | –
  [131:1:2.1] 14th (2015-20) | 42% | 4.305%
  [131:1:2.2] 15th (2021-26) | 41% (J&K became a UT) | 4.05%
  [131:1:2.3] 16th (2026-31), Arvind Panagariya | 41% | 4.217%
[131:1:3] - **Special Category:** began **1969**, credited to the **5th Finance Commission** (Mahavir Tyagi) in most books – strictly conferred by the **NDC** under the **Gadgil formula**; the **14th FC (2015)** stopped distinguishing special and general States.
[131:1:4] - **16th FC criteria:** income distance **42.5%**, population (2011) **17.5%**, demographic performance 10%, area 10%, forest 10%, **contribution to GDP 10% (new)**; tax effort dropped. 18 States asked for 50%; all five southern States gained.
[131:1:5] - **Grants:** post-devolution **revenue deficit grants** under the 14th and 15th FCs (Art. 275); **16th FC ended RD grants**. 16th FC award to AP (2026-31): rural local bodies ₹16,627 cr, urban ₹12,158 cr, disaster management ₹6,125 cr.
[131:1:6] - **SDRF/SDMF corpus ₹2,04,401 cr**, **75:25** (general), **90:10** (NE/Himalayan); heatwaves and lightning added. **14th FC SDRF to AP:** ~**₹2,492 cr** (2015-20).
[131:1:7] - Divisible pool ≈ 81% of gross tax revenue (cesses excluded). Tax devolution to AP: ₹50,475 cr (2024-25 BE), ₹52,080 cr (RE), **₹64,362 cr (2026-27 BE)**.
[131:1:8] > Exam angle: "Article 280" for the Finance Commission; States' and AP's shares by FC.
[131:1:9] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:130 sub 6 and 3:131 sub 2; web search: clearias.com / Wikipedia 'Gadgil formula' (SCS origin)
## SUB 2 | Plan-era Financing and the 2017-18 Reforms
[131:2:0] - State Plans = **own resources + Normal Central Assistance** via the **Gadgil (Gadgil-Mukherjee) formula**; NCA loans:grants = **70:30** general, **10:90** special category.
[131:2:1] - **Budget 2017-18:** Budget advanced to **1 February**, **Railway Budget merged**, **Plan/non-Plan** distinction ended (Outcome Budget came earlier, 2005); it did **not** increase the number of CSS.
[131:2:2] - Trap: Gadgil **70:30 = loans:grants**; CSS **60:40 = cost sharing**.
[131:2:3] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:131 sub 1
## SUB 3 | Package in Lieu of SCS and Polavaram
[131:3:0] - SCS not given; **Special Assistance Measure ₹24,350 cr**, incl. **₹3,979 cr RD grant**.
[131:3:1] - Centre pays **90%** of **externally aided project** cost; EAP inflow ₹16,252 cr (2024-25 RE), ₹21,585 cr (2025-26 RE). Amaravati: ₹15,000 cr special assistance (Jan 2026).
[131:3:2] - **Polavaram Irrigation Project** = **national project** under **Section 90, APRA 2014**; the **Centre executes and funds** it (irrigation component); older estimate **₹55,548 cr**; Phase I revised cost ₹30,436.95 cr (Aug 2024). State lines ₹6,705 cr (2025-26), ₹6,105 cr (2026-27).
[131:3:3] - **APRA Eleventh Schedule para 8:** **ongoing and new projects on the Krishna and Godavari** are executed by the **State in which the project is located**.
[131:3:4] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt) s.90 and Eleventh Schedule; app notes 3:131 sub 3
## SUB 4 | Central Transfers to AP
[131:4:0] TABLE head: Item (₹ cr) | 2022-23 | 2023-24 | 2024-25 RE
  [131:4:0.0] Tax devolution | 38,177 | 45,711 | 52,080
  [131:4:0.1] FC grants | 13,174 | 9,641 | 4,922
  [131:4:0.2] Other GIA incl. CSS | 22,953 | 25,035 | 17,036
  [131:4:0.3] EAP and others | 8,432 | 5,037 | 16,252
  [131:4:0.4] Total | 81,563 | 84,251 | 89,157
[131:4:1] - **2025-26 RE: ₹1,04,493 cr**; largest = **FC transfers ₹62,188 cr**; other GIA ₹21,711 cr.
[131:4:2] - Central transfers ≈ **45%** of AP revenue receipts (2024-25 RE); AP gets **40–47%** of revenue from the Centre and **relies more on grants than the average State**. Cyclone Titli ₹1,200 cr.
[131:4:3] - **BE vs RE 2024-25:** grants ₹30,334 → ₹21,958 cr (short); total ₹80,809 → ₹89,157 cr only due to **EAP loans**. APSES 2024-25: "**lesser than anticipated flow of funds from GoI**" a fiscal stress.
[131:4:4] ~~ Sources: AP Socio-Economic Survey 2024-25 p.34-35 (Chart 3.4) and Annexure 3.3; app notes 3:131 sub 4

  As per SES 2025-26 (2025-26 RE), what were the total central transfers to Andhra Pradesh and their largest component?
  Rs 89,157 crore; EAP and others | *Rs 1,04,493 crore; Finance Commission transfers | Rs 1,04,493 crore; other grants-in-aid including CSS | Rs 81,563 crore; Finance Commission transfers
  x: 2025-26 RE total central transfers were Rs 1,04,493 crore, with the Finance Commission line (Rs 62,188 crore) the largest. Rs 89,157 crore is the 2024-25 RE total and Rs 81,563 crore the 2022-23 total; other GIA incl. CSS was only Rs 21,711 crore.
[g52a15e4c2ce7] sub4
  In the 2024-25 RE, central transfers formed about what share of Andhra Pradesh's revenue receipts?
  23.6% | *45% | 59% | 15%
  x: Central transfers were about 45% of AP's revenue receipts in 2024-25 RE.

=== NOTES 3:133 | A.p. Reorganisation Act 2014 – Structure, Schedules and Key Provisions
## SUB 0 | Structure of the Act – Parts, Sections, Schedules
[133:0:0] # The Act at a glance
[133:0:1] - **Size:** **12 Parts, 108 Sections, 13 Schedules** – Act **No. 6 of 2014** (Gazette of India Extraordinary **No. 6**, 1 March 2014).
[133:0:2] - **Popular name:** the "**Telangana Act**" (bifurcation Act); made under **Articles 3 and 4**.
[133:0:3] - **Character:** passed by Parliament although the AP Legislature had rejected the Bill; widely criticised as hurriedly drafted ("arbitrary, does not clearly address many issues").
[133:0:4] TABLE head: Part | Title | Sections
  [133:0:4.0] I | Preliminary (definitions: appointed day, population ratio 58.32 : 41.68) | 1–2
  [133:0:4.1] II | Reorganisation of the State of Andhra Pradesh | 3–11
  [133:0:4.2] III | Representation in the Legislatures | 12–29
  [133:0:4.3] IV | High Court | 30–43
  [133:0:4.4] V | Authorisation of Expenditure and Distribution of Revenues | 44–46
  [133:0:4.5] VI | Apportionment of Assets and Liabilities | 47–67
  [133:0:4.6] VII | Provisions as to Certain Corporations | 68–75
  [133:0:4.7] VIII | Provisions as to Services (not water) | 76–83
  [133:0:4.8] IX | Management and Development of Water Resources | 84–91
  [133:0:4.9] X | Infrastructure and Special Economic Measures | 92–94
  [133:0:4.10] XI | Access to Higher Education | 95
  [133:0:4.11] XII | Legal and Miscellaneous Provisions | 96–108
[133:0:5] - **⚠ Trap:** Part VIII = Services; water resources = **Part IX**; Part V = expenditure and revenues (not Part IV, which is the High Court).
[133:0:6] > 12 Parts – 108 Sections – 13 Schedules; II reorganisation, V revenues, IX water.
[133:0:7] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014, Gazette text (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt) p.1 and Part headings; job b1-r263 notes row-263
## SUB 1 | Enactment Timeline and the Appointed Day
[133:1:0] TABLE head: Event | Date
  [133:1:0.0] Bill introduced in Lok Sabha (Sushilkumar Shinde) | 13 February 2014
  [133:1:0.1] Passed by Lok Sabha | 18 February 2014
  [133:1:0.2] Passed by Rajya Sabha (Upper House) | 20 February 2014
  [133:1:0.3] President's assent (Pranab Mukherjee) and Gazette publication (Act 6, Gazette No. 6) | 1 March 2014
  [133:1:0.4] Notification fixing the appointed day | 4 March 2014
  [133:1:0.5] Appointed day – Telangana formed as the 29th State | 2 June 2014
[133:1:1] - **Parliament passed it in February 2014; the President signed it in March 2014.**
[133:1:2] - **"Appointed day" (Sec 2(a)):** the day the **Central Government appoints by notification in the Official Gazette** – fixed as 2 June 2014 (the notification itself was issued on 4 March 2014).
[133:1:3] - The State was bifurcated in **June 2014**; residuary AP continues the State of 1956 – only Telangana was new.
[133:1:4] - **Sec 10:** First Schedule of the Constitution amended – new entry "**29. Telangana**" (India now has 28 States after J&K became a UT in 2019).
[133:1:5] > LS 18 Feb – RS 20 Feb – assent 1 Mar – appointed day 2 Jun 2014.
[133:1:6] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text p.1 (assent 1 March 2014, Act No. 6), s.2(a), s.10; job b1-r263 notes row-263 (bill dates)
## SUB 2 | Part II – Telangana, Common Capital, Governor and Police
[133:2:0] TABLE head: Section | Provision
  [133:2:0.0] 3 | Formation of Telangana – 10 districts incl. Hyderabad, Medak, Karimnagar, Khammam (minus Polavaram villages); amended by the 2014 Polavaram Ordinance/Act to move 7 Khammam mandals to AP
  [133:2:0.1] 4 | Residuary AP – the remaining territories (13 districts, e.g. Srikakulam)
  [133:2:0.2] 5 | Hyderabad common capital for a period not exceeding ten years; then capital of Telangana, AP to get a new capital; common capital = area notified as GHMC
  [133:2:0.3] 6 | Central expert committee on AP's new capital – report within six months of enactment
  [133:2:0.4] 7 | Governor of existing AP to be common Governor for a period determined by the President
  [133:2:0.5] 8 | Governor's special responsibility for security of life, liberty and property of residents of the common capital (law and order, internal security, vital installations, Government buildings)
  [133:2:0.6] 9 | Central Government to assist both States to raise additional police; Greyhound Training Centre
  [133:2:0.7] 10 | First Schedule of the Constitution – Telangana as entry 29
[133:2:1] - **Sec 8(3):** Governor acts in his **individual judgment after consulting the Telangana Council of Ministers**; his decision in his discretion is **final**.
[133:2:2] - **Sec 8(4):** assisted by **two advisors** appointed by the **Central Government**.
[133:2:3] - **Sec 9(2):** Centre to maintain the **Greyhound Training Centre, Hyderabad** as a common centre for **three years**, then it goes to Telangana; **9(5):** **Greyhound and OCTOPUS** forces divided **on options from personnel**.
[133:2:4] - **Hyderabad's status:** it lay in Telangana's territory (not common territory); it was AP's **de jure** capital till 2 June 2024 while AP worked from Amaravati; tax revenue of Hyderabad is **not shared** with AP.
[133:2:5] - **⚠ Trap:** the common-capital limit is "not exceeding 10 years"; the common Governor's period is set by the President, not by the Act.
[133:2:6] > Sec 5 capital ≤10 yrs (GHMC); 6 committee 6 months; 7 common Governor; 8 Governor + 2 advisers; 9 police, Greyhounds 3 yrs.
[133:2:7] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text pp.2-4, ss.3-10; job b1-r263 notes row-263 (Polavaram ordinance)
## SUB 3 | Part III – Seats in Parliament and the Legislatures
[133:3:0] TABLE head: House | AP | Telangana | Section
  [133:3:0.0] Rajya Sabha | 11 | 7 | 12 (Fourth Schedule of Constitution), 13 + First Schedule of the Act
  [133:3:0.1] Lok Sabha | 25 | 17 | 14
  [133:3:0.2] Legislative Assembly | 175 | 119 | 17
  [133:3:0.3] Legislative Council | 50 (not more than) | 40 | 22, 23
  [133:3:0.4] Assembly after delimitation | 225 | 153 | 26
[133:3:1] - **Sec 15:** Delimitation of Parliamentary and Assembly Constituencies Order, **2008** amended (Second Schedule).
[133:3:2] - **Sec 18:** Governor may nominate **one Anglo-Indian** MLA in each State (Art 333) – ended by the **104th Amendment** (2020).
[133:3:3] - **Sec 22:** Legislative Councils (AP not more than 50, Telangana 40); existing members allotted by the **Fourth Schedule**.
[133:3:4] - **Sec 26:** seats to rise to **225 / 153** subject to **Art 170**; under **26(2)(a)** all constituencies are **single-member** (not Sec 22).
[133:3:5] - **AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2015:** AP Council raised from **50 to 58** (Art 171: one-third of 175).
[133:3:6] - AP's 175 seats: **29 SC, 7 ST**; most seats East Godavari, fewest Vizianagaram (9) in 2014.
[133:3:7] - Constitution Schedules amended by the Act: **First** (States) and **Fourth** (Rajya Sabha seats).
[133:3:8] > 25 + 17 Lok Sabha; 175/119 Assembly → 225/153; Council 50 (58 from 2015)/40; Sec 18 Anglo-Indian.
[133:3:9] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text pp.4-7, ss.12-26; prsindia.org billtrack AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Bill 2015; old app notes 3:133
[133:3:10] - **Sec 14:** Lok Sabha seats allotted by deeming the **First Schedule of the Representation of the People Act, 1950** amended.
## SUB 4 | Part IV – High Court
[133:4:0] - **Sec 30:** the **High Court of Judicature at Hyderabad** = **common High Court** for both States till AP's High Court is constituted (named "High Court of Judicature at Hyderabad for the State of Telangana and the State of Andhra Pradesh"); judges' salaries shared by population ratio.
[133:4:1] - **Sec 31:** a **separate High Court for AP**; the Hyderabad court becomes Telangana's; **31(2): principal seat at such place as the President notifies** – the Act names **no place**.
[133:4:2] - **Presidential order of 26 December 2018** (Art 214) → **High Court of Andhra Pradesh at Amaravati** functioning from **1 January 2019**.
[133:4:3] > Sec 30 common HC; Sec 31 separate AP HC; Amaravati, 1 Jan 2019.
[133:4:4] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text pp.8-9, ss.30-31; job b1-r263 notes row-263; old app notes 3:133
## SUB 5 | The Thirteen Schedules
[133:5:0] TABLE head: Schedule | Content
  [133:5:0.0] First (s.13) | Allocation of sitting members of the Council of States (Rajya Sabha)
  [133:5:0.1] Second (s.15) | Amendments to the Delimitation of Parliamentary and Assembly Constituencies Order, 2008
  [133:5:0.2] Third (s.24) | Modification of the Delimitation of Council Constituencies Order, 2006
  [133:5:0.3] Fourth (s.22) | List of members of the Legislative Councils of the two States
  [133:5:0.4] Fifth (s.28) | SC Order 1950 amended – item 9 Beda (Budga) Jangam deleted from AP's list (it stays in Telangana's list)
  [133:5:0.5] Sixth (s.29) | ST Order 1950 amended
  [133:5:0.6] Seventh (s.52) | List of Funds – A. Provident, Pension and Insurance Funds (incl. Central Government Employees Group Insurance Scheme); B. Sinking, reserve funds – "Industrial Development Funds" incl. Sericulture Development Fund; C. other funds incl. C.M. Relief Fund
  [133:5:0.7] Eighth (s.59) | Apportionment of pension liability
  [133:5:0.8] Ninth (ss.68, 71) | Government companies and corporations (e.g. SCCL, Sports Authority of AP)
  [133:5:0.9] Tenth (s.75) | Training institutions/centres – continuance of facilities; 107 institutions, mostly in Hyderabad (e.g. Telugu and Hindi Academies)
  [133:5:0.10] Eleventh (s.85) | Principles for the River Management Boards (drinking water first)
  [133:5:0.11] Twelfth (s.92) | Coal, Oil and Gas, Power – SCCL equity 51% Telangana, 49% GoI; APERC joint 6 months; SLDC shared 2 years
  [133:5:0.12] Thirteenth (s.93) | Central commitments – education institutions and infrastructure (within 10 years)
[133:5:1] - **Tenth Schedule (s.75):** terms between the two States to be agreed within **one year**; the **State Information Commission** is not listed, APPSC is dealt with by s.83.
[133:5:2] > I RS members, II delimitation, IV councils, VII funds, IX corporations, X institutions (107), XII coal-oil-power, XIII commitments.
[133:5:3] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text pp.28-71 (Schedules I-XIII), ss.75, 83; old app notes 3:133
## SUB 6 | Other Key Sections – Assets, Services, Water, Education, Disputes
[133:6:0] TABLE head: Section | Provision
  [133:6:0.0] 46 | 13th FC award split by population ratio; 14th FC to make separate awards
  [133:6:0.1] 66 | Centre may adjust allocations on a reference within three years (division of assets – deadline 2 June 2017)
  [133:6:0.2] 74 | Set-off and carry forward of losses (Income-tax Act) of transferred State PSUs
  [133:6:0.3] 75 | Continuance of facilities in Tenth Schedule institutions
  [133:6:0.4] 79 | Continuance of officers in the same post
  [133:6:0.5] 83 | APPSC continues for AP; new PSC for Telangana
  [133:6:0.6] 84–85 | Apex Council (Union Water Resources Minister + both CMs); KRMB (in AP) and GRMB (in Telangana)
  [133:6:0.7] 89 | Term of the Krishna Water Disputes Tribunal extended
  [133:6:0.8] 90 | Polavaram – national project
  [133:6:0.9] 93 | Thirteenth Schedule measures within 10 years
  [133:6:0.10] 94 | 94(1) tax incentives; 94(2) backward areas; 94(3) new capital; 94(4) denotify forest land
  [133:6:0.11] 95 | Equal opportunities for quality higher education – Art 371D admission quotas continue for ten years
  [133:6:0.12] 96 | Art 168(1)(a) amended – Telangana gets a Legislative Council
  [133:6:0.13] 97 | Art 371D amended to cover AP or Telangana
  [133:6:0.14] 98 | Sec 15A of the RP Act, 1951 amended (Telangana Council)
  [133:6:0.15] 105 | Pending cases: disputes over transfer decided finally by the High Court at Hyderabad
  [133:6:0.16] 108 | President may remove difficulties (orders within three years)
[133:6:1] > 74 losses, 79 officers, 95 higher education, 108 President.
[133:6:2] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text pp.11-27, ss.46, 66, 74-75, 79, 83-95, 108
## SUB 7 | Capital Question After 2014
[133:7:0] - **Sivaramakrishnan Committee** (Sec 6) on the new capital; AP chose **Amaravati** (2015).
[133:7:1] - Common capital ended on **2 June 2024**; Hyderabad is now Telangana's capital only.
[133:7:2] - AP Assembly resolution of **28 March 2026** asked to insert "**at Amaravati**" in Sec 5(2); the **AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2026** named **Amaravati** the sole capital with effect from **2 June 2024** – the first law in which Parliament named a State's capital. AP High Court capital order: 3 March 2022.
[133:7:3] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:133 and 3:134 (Amendment Act 2026, not re-checked); job b1-r263 notes row-263

=== NOTES 3:134 | Special Assistance and the A.p. Reorganisation Act Commitments
## SUB 0 | Financial Provisions and the Population Ratio
[134:0:0] # Population ratio and revenue
[134:0:1] - **Population ratio (Sec 2(h)):** AP **58.32** : Telangana **41.68** (Census 2011) – the basis of most liability splits.
[134:0:2] - Residuary AP got about **46% of the estimated revenue** at bifurcation while carrying a larger share of debt; it lost Hyderabad's revenue.
[134:0:3] TABLE head: Section | Provision
  [134:0:3.0] 46(1) | 13th FC award split by population ratio and other parameters; 14th FC to make separate awards
  [134:0:3.1] 46(2) | Grants to meet the resource gap of residual AP (₹2,303 cr in 2014-15; ₹3,979.50 cr in all) and a special development package for backward areas
  [134:0:3.2] 46(3) | Incentives in particular for Rayalaseema and north coastal regions – ₹350 cr a year for 7 backward districts
  [134:0:3.3] 94(1) | Fiscal measures including tax incentives
  [134:0:3.4] 94(2) | Support for backward areas' infrastructure
  [134:0:3.5] 94(3) | Special financial support for the new capital (Raj Bhavan, High Court, Secretariat, Assembly, Council) – ₹2,500 crore released
  [134:0:3.6] 94(4) | Denotify degraded forest land for the capital
[134:0:4] # Division of assets and liabilities
[134:0:5] - **General rule:** assets on **location** basis; liabilities on **population** basis; power on the last **five years' consumption** (AP got about **46%** of power).
[134:0:6] TABLE head: Item | Section | Basis
  [134:0:6.0] Treasury and bank cash balances | 49 | Population ratio
  [134:0:6.1] Seventh Schedule funds (provident, pension, insurance) | 52 | Population ratio (Calamity Relief Fund by area)
  [134:0:6.2] Commercial undertakings | 53 | Operational units by location; headquarters by population
  [134:0:6.3] Public debt | 54 | Population ratio
  [134:0:6.4] Tax refunds | 56 | Population ratio
[134:0:7] - **Central assistance released:** ₹6,403 cr to 31 Mar 2016; about **₹10,461 cr by mid-March 2017** (APPSC); **₹12,476.76 cr by March 2018** incl. ₹2,500 cr for the capital.
[134:0:8] - **AP White Paper (Dec 2018):** of **14** assurances in the Act, **none (0) fully implemented**.
[134:0:9] > 58.32 : 41.68; 46% revenue; Sec 49 cash by population; Sec 94(3) capital ₹2,500 cr.
[134:0:10] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text ss.2(h), 46-56, 94; thehansindia.com 24 Dec 2018 (white paper); old app notes 3:134
## SUB 1 | Special Category Status and the Special Assistance Measure
[134:1:0] - **SCS** began in **1969** (Fifth FC era) – first **J&K, Assam, Nagaland**; CSS funded **90:10** (others **60:40**), concessional loans, tax breaks.
[134:1:1] - **SCS is not in the APRA 2014** – it was a political assurance (Rajya Sabha, Feb 2014); the **14th FC** removed the special/general distinction in devolution.
[134:1:2] - AP, **Bihar and Odisha** still demand SCS (as of January 2026).
[134:1:3] - **Special Assistance Measure** approved by the Union Cabinet on **15 March 2017**:
[134:1:4]   - **EAPs** signed 2015-16 to 2019-20: Centre repays loan and interest – bears **90%** of EAP cost.
[134:1:5]   - **Polavaram:** Centre funds **100% of the remaining cost of the irrigation component** (cost as on 1 April 2014).
[134:1:6] - Largest central transfer to AP in 2023-24 RE: **Finance Commission grants**.
[134:1:7] > 90:10 = SCS CSS share; 90% = Centre's share of EAP cost.
[134:1:8] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:134 (SCS, special assistance; not re-checked)
## SUB 2 | Central Institutions under the Thirteenth Schedule
[134:2:0] - **Thirteenth Schedule (Education):** GoI to set up **institutions of national importance in the 12th and 13th Plan periods** – one **IIT, NIT, IIM, IISER, Central University, Petroleum University, Agricultural University, IIIT**; plus an **AIIMS-type** hospital, a **Tribal University** in each State, and **NIDM** in AP; a Horticulture University in Telangana.
[134:2:1] - **11 national-level institutions** for AP; all **11** are functional.
[134:2:2] TABLE head: Institution | Location
  [134:2:2.0] IIT | Tirupati
  [134:2:2.1] IISER | Tirupati
  [134:2:2.2] IIM | Visakhapatnam
  [134:2:2.3] IIPE (Petroleum University, Act of 2017) | Visakhapatnam
  [134:2:2.4] NIT | Tadepalligudem
  [134:2:2.5] AIIMS | Mangalagiri
  [134:2:2.6] IIITDM | Kurnool – first functioned from the IIITDM Kancheepuram campus in Chennai (2015)
  [134:2:2.7] IIIT | Sri City
  [134:2:2.8] Central University | Anantapur (Central Universities (Amendment) Act, 2019)
  [134:2:2.9] Central Tribal University | Vizianagaram (same Act)
[134:2:3] > Tirupati = IIT + IISER; Visakhapatnam = IIM + IIPE.
[134:2:4] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text p.70 (Thirteenth Schedule); deccanchronicle.com 28 Jun 2015 (IIITDM Kurnool in Chennai); old app notes 3:134
## SUB 3 | Thirteenth Schedule – Infrastructure Commitments
[134:3:0] TABLE head: Commitment | Place
  [134:3:0.0] SAIL integrated steel plant (feasibility in 6 months) | YSR district (Kadapa) – not Kurnool
  [134:3:0.1] New major port (Phase I by end-2018) | Duggirajupatnam
  [134:3:0.2] Greenfield refinery and petrochemical complex (IOC or HPCL) | Kakinada – GAIL-HPCL 50:50 JV
  [134:3:0.3] Vizag-Chennai Industrial Corridor | Modelled on DMIC
  [134:3:0.4] New railway zone | South Coast Railway, HQ Visakhapatnam – 18th zone; divisions Guntakal, Guntur, Vijayawada, Visakhapatnam (not Secunderabad)
  [134:3:0.5] Airports to international standards | Visakhapatnam, Vijayawada, Tirupati
  [134:3:0.6] Metro rail (examine in 1 year) | Visakhapatnam and Vijayawada-Guntur-Tenali (not Tirupati)
[134:3:1] - **Telangana's items:** SAIL steel plant in **Khammam**, **NTPC 4,000 MW**, Rail Coach Factory, NHAI roads.
[134:3:2] - **Sec 93:** Centre to take these measures within **10 years**.
[134:3:3] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text pp.70-71 (Thirteenth Schedule), s.93; old app notes 3:134
## SUB 4 | Polavaram and the River Boards
[134:4:0] - **Sec 90:** **Polavaram** declared a **national project**; Telangana's consent deemed given (Sec 89 = KWDT term – not Polavaram).
[134:4:1] - Executed by the **Government of AP on behalf of GoI** (reimbursement); **CWC** clears designs – spillway blocks cleared April 2026.
[134:4:2] - Irrigation **2.91 lakh ha** (East Godavari, Visakhapatnam, West Godavari, Krishna); Union Budget 2026-27: **₹3,320 crore**, storage up to **+41.15 m**; friction = cost revision, land acquisition, R&R.
[134:4:3] - **Apex Council (Sec 84):** Union Water Resources Minister (chair) + both CMs; boards within **60 days** (Sec 85): **GRMB HQ in Telangana, KRMB HQ in AP**; Eleventh Schedule – no new project without Apex Council sanction; jurisdictions notified by the **Ministry of Jal Shakti** on 15 July **2021**.
[134:4:4] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 Gazette text ss.84-90, Eleventh Schedule; old app notes 3:134
## SUB 5 | Amaravati – Sole Capital (2026)
[134:5:0] - **AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2026:** Section 5 amended – **Amaravati** = AP's **sole and permanent capital**, **retrospective from 2 June 2024**; Lok Sabha debate 1 April 2026.
[134:5:1] ~~ Sources: old app notes 3:134 (not re-checked)

  x: Schedule XIII includes an IIT, NIT, IIM, IISER, Central, Petroleum and Agricultural Universities, an IIIT, an AIIMS-type hospital, a Tribal University and NIDM. An Indian Institute of Science is not listed.

=== NOTES 3:135 | Division of Assets, Liabilities, Employees and Institutions; Ninth and Tenth Schedule Institutions
## SUB 0 | Principles of Division – Item → Basis → Section
[135:0:0] # The core rule
[135:0:1] - **Act:** AP Reorganisation Act, 2014 – President's assent **1 March 2014**; appointed day **2 June 2014**.
[135:0:2] - **Core rule:** **assets by location**, **liabilities (debts) by population ratio**.
[135:0:3] - **Population ratio** (Census 2011): **AP : Telangana = 58.32 : 41.68** (≈ **58 : 42**).
[135:0:4] - **Result:** AP got about **46% of revenue** but inherited about **58% of debt** – major assets built in the united State stand in **Hyderabad**, which went to Telangana; so the location rule is unfair to AP.
[135:0:5] - **Financial division:** **Part VI**, "Apportionment of Assets and Liabilities", **Sections 47-67** (the money items are **Sections 47-64**, Sec 64 = residuary; 76-83 = services); disputes settled by mutual agreement, failing which by **Central order on the advice of the CAG** (Sec 47(4)).
[135:0:6] # Item → basis → section
[135:0:7] TABLE head: Item | Basis | Section
  [135:0:7.0] Award of the 13th Finance Commission | Population ratio and other parameters (Centre apportions) | 46(1)
  [135:0:7.1] Land, stores, goods inside a State | Location | 48
  [135:0:7.2] Stores, goods, property outside the existing State (e.g. AP Bhavan) | Population ratio | 48
  [135:0:7.3] Treasury and bank cash balances | Population ratio, by adjusting credit balances in the books of the RBI – no transfer of cash between treasuries | 49
  [135:0:7.4] Arrears of property tax / land revenue | State where the property lies | 50
  [135:0:7.5] Arrears of any other tax | State of the place of assessment | 50
  [135:0:7.6] Loans and advances to a body or person inside the State (incl. deferred taxes of industrial units) | State where the area / unit is located | 51(1)
  [135:0:7.7] Loans and advances to a person or institution outside the State | Right to recover belongs to Andhra Pradesh; sums recovered shared by population ratio | 51(2)
  [135:0:7.8] Securities against Seventh Schedule funds (List of Funds in the Public Account) | Population ratio | 52
  [135:0:7.9] Calamity Relief Fund securities | Area ratio (the only area-based item) | 52
  [135:0:7.10] Commercial / industrial undertakings (State PSUs) | Operational units by location; headquarters by population ratio | 53
  [135:0:7.11] Public debt and Public Account liabilities | Population ratio (re-lent loans follow the area of the borrower) | 54
  [135:0:7.12] Floating loans | Exclusive purpose → that State; rest by population ratio | 55
  [135:0:7.13] Refund of property tax collected in excess | State where property lies | 56
  [135:0:7.14] Refund of other taxes collected in excess | Population ratio (58.32 : 41.68) | 56
  [135:0:7.15] Provident Fund | State to which the employee is permanently allotted | 58
  [135:0:7.16] Pensions | As in the Eighth Schedule (population ratio) | 59
  [135:0:7.17] Salaries of judges of the common High Court | Population ratio | 30(2)
  [135:0:7.18] CM Relief Fund (and other common funds) | Population ratio | –
[135:0:8] > Population ratio is used most often to divide **assets** held in common (cash, securities, HQ, outside property); the summary rule still reads "assets by location, debts by population".
[135:0:9] > Matching trap: **Arrears of taxes = Sec 50** (correct pair); treasury = 49 (not 53), PF = 58 (not 59), public debt = 54 (not 58).
[135:0:10] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (src/ap-reorganisation/apra.txt, Parts V-VI); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act 2014 notes Part 1 p.5, 15-20 (src/ap-reorganisation/ap-bifurcation-act-notes-01.txt)
## SUB 1 | Schedule IX and Schedule X Institutions
[135:1:0] TABLE head: Feature | Schedule IX | Schedule X
  [135:1:0.0] What it lists | Government companies and corporations | State institutions whose facilities continue for both States
  [135:1:0.1] Count | 89 in the Act as enacted (91 commonly cited after additions) | 107 in the Act; 142 after 35 were added by notification GSR 369(E) of 7 May 2015
  [135:1:0.2] Examples | APSFC, APGENCO, APTRANSCO, SCCL, Sports Authority of AP (SAAP) | APSCHE, Shilparamam Arts, Crafts and Cultural Society, training institutes
  [135:1:0.3] Governing section | Sec 68 (continue to function) + Sec 53 (manner of division) | Sec 75 (continuance of facilities)
  [135:1:0.4] Division rule | Operational units by location; headquarters by population ratio | Assets by location; cash by population (APSCHE ruling)
  [135:1:0.5] Time limit | – | Terms for continuing facilities agreed within one year
  [135:1:0.6] Committee | Sheela Bhide Committee | –
[135:1:1] - **APSFC** (AP State Financial Corporation): Schedule IX; divided under **Section 70** – shareholder-approved scheme → Centre's sanction; if not approved, referred to a **High Court judge**, whose scheme is final.
[135:1:2] - **Section 74**: set-off and carry-forward of PSU losses under the Income-tax Act.
[135:1:3] - **Shilparamam** = Arts, Crafts and Heritage villages showing handicrafts of artisans (Schedule X).
[135:1:4] - **SAAP** = **Sports Authority of Andhra Pradesh** (Schedule IX).
[135:1:5] - **Section 66**: Centre may order allocation of benefits/liabilities; AP uses it for institutions in no schedule.
[135:1:6] # Supreme Court and Home Ministry
[135:1:7] TABLE head: Case / Body | Ruling / Rule
  [135:1:7.0] APSCHE (Schedule X), Supreme Court 2016 | Division under Section 47 (Sec 75 only keeps facilities going); assets by location, cash and bank balances by population ratio
  [135:1:7.1] Union Home Ministry order on APSCHE | Assets belong to the State of location; cash deposits/balances shared 52 : 48; the MHA then applied the same formula to other Schedule X institutions
  [135:1:7.2] APSRTC hospital and Kalyana Mandapam, Hyderabad | Treated as not part of APSRTC headquarters (HQ read narrowly) → go by location
  [135:1:7.3] AP Bhavan, New Delhi | Outside the State → population ratio; Telangana claims it as Nizam's property
  [135:1:7.4] AP plea, Supreme Court, December 2022 | Quick division of Schedule IX and Schedule X assets; AP's plea (advocate Mahfooz Ahsan Nazki, 2023) said non-division benefits Telangana and about 91% of assets lie in Hyderabad
[135:1:8] > APSCHE = Schedule X; APSFC and SAAP = Schedule IX; Shilparamam = Schedule X.
[135:1:9] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt, Ninth Schedule entries 1-89 incl. 19 APSFC, 65 SAAP; Tenth Schedule entries 1-107 incl. 27 APSCHE, 99 Shilparamam; Secs 68-75); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 2 p.1-2, 12-13, 19; app notes 3:135 (old-notes.txt)
## SUB 2 | Committees and Employee Apportionment – Part VIII
[135:2:0] TABLE head: Committee | Chair | Subject
  [135:2:0.0] Sheela Bhide Committee | Sheela Bhide (retd IAS) | Assets, liabilities and employees of Schedule IX companies and corporations; set up by the undivided AP government
  [135:2:0.1] Kamalanathan Committee | C.R. Kamalanathan | Allocation of State-level employees
  [135:2:0.2] Pratyush Sinha Committee | Pratyush Sinha | Allocation of All India Services officers (IAS/IPS/IFoS), under Sec 80
  [135:2:0.3] Justice D.M. Dharmadhikari Committee | One-man, set up by the Supreme Court (28 Nov 2018) | Division of power-utility employees
[135:2:1] - Sheela Bhide Committee recommended division of most Schedule IX bodies (89 of 91 reported); Telangana opposed some; its term expired Aug 2015 and the Centre revived it.
[135:2:2] - **Part VIII**, "**Provisions as to Services**" (**Sections 76-83**): AIS and State employees in local, district, zonal, multi-zonal and State cadres.
[135:2:3] - **Section 76**: two separate **State cadres** of **IAS, IPS, IFoS** (creates the Telangana State cadre).
[135:2:4] - **Section 77**: State employees; does **not** apply to All India Services.
[135:2:5] - **Section 80**: **Advisory Committees** within **30 days** of enactment; allocation by the Centre on their recommendation.
[135:2:6] - **Section 82**: PSU staff stay for **one year**; the **corporate body itself** decides the distribution.
[135:2:7] - **Section 83**: APPSC continues for AP; Telangana to constitute its own PSC.
[135:2:8] - Non-opters allotted on **native (domicile) status**; seniority and pension disputes under **Sec 59 / Eighth Schedule**; DoPT one-member **Deepak Khandekar** committee (March 2024) on AIS cases.
[135:2:9] - Comparison: the **1953 Andhra State** partition committee was chaired by **T.T. Krishnamachari**.
[135:2:10] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt, Part VIII Secs 76-83); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 2 p.2-4; app notes 3:135-136 (old-notes.txt)
## SUB 3 | Twelfth Schedule – Power and Coal
[135:3:0] - **Twelfth Schedule** = principles for **coal, oil and gas, and power** (Sec 92).
[135:3:1] - **Coal:** **SCCL** equity **51% Telangana, 49% Government of India** – AP holds **no** equity; **existing coal linkages continue** without change; new linkages under the New Coal Distribution Policy.
[135:3:2] - **Power:** **APGENCO units divided by geographical location** of the plants; existing **PPAs continue**.
[135:3:3] - **Central Generating Stations:** power shared on the **actual energy consumption of the DISCOMs in the last 5 years**.
[135:3:4] - Deficit State gets **first right of refusal** over the other's surplus power for **10 years**; APERC joint for 6 months, SLDC common for 2 years.
[135:3:5] - AP got about **46% of power** for **58% of population**.
[135:3:6] > The amicable division ratio is **AP : Telangana = 58 : 42** – a statement giving it as "Telangana and AP … 58 : 42 respectively" is wrong.
[135:3:7] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt, Twelfth Schedule); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 2 p.14-16
## SUB 4 | Access to Higher Education – Part XI
[135:4:0] - **Section 95**, **Part XI** (Access to Higher Education): **equal opportunities for quality higher education**.
[135:4:1] - Existing **Article 371D** admission quotas in all government/private, aided/unaided institutions of higher, technical and medical education continue for **10 years**, with the **common admission process**.
[135:4:2] - **Section 97** amended **Article 371D** to read "**Andhra Pradesh or Telangana**".
[135:4:3] > 10 years = common admission (Sec 95) = surplus-power right = common capital Hyderabad.
[135:4:4] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt, Part XI s.95, s.97); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 2 p.9-11
## SUB 5 | Unresolved Division – Status
[135:5:0] - **CAG (2019):** **₹1,51,349.67 crore under capital heads** and ₹28,099.68 crore of loans and advances still **not apportioned** between AP and Telangana (still pending after 7 years).
[135:5:1] - **Common institutions:** Telangana claims about **₹408 crore** from AP for running the High Court, Raj Bhavan etc. in Hyderabad (2022); the exact sum AP undertook is not established.
[135:5:2] - Over **140** corporations/institutions worth more than **₹39,000 crore** undivided; AP wants **population ratio**, Telangana insists on **location**.
[135:5:3] - **Unapportioned public debt** of over ₹24,000 crore on AP's books cuts its **FRBM borrowing room**.
[135:5:4] - **Southern Zonal Council** directed AP to pay Telangana its share of a central subsidy credited to AP.
[135:5:5] - Criticism: schedules not exhaustive; "**headquarters**" undefined → litigation.
[135:5:6] ~~ Sources: web search result, Business Standard/PTI 23 Sep 2019 (CAG Finance Accounts) – page not opened; web search result, Lok Sabha reply AU1541 and Deccan Chronicle (₹408 crore) – pages not opened; app notes 3:135 (old-notes.txt)

=== OWN MCQs of 3:135 (53)
[na6215716905] sub0
  Andhra Pradesh complained that after bifurcation it got about 46% of revenue but inherited about what share of the debt?
  42% | 46% | 54% | *58%
  x: Assets followed location but debts followed population, so AP got about 46% of revenue but inherited 58% of the debt.
[f999204751e8c] sub0
  Which date was the appointed day under the AP Reorganisation Act, 2014?
  1 March 2014 | *2 June 2014 | 2 June 2015 | 1 March 2015
  x: The Act received assent on 1 March 2014 and the appointed day was 2 June 2014.
[f0c9167adf4ed] sub0
  The financial division provisions of the AP Reorganisation Act, 2014 lie in which Sections?
  Sections 76-83 | Sections 66-75 | *Sections 47-64 | Sections 90-97
  x: Sections 47-64 deal with the apportionment of assets and liabilities; Sections 76-83 are about services.
[n646adabcca4] sub0
  On what basis are land, stores and goods situated within a particular State divided?
  Employee allotment | Place of assessment | *Location | Population ratio

=== NOTES 3:137 | Central Government's Assistance for Building A New Capital – Commitments and Actual Releases
## SUB 0 | Act Sections and Committees on the Capital
[137:0:0] TABLE head: Section | Provision
  [137:0:0.0] 5 | Hyderabad = common capital for a period not exceeding 10 years (GHMC area); then capital of Telangana and a new capital for AP
  [137:0:0.1] 6 | Centre's Expert Committee to study alternatives for AP's new capital, report within six months
  [137:0:0.2] 94(3) | Central Government to give special financial support for essential facilities in the new capital: Raj Bhavan, High Court, Secretariat, Legislative Assembly, Legislative Council and other essential infrastructure
  [137:0:0.3] 94(4) | Centre to facilitate the capital by denotifying degraded forest land, if required
  [137:0:0.4] 95 | Not the capital – higher-education quotas (Part XI)
[137:0:1] # Committees on the capital
[137:0:2] TABLE head: Committee | Set up by / Chair | Outcome
  [137:0:2.0] Expert Committee (Sec 6) | Centre; K.C. Sivaramakrishnan | Reported Aug 2014; gave loose options, no firm site; opposed a single "super capital" (ecological fears)
  [137:0:2.1] Capital City Advisory Committee | AP government; P. Narayana | Backed the Vijayawada-Guntur region
  [137:0:2.2] G.N. Rao Committee + BCG study | AP government | Basis of the three-capitals plan (2020)
[137:0:3] - The **AP Cabinet** (not the Centre) selected **Amaravati** on the **Krishna**, between Vijayawada and Guntur.
[137:0:4] > APPSC key once marked "Section 95" for the capital-support provision; the Act's provision is **Section 94(3)**.
[137:0:5] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt, ss.5, 6, 94); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 1 p.6-8, Part 2 p.9; app notes 3:137 (old-notes.txt)
## SUB 1 | Central Releases and Funding for Amaravati
[137:1:0] - Direct grants under Sec 94(3): **₹2,500 crore** = **₹1,500 crore** initial (capital buildings) + **₹1,000 crore** later (counted as the 2017-18 special assistance for the capital).
[137:1:1] - The ₹1,000 crore went to **underground drainage** works in **Vijayawada and Guntur**.
[137:1:2] - Finance Ministry: balance ₹1,000 crore only after **utilisation certificates (UCs)** for the ₹1,500 crore; AP (2018) said **NITI Aayog** had not rejected its UCs.
[137:1:3] - Cost gap: AP's estimate about **₹1.2 lakh crore** vs Centre's commitment about ₹27,000 crore.
[137:1:4] - **Union Budget 2024-25**: **₹15,000 crore** for Amaravati, as **Centre-facilitated multilateral** support (World Bank + ADB, 0.6% + TONA).
[137:1:5] TABLE head: Lender | Amount
  [137:1:5.0] World Bank + ADB | ₹15,000 crore
  [137:1:5.1] HUDCO | ₹11,000 crore term loan
  [137:1:5.2] NaBFID | ₹7,500 crore
  [137:1:5.3] NABARD (NIDA) | ₹7,387.70 crore
[137:1:6] - Dispute: AP wants **non-repayable grants**; Centre gives **loans with sovereign guarantees** (**Article 293(2)**) → repayment burdens may crowd out welfare spending; AP debt projected **₹7.11 lakh crore** (~**36% of GSDP**).
[137:1:7] ~~ Sources: NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 2 p.9 (₹2,500 crore under 94(3)); app notes 3:137 (old-notes.txt)
## SUB 2 | Amaravati Project – Timeline
[137:2:0] - **APCRDA Act, 2014** in force **30 December 2014**; Land Pooling Scheme Jan 2015; consent for about **33,000 acres** by March 2015; original **29 villages**.
[137:2:1] - Pooling farmers exempt from **capital gains tax and stamp duty** on first sale.
[137:2:2] - **22 October 2015**: PM **Narendra Modi** laid the foundation stone at **Uddandarayunipalem**.
[137:2:3] TABLE head: Three capitals (2020) | City
  [137:2:3.0] Legislative | Amaravati
  [137:2:3.1] Executive | Visakhapatnam
  [137:2:3.2] Judicial | Kurnool
[137:2:4] - **3 March 2022**: AP High Court struck down the CRDA Repeal Act (2020).
[137:2:5] - **AP Reorganisation (Amendment) Act, 2026**: Amaravati = **sole and permanent capital**, retrospective from 2 June 2024.
[137:2:6] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:137 (old-notes.txt)

[nce8aabc68fd] sub1

  x: Borrowing instead of receiving grants adds repayment obligations and can reduce the fiscal space available for welfare and development expenditure.

  x: The Budget promised Rs 15,000 crore of Centre-facilitated multilateral support.
[fc13a585348a1] sub1

  *Article 293(2) | Article 371D | Article 282 | Article 275
  x: Loans with sovereign guarantees need approval under Article 293(2).

=== NOTES 3:140 | Vizag Railway Zone - Promise, Status and Issues
## SUB 0 | The Act Commitment and South Coast Railway
[140:0:0] TABLE head: Item | Fact
  [140:0:0.0] Where | Thirteenth Schedule (Infrastructure), AP Reorganisation Act, 2014
  [140:0:0.1] Wording | Indian Railways shall, within six months from the appointed day, examine establishing a new railway zone in AP and take an expeditious decision
  [140:0:0.2] Telangana item | Railways to examine a Rail Coach Factory
  [140:0:0.3] Constitutional basis | Railways = Union subject, Entry 22, List I
  [140:0:0.4] How a zone is made | Executive decision + gazette notification of the Ministry of Railways
[140:0:1] # Timeline of South Coast Railway
[140:0:2] TABLE head: When | Step
  [140:0:2.0] 2019 | South Coast Railway (SCoR) announced, HQ Visakhapatnam
  [140:0:2.1] Apr 2026 | Temporary office at Siripuram
  [140:0:2.2] 4 May 2026 | Gazette notification
  [140:0:2.3] 1 June 2026 | SCoR fully working = India's 18th zone
[140:0:3] - Demand pressed for over **four decades**; 17th zone = **Metro Railway, Kolkata**.
[140:0:4] - HQ: **₹184 crore** zonal headquarters at **Mudasarlova**, Visakhapatnam.
[140:0:5] > AP = railway zone (Schedule 13); Telangana = Rail Coach Factory. "Examine", not "establish".
[140:0:6] ~~ Sources: AP Reorganisation Act 2014 (apra.txt, Thirteenth Schedule item 8); NextGen AP State Reorganisation Act notes Part 2 p.18; app notes 3:140 (old-notes.txt)
## SUB 1 | Divisions, Waltair Split and the K-K Line
[140:1:0] - SCoR has **4 divisions**: **Visakhapatnam, Vijayawada, Guntur, Guntakal**; Vijayawada, Guntur and Guntakal came from **South Central Railway**, which shrinks to **three divisions**.
[140:1:1] - **South Central Railway** itself began functioning on **2 October 1966**.
[140:1:2] TABLE head: Part | Goes to
  [140:1:2.0] Andhra part of Waltair | Visakhapatnam division of SCoR
  [140:1:2.1] Rest of Waltair | New Rayagada division of East Coast Railway (HQ Bhubaneswar)
[140:1:3] - **Kottavalasa-Kirandul (K-K) line** = "revenue lifeline" of Waltair; Odisha claimed it; AP argued Waltair earned more than **Vijayawada, Guntur and Guntakal combined**; AP offered only Odisha stretches.
[140:1:4] - A dedicated zone helps **VCIC** by cutting freight costs and improving port connectivity.
[140:1:5] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:140 (old-notes.txt); general knowledge, not checked (SCR 1966)
## SUB 2 | Rail Projects in AP
[140:2:0] TABLE head: Line | Purpose
  [140:2:0.0] Errupalem-Amaravati-Namburu (~57 km, ~₹2,245 crore, approved Oct 2024) | Amaravati rail link
  [140:2:0.1] Ramayapatnam-Kadapa | Dedicated steel plant line
  [140:2:0.2] Kotipalli-Narsapur | New line
[140:2:1] - **July 2021**: Railways announced **33 projects** (new lines and doubling) in AP.
[140:2:2] - AP outlay **₹10,134 crore (2026-27)**; as of 1 Apr 2026, **42 projects**; network **100% electrified**; **74** Amrit Bharat stations (Sullurupeta opened 22 May 2025).
[140:2:3] - Budget 2026-27: **7** high-speed rail "growth connectors".
[140:2:4] ~~ Sources: app notes 3:140 (old-notes.txt)

=== OWN MCQs of 3:140 (32)
[ncf8c85f274b] sub0
  Within what period did Indian Railways have to examine a new railway zone for Andhra Pradesh under the AP Reorganisation Act, 2014?
  Three months from the appointed day | One year from the appointed day | *Six months from the appointed day | Two years from the appointed day
  x: The Thirteenth Schedule required the Railways to examine a new zone in Andhra Pradesh within six months from the appointed day.
[ndd51a7512ea] sub0
  What was the obligation relating to the proposed railway zone in Andhra Pradesh under the Thirteenth Schedule?
  *Railways was required to examine establishing the zone and take an expeditious decision | The State Government was authorised to establish the zone independently | The Union Cabinet was required to establish the zone through a constitutional amendment | Railways was required to establish the zone within six months
  x: The statutory wording created a feasibility-oriented obligation to examine the establishment of a zone and take an expeditious decision; it did not itself establish the zone.
[n27d5cd611ee] sub0
  Which of the following is NOT correct about creating a new railway zone?
  Railways is a Union subject | *Railways is listed in Entry 22 of List II (State List) | A zone is created by an executive decision of the Ministry of Railways | A zone is brought into being by a gazette notification of the Ministry of Railways
  x: Railways is a Union subject in Entry 22 of List I, not List II; a zone is made by executive decision plus gazette notification.
[f7994d9554aae] sub0
  What does the Thirteenth Schedule ask Indian Railways to examine setting up in Telangana?
  Railway zone | *Rail Coach Factory | Wagon workshop | Railway division
  x: The Act asked Railways to examine a Rail Coach Factory for Telangana and a railway zone for AP.

  *Four decades | Three decades | Two decades | Five decades
  x: The demand had been pressed for over four decades; the statutory promise of 2014 was fulfilled only after about a decade.
[nb9bfd0274ce] sub0

  x: The gazette came on 4 May 2026 and SCoR became fully operational on 1 June 2026.
